- **Stage-to-plan setting** ("Start this plan when a person enters stage …" on the playbook page). Module 67 gains `GET /playbooks/:id/auto-enroll-stage` (`self`: the RLS-visible playbook row only, 404 otherwise, exactly as `GET /playbooks/:id`), answering the stage and `canChange`, the same `can('playbook.auto_enroll_stage')` on the same facts as the PUT, so the control is offered exactly where the PUT would accept it (team playbooks Owner/Admin; an agent their own personal playbook); others read the stage it starts on. It writes nothing and shows nothing the playbook row does not. The stage names are the tenant's labels; the 409 (`AUTO_ENROLL_STAGE_TAKEN`, with a link to the plan holding the stage) and the 422s (`AUTO_ENROLL_NOT_ALLOWED`, `VALIDATION_FAILED`) and `PLAYBOOK_RETIRED` are said in plain words; an instant reply to new leads is told it never starts on a stage instead of being offered the control.
- **The phone bulk bar (left to the owner).** On a phone the bar stays one row that scrolls sideways (module 61's design), so Assign and Start plan come right after Tag. At 390 px (the e2e's iPhone 13) Assign, and an agent's Start plan, are wholly in view; an Owner/Admin/Team Lead's Start plan, third, shows most of itself at the edge (the e2e asserts at least half). Every tap the e2e counts first asserts that its target is wholly in view (`toBeInViewport({ ratio: 1 })`, whose IntersectionObserver counts the bar's clipping), because Playwright's tap would scroll a hidden target into view and so hide a swipe from the count. Remove tag, Change stage, Email and Clear follow, at or behind the bar's edge; Email is wholly behind it for every role, as it already was before these follow-ups, so a bulk email started from the bar on a phone takes one sideways swipe on top of §5's 6 taps. Showing every action on a phone takes another layout (a second row, or a More sheet that costs a tap): the owner's call.
- **Who bulk assign offers (left to the owner).** The bulk picker offers whom the engine accepts. An Owner/Admin sees every active member who can hold leads, Owners and Admins included, and members who are not taking new leads (`accepting_leads` steers routing, not who may be assigned); a Team Lead sees the scoreboard's rows. The Team page's single-lead Reassign picker (`useReassignCandidates` in `apps/web/src/surfaces/team/TeamPage.tsx`) is narrower for an Owner/Admin: active agents, Team Leads and ISAs who are taking new leads (a Team Lead gets the same scoreboard rows in both). The two differ until the owner picks one rule for both.


### FUB parity module 67 `stage-automation` (lane par-stage-automation): security notes
One migration (`0036_stage_automation.sql`), one new table, no `SECURITY DEFINER` function. What the lane adds to the security surface, for review:
- **`stage_automation_decisions`** (one row per `contact_stage_events` row; PK `(tenant_id, stage_event_id)`): ENABLE + FORCE RLS, the RESTRICTIVE staff-only `tenant_isolation`, `support_read_only_{ins,upd,del}` and the `support_read_only` statement trigger, composite FKs. Read exactly when the contact's detail is visible. INSERT only by the system actor (`actor_kind = 'system' AND is_tenant_wide()`); no UPDATE/DELETE grant to `app_user`, so a decision is final. The skip reason is a CHECKed closed set (equal to core-side `SKIP_REASONS`, tested). It holds no message content and no PII. Rows cascade with the contact and the event. One partial index on `contact_stage_events (tenant_id, occurred_at) WHERE actor_kind = 'user'` for the backup scan.
- **Autonomy stays inside approved playbooks.** A stage entry enrolls only through the existing playbook path (`P.insertEnrollment` + `enqueueStep`, `enrolled_by = 'stage_change'`), so `playbook_enrollments_guard` (approved version only) and `enrollment_one_live` (one live enrollment per contact) decide again under any bypass (tested with core bypassed). Nothing here sends: the first step is the `playbook.step` job, and its message goes through `dispatchOutbound`, where `canContact` and both DB dispatch guards decide at send time (D1).
- **Who may set the stage.** `playbook.auto_enroll_stage` (core authz): team playbooks Owner/Admin; an agent their own agent playbook only; TL, ISA, TC and support never (404; support also refused by the DB trigger). An agent playbook covers only its owner's contacts (the candidate query and core `choosePlaybook`; the step runner also pauses it on an owner change). Audited as `settings.changed` on the playbook. Reading it (`GET /playbooks/:id/auto-enroll-stage`, added by the parity follow-ups) is `self` under the playbook's own RLS, with `canChange` computed by the same `can()` and facts as the PUT.
- **The stage-changer's visibility is decided by their own RLS.** Before the decision, a separate read-only transaction runs as the member who changed the stage (as R-PAR-2 mention checks do; no definer re-implements visibility). It runs sequentially, never nested inside the system transaction (a nested connection per call would starve the pool). A member who lost the person, or is no longer active, gets `not_visible`.
- **Only a person's stage change enrolls.** Events with `actor_kind` other than `user` (import, migration, system) never enroll (`not_a_person_change`), and the scan never selects them. A merge's copies of the loser's history (0027 keeps the original instant and actor) are recognized in SQL against the event row (microsecond-exact) and never reconciled. A playbook changed after the stage entry does not reach back to it (`playbooks.updated_at <= occurred_at`).
- **Opt-outs (D1, R-PAR-12).** Reachability is `canContact` on the gate input `buildGateInput` builds for each channel/purpose/category the approved version messages on, plus the opt-out rows of the person's removed phones (except confirmed wrong numbers). Only opt-out reasons skip the plan (`CONSENT_REVOKED`, `SUPPRESSED`, `PERSON_DO_NOT_CONTACT`, `WRONG_NUMBER`); core may skip more than the DB guard would block, never less. The same function (`stageAutomation/reach.ts` `reachableForVersion`) decides a person's direct enrollment (`POST /contacts/:id/enrollments`, B9): someone no channel of the plan can reach is refused with 409 `CANNOT_BE_REACHED` (`details.reason = cannot_be_reached`, the skip reason) before anything is written, so the plan never shows as live while it can never send. In both paths a version with no text or email step is reachable (it sends nothing). A merged-away record is never enrolled by any path (F2), as a stage entry skips it (`person_removed`): the direct route refuses it with 409 `CONTACT_MERGED` (`details.reason = person_removed`; `details.mergedInto` names the survivor only to whoever sees it) before anything is read or written, the post-closing path skips it (a merge moves no deal), and the step runner stops an enrollment that is live on one anyway (`stop_reason = merged`, as the merge itself stops the loser's), so one person never has two live plans (R-OWN-24). Bulk Enroll is not changed: its preflight (`bulk/facts.ts`, comms `reachabilityOf`) counts any open text or email address for a human message's purpose now, not the channels and purposes the plan messages on, so it can accept a person a text-only plan cannot reach (the send-time gate still blocks every message).
- **Concurrency.** Every decision runs under the contact lock and reads its decision row first; the request path and the worker's backup scan can race and agree (tested with concurrent reconciles of the same and of competing events). Annual reminder tasks use a UUIDv5 of (contact, field, occurrence) as the task id: a rerun inserts nothing, and the task is never a send.

### Lead-handling follow-ups (migration `0037_lead_handling_followups.sql`): security notes
One migration, no new table (it was revised after its review: the do-not-contact items below). What it changes in the security surface, for review:
- **`GRANT DELETE ON contact_addresses TO app_user`**, narrowed by a new RESTRICTIVE policy `contact_addresses_delete` (FOR DELETE): `app.contact_detail_visible(tenant_id, contact_id)` AND `app.actor_kind() = 'user'` AND `app.my_role() IN ('owner','admin','team_lead','agent','isa')`, the database form of `contact.edit` that the parity child tables use. The table keeps FORCE RLS, the permissive `contact_detail` policy, `tenant_isolation`, `support_read_only_del` and the `support_read_only` statement trigger, so a TC (even a collaborating one who sees the detail), the system actor, support, another tenant and a caller who cannot see the person delete nothing, with the API bypassed too (`people/addresses.test.ts`, `packages/db/test/lead_handling_followups.test.ts`). A delete cascades only to the address's `property_records` row and sets a `home_valuations` reference to it to NULL. The route also checks `contact.edit` for the caller's scope (own / collaborator / team; the role matrix test covers every role).
- **Erasure and purge take the whole person.** `app.purge_contact_internal`, `app.erase_contact` and `app.retention_sweep` (SECURITY DEFINER, owner `app_retention`, pinned `search_path`, grants unchanged) are replaced: each keeps its latest version's statements, now run over `app.contact_merge_tree(tenant, contact)`, the contact and every contact merged into it along merge chains, deepest first. Before, `merged_into_contact_id ON DELETE SET NULL` plus the contacts CHECK (a `merged` record needs its survivor) made the erasure of any survivor fail (the API answered a 422), and made the hourly sweep fail as a whole (every tenant, every retention step) once a soft-deleted survivor passed `purge_after`; nothing resurfaced, since everything rolled back. Now the deal refusal (`CONTACT_ON_DEAL`) and the document refusal (`DOCUMENT_RETAINED`) cover every record of the person, the sweep skips a person it may not purge instead of failing, and erasure writes one `contact.erase` row per record (ids only). Consent, suppressions and the ledgers stay (HMAC, no PII). **Owner / counsel question (VERIFY WITH COUNSEL):** whether erasing a person must also erase the duplicates merged into them; the migration implements the most privacy-protective answer (yes). The erasure page says how many merged records go with the person, and its deal list and "remove from deal" step cover them too.
- **A person's do-not-contact outlives the person (D1; found in review).** The person-level do-not-contact (`contacts.do_not_contact_at`, 0018) is a column of the person's rows, so an erasure or a purge deleted it, and the HMAC-keyed ledger, which is all that survives, did not hold all of it: `POST /contacts/:id/do-not-contact` revokes only the live points the record had then, and a merge copies only the flag onto the survivor (`contacts_dnc_follow_merge`). So the survivor's own phones and emails, and any point added after the do-not-contact, had no revoke, and a contact re-created with one of them after the erasure (or the automatic purge of a soft-deleted person, 30 days on) was texted. The earlier claim that "the revokes the do-not-contact wrote keep those addresses blocked" held only for the addresses that record had at that moment. Now, before any record of the person is deleted, `admin/optOutCarry.ts` records an `agent_revoke`, purpose `all`, on every (address, channel) that `app.person_optout_uncarried` lists: every point of every record in the merge tree, live or removed except one removed as a confirmed wrong number, on each channel the guards map to its kind, unless it already carries a revoke only the person's own action lifts (a keyword STOP, which a START pops, and opt-out language, which a reviewer may void, do not count; a person-level do-not-contact yields to neither). Evidence: `{scope: 'person', carries_do_not_contact_event_id}` (ids only); audited as `consent.recorded`. The erase route runs it in its transaction as the Owner/Admin; the worker runs it hourly, just before the sweep, as the system actor in each due person's own tenant transaction (R-TEN-6). **Database backstop:** `app.purge_contact_internal` raises `PERSON_DO_NOT_CONTACT_UNCARRIED` while the predicate lists anything, and the sweep's candidate filter skips such a person, so a missed or failed carry refuses the deletion instead of dropping the opt-out. The erase route answers it as a 409 (worded on the erasure page; nothing is erased). An address no core channel can carry (an out-of-scope Messenger/Instagram identity, D2) keeps that person from being erased or purged at all; the worker alerts once per process. Tests: `admin/erasure.optout.test.ts` (both paths through the real server and worker; re-created contacts stay blocked for texts, calls and email, nothing reaches the fake providers) and `packages/db/test/lead_handling_followups.test.ts` (the predicate case by case, the backstop, the sweep, the context-free listing).
- **The sweep never fails on one person.** Each purge runs in its own subtransaction: a purge refusing with `PERSON_DO_NOT_CONTACT_UNCARRIED` or `DOCUMENT_RETAINED` after the candidate scan (a race; or a far-future `p_now`) skips that person until the next sweep and is counted (`contacts_skipped`). Every other error still fails the sweep.
- **New function `app.contact_merge_tree(uuid, uuid)`**: plain SQL, not a definer, pinned `search_path`, explicit tenant filter, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC and granted to `app_retention` (its definer callers) and to `app_user`, only so that `app.person_optout_uncarried` can run as its caller (called by `app_user` it returns only the records the caller's RLS shows). A `CYCLE` clause makes it end even on a corrupt merge cycle (tested with a superuser fixture).
- **New function `app.person_optout_uncarried(uuid, uuid)`**: plain SQL, not a definer, pinned `search_path`, explicit tenant filter, writes nothing, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC and granted to `app_user` and `app_retention`. Called by `app_user` it reads `contact_points`, `consent_state` and `consent_events` through the caller's own RLS, so it shows nothing the caller could not select (tested from another tenant); the API calls it as the Owner/Admin and the worker as the system, both tenant-wide.
- **New SECURITY DEFINER function `app.retention_optout_carry_due(timestamptz, integer)`**: owner `app_retention`, pinned `search_path`, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC and granted to `app_user`; it answers only with no tenant and no user context (as 0004's worker read-outs; tested empty for a tenant session, the system's included) and returns at most 500 (tenant id, contact id) pairs of soft-deleted people past `purge_after` whose do-not-contact is not carried yet. No names, addresses or payloads. Residual risk as for 0004: a process that clears its GUCs can learn those ids.
- **Address Undo (API only, no database change):** the restore token is sealed by the envelope with the tenant's field key and an AAD naming `contact_addresses`/`restore_token`/the address id, and is opened only inside a transaction of an active member of that tenant (the envelope's own check). The restore inserts under the caller's RLS (`contact_detail` WITH CHECK) after `contact.edit`; it never takes a field from the client, so it cannot set a jurisdiction or an import batch the row did not have. The token lives in that user's page while the toast is open.
- **`app.contact_points_immutable()`** (invoker trigger function) is replaced verbatim except one message: restoring a confirmed wrong number said "add the number again", which the person's unique key refuses; it now says the number is final for that person. Every condition and the `REMOVED_REASON_IMMUTABLE` prefix are unchanged.
- **No database change, API only:** the dispatch-guard refusal mapping above now covers `PERSON_DO_NOT_CONTACT` and `RECIPIENT_SUPPRESSED`; it only turns a refusal that already stopped the send into a recorded blocked result.

## 21. Phase 2-3 architecture
Authoritative for Phases 2 and 3; the build order, decisions and owner needs are in `docs/PHASE2_3_PLAN.md`, tables and rules in `docs/DATA_MODEL.md` Part 2. Everything in §1–§20 still holds. Owner decision **D2** (2026-11-11, PHASE2_3_PLAN §2.2) changed the scope: no Messenger/Instagram DMs, Meta Lead Ads or Meta ads; posting to Facebook Pages and Instagram business accounts; Gmail inbox sync added (§21.10, §21.11). 0018's Meta enum values, CHECKs and guard branches stay until the D2 cleanup migration (PHASE2_3_PLAN §3).
### 21.1 Principals and entry points
- **Staff** (Phase 1) and **client** (Phase 3 portal). A client context carries `app.tenant_id`, `app.principal_type = 'client'` and `app.portal_account_id`. Only `withPortalTx` sets them, from `portal.resolve_session`. A client reads `portal_accounts`, `portal_grants` and `portal_items` only, never writes (`CLIENT_READ_ONLY`), and sees nothing else (DATA_MODEL §7). A grant names a deal or listing the client is a party to; `app.portal_party_ok` checks this at grant time and again on every read, so removing a co-buyer hides everything at once. Grants and items never move, and revocation and retraction are final. The portal has its own cookie (`__Host-psid`), CSRF secret, rate limits, Fastify plugin and Vite bundle; lint keeps `withTenantTx` and `withPortalTx` apart.
- **Not principals:** web-chat visitors and public listing-page viewers. Visitors get a public widget key, an Origin allowlist, a server-issued session token, rate limits per widget and per IP, a honeypot and a 2,000-character limit. They reach data only through `app.webchat_poll` and `app.public_listing_page`. Whatever a visitor types is an unverified claim on the session, never a contact point and never an attachment to an existing contact.
- **Definer catalog additions.** Each is a security-review item. In 0015: `app.ad_allocation_cap_check` (deferred constraint trigger), `app.contacts_dnc_follow_merge`, `app.portal_party_ok`, and the two replaced dispatch guards. Also replaced in 0015 and still owned by `app_retention`: `app.purge_contact_internal` and `app.retention_sweep`. In module migrations: `app.resolve_channel_account`, `app.resolve_webchat_widget`, `app.webchat_session`, `app.webchat_poll`, `app.public_listing_page`, `app.record_person_opt_out`, `app.reinstate_person_contact`, `app.undo_language_opt_out`, `portal.request_login`, `portal.consume_login`, `portal.resolve_session`, `portal.end_session`, `portal.issue_document_stream`, `portal.document_object`, `portal.record_audit` (all in `0023_p3_portal_auth.sql`, §21.7) and `app.coaching_trends`. In the D2 migration: `app.mail_copy_live` (boolean, keyed on a synced copy's own row; the RESTRICTIVE read gate, §21.10) and the trigger `app.mail_sync_points_guard`. The D2 review fix migration replaces `app.resolve_channel_account` and `app.mail_copy_live` (same owner, search_path and grants; §21.8, §21.10). The batch 6 review migration `0047_api_accountability_period_end_withdrawn.sql` replaces `app.scoreboard_speed` and `app.scoreboard_rollup_roll` (same owner `app_definer`, pinned search_path, EXECUTE for `app_user` only, the same columns: timings and counts, no contact id): a first-touch window also ends at any event that takes the lead from its owner (§14); it adds no definer. The batch 6 review adds the trigger `app.webchat_sessions_follow_merge` (a website chat follows a person merge, §21.13). Module 34's `0049_calendar_member_removed.sql` adds the trigger `app.calendar_links_revoke_on_member_removed` (removing a member revokes their calendar link in the removal transaction, §21.16).
- **Webhooks** follow §3: verify the signature, idempotency, then `withTenantTx({kind:'system', via:'provider'})`. The tenant comes only from a `connected` or `degraded` connection. Its external account is globally unique, like a phone number, and only the provider callback writes it, after proving control of the account (R-INT-3, R-TEN-11). A lead email is a source lead only with a DKIM pass aligned to an allowlisted sender domain. **D2: Meta webhooks are out** (no DMs, no Lead Ads; the publishing connection subscribes to none, R-MKT-12); Gmail sync polls and receives no push (§21.10).
- **Decisions are people's.** Approving or rejecting an assistant action needs a user actor naming itself, with its role recorded. Ad money needs an Owner or Admin, and a decision is final (`assistant_actions_decision_guard`). Confirmed `manual` deal dates, legal holds, shorter retention and voice-on policies are written by people too: by the user actor, by an Owner/Admin, or by the Owner, respectively (DB).
### 21.2 Channels and the one outbound path
| Channel | Consent channel | Point kind / address | Sender | Allowed when |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| sms, mms | sms | phone | `phone_numbers` | Phase 1; outbound `mms` only as an agent's own one-to-one text (D5, R-MMS-1) |
| email | email | email | `email_identities` | Phase 1 |
| messenger | messenger | `fb:{page}:{psid}` | connection `messenger_page` | **Out of scope (D2): never cleared; removed by the D2 cleanup.** Always `PLATFORM_NOT_CLEARED` (`platform:meta` is never cleared); no platform window is built. |
| instagram | instagram | `ig:{account}:{igsid}` | connection `instagram_account` | **Out of scope (D2): never cleared; removed by the D2 cleanup.** Same as messenger. |
| web_chat | web_chat | `wc:{32 hex}` | connection `web_chat_widget` | while the session is open |
- Opt-outs. A keyword applies to that channel on all of the contact's points of that kind. Opt-out language, an agent's "do not contact", or closing a lead with reason `do_not_contact` sets a person-level do-not-contact and revokes every channel on every point. Both DB guards refuse `PERSON_DO_NOT_CONTACT`, including when a do-not-contact duplicate was merged into the recipient; the merge also copies it to the survivor. From web chat, opt-out language applies to the session's own contact only. These rules only refuse more than Phase 1 did; D1 stands.
- `canContact` is `core-compliance/3.0.0` and blocks at least what the guard blocks. Since ISA-1 the label is `core-compliance/3.1.0`: it adds the AI ISA checks 0, 8 and 12, which apply to the AI ISA categories only (docs/AI_ISA.md §5.4). Decisions for every other category are unchanged. The shared case table `packages/db/test/fixtures/opt_out_cases.json` runs against the DB guards now; module 19 runs it against core.
- New outbound paths all go through `dispatchOutbound`, each with a blocked-send path test (R-GATE-14). They are: web-chat replies (D2: no DM replies; the 60-second first response with step channel `inbound_channel`, and approved drafts only), open-house email invites (per recipient, only from an approved invite version, which the DB enforces), seller updates, milestone updates, trigger alerts, home-value notes, review requests, portal login links and notices, and voice booking confirmations.
- Reserved provider names (lint `realty/no-provider-outbound` extended): `deliverChannelMessage` (`comms/dispatch.ts`, web chat; D2: `sendDirectMessage` is dropped), `publishPost` and `postReviewReply` (`marketing/publish.ts`; publishing, not contact communication, behind `platform:fair_housing`, and for a GBP location also `platform:gbp` (module 53, §21.39); `postReviewReply` only through `sendReviewReply`; D2: `publishPost` posts to a Facebook Page, an Instagram business account or a GBP location, §21.11), and `applyBudgetChange` and `pauseCampaign` (`ads/dispatch.ts`; money, behind the DB cap ledger and `platform:ads`; the pause at the monthly cap is the only automatic money action). No interface has an AI outbound-call method.
### 21.3 Autonomy (approve rules, not every message)
| Output | Runs on its own inside an approved rule? | Always a tap |
|---|---|---|
| 60-s first response on web chat (D2: no DMs) | yes (`new_lead_response`, step channel `inbound_channel`) | |
| New listing in a saved search | yes (Phase 1 saved-search alerts, now with MLS rows; the one sender) | |
| Trigger alerts: price drop, back on market, home anniversary, deal date coming up | yes (trigger playbook; the digest window is approved content) | |
| Rate move | deferred (N9, C7); when it lands: one feed card per agent, a draft only on tap | |
| Milestone update to client/lender | yes (`transaction_updates`; human-confirmed dates only; contract or money wording ⇒ draft) | |
| Home value note | yes, once `platform:avm` is cleared, if fresh, displayable and within the range and jump limits; otherwise a draft | |
| Review request | yes (post-closing, once `platform:review_requests` is cleared) | |
| Seller update | never | yes, plus the agent's own recommendation (≥ 20 characters, DB) |
| Marketing piece | | yes, once per version, bound to its content hash and assets (DB); publishing waits for `platform:fair_housing` |
| Publish a post (Facebook Page, Instagram business, GBP; D2) | never | yes, always a tap by a listing writer, as themselves (`marketing_publications`, R-MKT-12); export otherwise |
| Voice answering | yes: inbound only, closed tool set, mandatory handback; needs `platform:voice` and the Owner's setting (DB) | the booking confirmation: a one-tap draft on the handback, never sent by the session (R-VOICE-17) |
| Ad budget shift | | yes (`money`; an Owner/Admin tap, single-use, exact amounts, DB ledger), pending D-ADS |
| GBP review reply (module 53) | never (the assistant drafts only on a person's tap) | yes: an Owner's or Admin's one tap, which is the claim (one provider call per approval); needs `platform:gbp` and `platform:fair_housing` |
| Contract extraction | | yes |
| Weekly plan, daily fix, prediction card | internal (no client message) | accepting creates tasks |
| Gmail sync (D2) | no output: read-only copies into the timeline; never sent, never in a model; a live client reply at most 1 hour old at fetch pauses and hands back once (R-GMAIL-10) | |
An inbound on any channel pauses automation and hands back (§10), including trigger firings: the guard refuses an autonomous send while paused, and module 22 cancels the contact's scheduled firings at ingestion. Voice answering is the one bounded live exception (D-VOICE). The call pauses enrollments as any inbound does, the session is limited to the closed tools of an approved `voice_answering` playbook, tool requests start only after the disclosure and the recording announcement, and the call always ends in a handback.
### 21.4 Timers (R-OWN-27)
Every Phase 2/3 timer stores its instant and is fenced. The owning module wakes it with one per-tenant reconcile handler (`JobHandler.every`, one line in `worker/handlers/index.ts`); no module edits `app.due_work`.
| Timer | Stored instant / fence | Handler (module, cadence) |
|---|---|---|
| MLS and feed sync | `integration_connections.next_sync_at`, sync cursor | 24, 60 s |
| Trigger firings | `playbook_step_runs.scheduled_for`, `trigger_key` | 26, 60 s → `trigger.step {stepRunId}` → `executeTriggerRun` |
| Home values, anniversaries | `avm:{contact_address}:{yyyy-mm}`, `anniversary:{deal}:{yyyy}` | 26, daily |
| Seller updates | `listings.seller_update_next_due_at` | 28, 5 min |
| Milestone reminders | `deal_dates.reminder_at`, `due_version`; repairs a confirmed date with no `due_at` | 31, 5 min |
| Weekly plan / daily fix | per user and local Monday / local date (dedupe keys) | 33 / 36, hourly |
| Prediction refresh | per tenant and date | 50, nightly |
| Homeowner refresh (D10) | `homeowner_profiles.next_refresh_at` (30 days after an answer, 1 day after an unavailable or timeout failure, 30 days after a rejected or invalid address; an address edit makes it due at once), a lookup budget per tenant and local date (100 by default); each write fenced on the address's `updated_at` and on recognition | 78, hourly at :41, acting only in the tenant's local night 02:00-05:00 (`homeowner.refresh`, standalone, 3 attempts); purges first, even while public records are not configured |
| Documents reconcile | `documents.scan_status = 'pending'` older than 2 minutes (re-enqueue the scan, deduped per document), `retention_until` empty on a closed scope (stamp close + 7 years), a blocked object no longer held (shred) | 42, 5 min (`documents.reconcile`, as the system) |
| Ad reconcile | per connection and hour; month-to-date pause at the cap; readback of unknown applies; weekly proposal via an hourly wake (the tenant's local Monday, once per ISO week) | 52, hourly |
| Voice handback reconcile | an AI call with no handback (partial index `calls_voice_handback_missing`) 10 minutes after it ended, or `maxCallSeconds` + 10 minutes after the takeover; a re-run writes nothing (R-VOICE-21) | 48, 5 min (`voice.reconcile`) |
| Gmail sync (D2) | `user_mail_links.next_sync_at` (plus revoked links that still have copies), Gmail `history_id` cursor (advanced only after the page commits) | 68, every `platform:gmail` `poll_interval_seconds` (120 s) → `mail.sync` |
| Portal grant expiry | `portal_grants.expires_at`, also compared with `now()` in the policy | none needed |
The calendar has no renew timer: tokens refresh lazily inside `calendar.push`, which is fenced by `appointments.sync_version`. The voice takeover is the telephony dial timeout decided when the call starts, not a timer.
### 21.5 Providers (all with deterministic fakes; real adapters refuse to start unconfigured)
Tenant connections (`integration_connections`, each with an honest status such as `needs_partner_access` or `pending_review`, shown in words): Meta for content publishing only (`facebook_page`, `instagram_business`; D2: no messaging, no Lead Ads, no ads; OAuth requests content-publishing permissions only, **VERIFY** names), lead feeds (Zillow, realtor.com) and the lead-email inbox, MLS (RESO-shaped), the showing service, ads (Google only, `ads_google`; D2) and GBP. Per-user: the calendar (Google, Microsoft) and Gmail (`user_mail_links`, read-only, §21.10; Microsoft mail is not requested). Platform configuration, not tenant rows: public records, the voice stack, the malware scanner, the document text extractor and the PDF renderer. Public records (module 50's one `PublicRecordsProvider`) are, by `PUBLIC_RECORDS_PROVIDER`: the deterministic fake (dev, tests, a staging demo with `ALLOW_FAKE_PROVIDERS`; it says on the homeowner panel "This deployment uses test public records: no county was asked, and these answers are test data."), the unconfigured stand-in (production otherwise: "Public records are not set up on this platform"), or the Beaufort County GIS connector (D10, `providers/publicRecords/**`): it serves only `lookupHomeowner` for recognised homeowners, never module 50's property `lookup`, and its kill switch `PUBLIC_RECORDS_LIVE_CALLS` is false by default everywhere, so no request is built until a deployment turns it on after DEPLOY "Before any live county call" (code-constant https URLs on `PUBLIC_RECORDS_ALLOWED_HOSTS`, no credential; §21.34, SECURITY §5a). Jasper County is links only. Module 42's adapters say what is true in their own words (`documents/provider.ts`): with no scanner configured (production until N11) "This platform has no virus scanner yet" (every upload stays locked); with no PDF reader (N17) "Reading dates from PDFs is not set up on this platform"; the fakes say "This deployment uses a test virus scanner: files are not really checked." and "This deployment uses a test PDF reader: dates are read only from test files." No AVM vendor (D9): home values and CMAs are computed in-house from the tenant's own MLS sold data (core/valuation; the `AvmProvider` interface stays source-neutral, and its in-house source is lane V2-B's). Their availability is whatever the adapter reports, combined with the platform clearance packs (R-INT-6). No rate feed ships (rate moves are deferred). Facebook Marketplace has no general listing API that we know of, so it is an export/copy kit. Module 53: `GbpProvider` (`apps/api/src/gbp/provider.ts`; GBP-shaped, every field **VERIFY**): `publishPost`/`lookupPost`/`deletePost` for `gbp_location` plus `listReviews` and `postReviewReply`; the deterministic `FakeGbp` in dev and tests, `UnconfiguredGbp` in production (no Google app, N15), which refuses every call.
### 21.6 Residual risks (Phase 2-3)
| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| D1 plus more automated messages (trigger alerts, home values, milestone updates, web-chat replies) may meet state mini-TCPA limits such as Florida's FTSA | opt-out and person-level blocks everywhere (core + DB, merge-safe); email-first built-in templates; digest windows; counsel item C1; reinstating limits stays a policy change. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL.** |
| A compromised API process can set a client GUC with any account id | the same class as Phase 1 Q3; the actor is built only by the portal resolver (lint + type); open question Q11 (a separate DB login for the portal) |
| ~~Platform webhooks share one app secret across tenants~~ **Obsolete (D2): no platform webhook is received** (no Meta DMs or Lead Ads; Gmail polls) | kept for history: the tenant is resolved only from a connected connection whose account the provider callback proved; payloads never choose the tenant |
| A web-chat visitor types someone else's email or phone | the claim never attaches, never receives anything automated and never silences anyone; a human merges after checking |
| Forged lead emails | DKIM-aligned allowlisted senders only; anything else is `needs_review` with no autonomous reply |
| Superuser sessions skip the decision guard | maintenance only (migrations, fixtures); no application path connects as a superuser (§16) |
| Meta policy and App Review for content publishing (D2) | publishing only (no messaging permissions, no webhooks); honest connection status; export is always the fallback; the product says "Posted" only for a provider-confirmed row (N2, **VERIFY** permissions); `platform:meta` is never cleared |
| MLS licence violations and volume | display rules on every client-facing render (default not allowed), staleness pause, per-tenant copies benchmarked at 500k rows |
| Fair-housing lexicon false negatives | every piece is human-approved and bound to its content; publishing waits for `platform:fair_housing`; prediction models ship withdrawn |
| Voice: disclosure law, all-party recording, latency, a made-up booking | inbound only; disclosure and announcement enforced by the DB before any tool request; unverified details never become appointments; voicemail fallback; ships dark until `platform:voice` (counsel + vendor latency spike) and the Owner's setting |
| Employee monitoring (call reviews) | private by default; human-handled calls only, attributed to that call's agent; TL access is an Owner setting; off until `platform:coaching`; no rankings; only the Owner turns reviews on (re-auth), and an Admin may only turn them off: `tenant_settings_p23_guard` admits an Admin's UPDATE only when it turns `call_scoring_enabled` off and leaves `tl_sees_call_scores` and `coaching_rubric_disabled` as they were (owner decision 2026-11-11: Owner on, Admin off, as Gmail sync, except that the system cannot turn call reviews off and an Admin's off needs no re-auth; E283), and the API refuses anything else first (`OWNER_REQUIRED`); each request names only the settings it changes and is decided, `can()` included, on the settings row read `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, so an Admin's off is never written back by an Owner's page loaded before it |
| Ads: platform overspend, attribution lag | ships dark until `platform:ads` (counsel review and the vendor check; outside D11): no proposal, approval, plan or pause while dark, the cap only alerts; DB cap ledger (cap version in force, exact approved amounts, single-use, tenant-wide total under a lock), hourly reconcile pause, sample holds, honest copy. Campaigns not managed here sit outside the daily ceiling (their budgets are shown, not counted) but their spend counts toward the monthly cap and they are paused at it (Q-ADS-3) |
| Gmail: Google's restricted-scope verification and security assessment (lead time, annual renewal) | ships dark: `platform:gmail` is cleared only with `rules.vendor_ready_at` (N19); the product works without it. **VERIFY** current requirements, lead time and renewal cadence |
| Gmail: a compromised system actor can read synced mail and decrypt refresh tokens | the same class as every other system read (Phase 1 Q3); tokens are envelope-encrypted with row-bound AAD, access tokens stay in memory, no bodies or addresses in logs, no AI use, scopes read-only by CHECK |
| Gmail: personal mail leaks through a contact who is also family or a friend | only mail with a current email point of a related contact is stored; "Remove this thread from Realty OS" hides at once and is final; the connect disclosure says so |
| Gmail: team members (TL, Owner, collaborators) read an agent's client mail | visibility is the contact timeline's, stated in the connect disclosure; employee notice and third-party correspondents are counsel item C14. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL** |
| Gmail: Google deletes or limits API access, or revokes the token | `invalid_grant` ⇒ `PROVIDER_REVOKED`, handled as a disconnect (copies deleted); the timeline keeps platform email; honest status |
| Parallel lanes landing module migrations out of order | the runner refuses a pending file that sorts before an applied one; each module migration (M21, M22, M29, M33, M43, M54) takes the next free number at merge, so migrations apply in merge order (PHASE2_3_PLAN §4) |

### Real-provider gaps (lane migration `0019_telephony_email_gaps.sql`)

| Id | Gap | Resolution |
| T1 | A real inbound call got empty TwiML and hung up. | `comms/voice.ts`: ring the owner (else the on-duty routing target, peeked, never advanced) from the team number; announcement first when the policy announces; a press-1 whisper; unanswered ⇒ missed call + voicemail with a fixed recording notice, copied encrypted and transcribed. The plan is stored on the call (`calls.ring_user_id`, `recording_decision.planned`), so a retried fetch gets the same TwiML. `agent_user_id` is set only on accept (recording scope R-PERM-8). |
| T2 | MMS media were counted, never stored. | `comms/media.ts`: fetch by SID only, type allowlist + magic bytes, 10 × 5 MB, per-object DEK, provider copy deleted, refusals recorded (`message_media_skipped`); download with nosniff and a sandbox CSP. |
| T3 | SES and Mailgun events were not parsed. | SNS signature (v1/v2, cert host rule, topic allowlist, 1 h window) and Mailgun HMAC (±5 min) feed the same `handleEvent` as Postmark/SendGrid. |
| T4 | `webhook_events` grew forever. | `app.purge_webhook_events()` (definer, real clock, no arguments): telephony 400 d, email 35 d, lead 2 d, rejected 30 d; downstream unique keys keep deduplicating after. |

### 21.7 Portal authentication (module 43): security notes for each definer
Migration `0023_p3_portal_auth.sql` (lane number; the orchestrator renumbers it) adds schema `portal`, three definer-only tenant tables and the client transaction helper `withPortalTx` (`packages/db/src/portalTx.ts`). Every function below is `SECURITY DEFINER`, owned by `app_definer` (NOLOGIN, BYPASSRLS), with `search_path = pg_catalog, public`, `EXECUTE` revoked from PUBLIC and granted to `app_user`. The migration's own self-check fails if any `portal` function loses its owner, its pinned search path or becomes PUBLIC-executable, or if `app_user` gains any privilege on the three tables (0001 §22 scans only `app` and `auth`). **No definer takes a caller-supplied "now"**: every expiry is compared with the database clock, so a caller cannot back-date a check (the class 0016 fixed in `app.retention_sweep`). Tests: `packages/db/test/portal_auth.test.ts` and `portal_auth.adversarial.test.ts` (a fast-check property over the download chain, plus a single-fault sweep that killed both hand-made mutants of the chain helper).
| Definer | Context | Reads / writes | What an attacker controls, and why it is safe |
|---|---|---|---|
| `portal.request_login(slug, address_hash, token_hashes[])` | pre-tenant | reads tenants, portal_accounts, contact_points; writes portal_login_tokens (supersedes older), audit_log | slug and hash are matched by equality only (no pattern); unknown slug/address, suspended tenant, removed point, disabled account and a throttled account (5 links per account per hour) all return zero rows and write nothing; refuses (`PORTAL_NOT_CLEARED`) until `platform:portal` is counsel-cleared, the same for every address. Returns the tenant, account, contact and login point so the API can send the link through `dispatchOutbound` (opt-outs apply); **the API must answer the HTTP request identically whatever it returns** and should send asynchronously so timing does not reveal a match (module 45). Spouses sharing an email get one link per account (E127). |
| `portal.consume_login(token_hash, session_hash, ip, ua)` | pre-tenant | updates portal_login_tokens (used_at), portal_accounts (status/activated_at only: column grant), inserts portal_sessions, audit_log | single use under a row lock (concurrent attempts: exactly one wins); 15-minute expiry and supersession by the DB clock; the account must still be invited/active and its login point must be the point the link was sent to and still current; a used token stays used even when a later check fails. Session: idle 24 h, absolute 14 days (CHECK caps 7 d / 30 d). **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL (C10) and the owner** for the lengths. |
| `portal.resolve_session(session_hash)` | pre-tenant | reads portal_sessions, portal_accounts, tenants; updates last_seen/idle expiry | the only source of a client context. Live, unexpired, unrevoked session of an active account of an active tenant while `platform:portal` is cleared (withdrawal stops every session at the next request). Staff sessions live in `sessions`, portal sessions here: neither resolver accepts the other's token (E148, DB half). |
| `portal.end_session(session_hash, reason)` | pre-tenant | updates portal_sessions, audit_log | revokes only the session whose token the caller holds. |
| `portal.issue_document_stream(item_id, token_hash)` | client (`withPortalTx`) | reads the chain (below); inserts portal_stream_tokens | refuses unless `app.principal_type() = 'client'`; never trusts the context alone: re-checks via `portal.document_chain_internal`. A staff session that sets `app.portal_account_id` gets NULL from `app.portal_account_id()` and nothing here (E149). |
| `portal.document_object(token_hash, ip, ua)` | client | updates portal_stream_tokens (used_at), audit_log; returns the object row (DEK still wrapped under the platform KEK) | token bound to tenant and account, 60 s, single use; **the chain is re-checked at download time** (unshared, retracted, revoked, lapsed, party removed, account disabled, object shredded, tenant suspended or clearance withdrawn after the token was issued: refused). Audited as `portal.document_downloaded`. |
| `portal.record_audit(action, entity_type, entity_id, ip, ua)` | client | inserts audit_log | the portal audit definer: a client principal cannot write `audit_log` (staff-only Layer 1). Only for the calling client's own active account; action `portal.<verb>` and four entity types only. |
| `portal.audit_internal(...)`, `portal.document_chain_internal(item)` | helpers | — | invoker rights, **not executable by app_user** (only by app_definer inside the definers above). The chain: client context names an active account of an active tenant → the item is an unretracted document item of an unrevoked, unexpired grant of that account → `app.portal_party_ok` still holds → the document is that item's document, of the grant's own deal or listing, not contact-scoped, shared and clean → its stored object is a live `document` object → `platform:portal` is cleared. |
- **Audit identity.** `audit_log.actor_kind` has no `client` value and this module changes no table, so portal rows are `actor_kind = 'system'`, `actor_via = 'api'`, with `changes.principal = 'client'` and `changes.portal_account_id`. Reports that count client actions filter on `changes->>'principal'`.
- **Tables.** `portal_login_tokens`, `portal_sessions` and `portal_stream_tokens` keep the staff Layer 1 policy, the support read-only policies and statement trigger, and a single permissive policy `USING (false)`; `app_user` has no privilege on them at all. `portal_stream_tokens` is not in PHASE2_3_PLAN §4's list: R-PORTAL-7's single-use 60 s token needs state, and it is definer-only like the other two.
- **The client actor.** `ClientActor` carries a module-private brand: only `resolvePortalSession` (and `consumePortalLogin`, which resolves the new session) constructs one. Lint `realty/portal-tx-separation` bans `withPortalTx` and client-actor literals outside `packages/db/src/portalTx.ts` and `apps/api/src/portal/client/**`. Inside `apps/api/src/portal/client/**` it bans by name every helper that opens its own staff, pre-tenant or no-context transaction: `withTenantTx`, `withPreTenant`, `withPreTenantThenTenant`, `withNoContext`, `actorGucs`, the ownership engine's `applyOwnershipCommand` and `applyOwnershipCommandThen` (for a staff actor) and `runOwnershipFollowup` (for the system actor, which sees the whole tenant), and the job queue's `claimJobs`, `finishJob`, `reapJobs` and `dueWork`. It also bans the pool itself (`getDatabase`, `createDatabase`). From `@realty/db` that code may take only the value exports of `portalTx.ts` (`withPortalTx` and the portal wrappers). Any other value import or re-export is reported, and so is taking the package whole (a namespace import, `export *`, `import()`, `require()`), so a helper the package exports later is refused there until it is listed. Type-only imports are fine, and `packages/eslint-plugin/test/config.test.js` fails when the list and `portalTx.ts` differ. Module 45 puts its client routes in that directory and its staff invite/grant/publish routes elsewhere, e.g. `apps/api/src/portal/staff/**`. `withPortalTx` sets exactly `app.tenant_id`, `app.principal_type = 'client'` and `app.portal_account_id` and clears `app.user_id`, `app.actor_kind` and `app.ownership_command`, transaction-locally.
- **Residual (Q11).** A compromised API process can still put any account id in the client GUCs: RLS trusts the context, as Phase 1 trusts `app.user_id`. The brand, the lint rule and the definers' re-checks narrow it; a separate DB login role for the portal remains open question Q11.
- **Not done here (later modules or ops).** Sending the link (module 45, `dispatchOutbound`), HTTP rate limits per IP and per address (module 45, `auth.rate_limit_hit`), the `__Host-psid` cookie and CSRF secret (module 45), and purging expired tokens and sessions (inert once expired; a retention step is not yet written).
### 21.8 Security notes: module 21 `api/integrations`
- **`app.resolve_channel_account(kind text, external_account_id text) → (tenant_id, connection_id)`** (module migration `*_integrations.sql`; security-review item). Pre-tenant `SECURITY DEFINER`, owned by `app_definer`, `search_path = pg_catalog, public`, `STABLE`, `EXECUTE` revoked from `PUBLIC` and granted to `app_user` only. It reads `integration_connections` joined to active `tenants` and returns two ids, nothing else (no credential, no status, no payload). It routes only `connected`/`degraded` rows (R-TEN-11): `pending_review` reserves an account but routes nothing, and every other status, a suspended tenant, an unknown account and the keyed kinds (web-chat widget, lead inbox) resolve to no row, which the caller records as unrouted (E136). The kind is part of the key, and the 0018 global live-account index means at most one row can match. Owner decision D2 removes Messenger/Instagram DMs, Meta Lead Ads and Meta ads, so `messenger_page`, `instagram_account`, `meta_lead_ads` and `ads_meta` are not routable at all (`ads_meta` since the D2 review fix migration, which replaces the resolver with 0024's body less that kind and the same owner, search_path and grants; 0024 is not edited). Module 21 never offers or connects them or `ads_meta`: the API lists them as "Not offered", refuses their connect start (`INTEGRATION_NOT_CONNECTABLE`), ends a callback for one with `STATE_INVALID` before any provider call, and the status machine never moves them toward a connection; a row made before D2 can still be disconnected. `ads_meta` stays in the kind CHECK until the D2 cleanup, and no path connects such a row. Publishing uses the 0025 kinds `facebook_page` and `instagram_business`, which are not routable either: they are never an inbound channel, so they never carry a lead source (CHECK `integration_connections_publishing_no_source`; the API refuses one first with `LEAD_SOURCE_NOT_ALLOWED`). The caller passes the account id from a signature-verified envelope; payload fields never choose the tenant. Tests: `apps/api/src/integrations/integrations.test.ts` (routing matrix, live pre-D2 `messenger_page`/`instagram_account`/`meta_lead_ads` rows that route nothing, owner/definer/search_path/ACL check) and `adversarial.test.ts`.
- **The provider callback is the only writer of routing keys** (R-INT-3). `GET /api/admin/integrations/:kind/callback` is a `public` route whose only authority is a MAC-signed OAuth state (HMAC-SHA256 under a key derived from the server session secret) naming tenant, connection, starter, kind, the requested account, a 10-minute expiry and the row's `updated_at` fence. It runs as the system actor of the tenant in the verified state, never request input. It re-checks the fence under a row lock, so a state is single-use and a race of two callbacks links once. It checks that the starter is still an active Owner/Admin. It proves control by listing the accounts the token manages, so a Page the tenant does not manage is refused and nothing is linked (E160). The global live-account index keeps an account live in one tenant (E135). The PKCE verifier is derived from the server secret and never leaves the server, so an injected authorization code fails the exchange. The token is stored only as an envelope-encrypted `provider_credentials` row. On every refusal the token is revoked at the provider (best effort) and only `last_error_code` is written. Disconnect sets the status and revokes the credential locally in the same transaction (R-INT-5). An `integrations.revoke_grant` job then decrypts the token as the system actor, revokes it at the provider (best effort) and purges the secret. The 0018 guard stays the backstop: a person writing `external_account_id`, `credential_id`, a live status or `capabilities` is refused.
- **Honesty.** In production, without an explicit staging demo flag, every adapter reports "not set up on this platform" (OAuth `configured: false`, partner feeds and MLS `needs_partner_access`). No fake runs for a real team. Requested OAuth scopes are least-privilege catalog data marked VERIFY. Meta requests publishing scopes only, and a test proves that no messaging or Lead Ads scope is ever requested, including a Meta scope under a new name that mentions messaging. Each platform pack's sentence says "cleared" only when `app.platform_cleared` clears it (R-INT-6): `platform:voice` and `platform:gmail` stay off until `rules.vendor_ready_at` is set, even after counsel review, and `platform:meta` says it is never cleared (D2) rather than awaiting review.
### 21.8a Security notes: lead-email intake (module 22's lead-email slice, pulled forward 2026-11-11)
- **Webhook.** `POST /api/webhooks/lead-email/inbound` (generic, X-Realty-Signature over the raw body, 5-minute window), `…/postmark/inbound` and `…/sendgrid/inbound` (Basic, only when the format is enabled). The guard verifies before the handler; a bad signature writes only a global rejected `webhook_events` row. Body caps (1 MB JSON, 5 MB multipart) apply before parsing; attachments are dropped unread; HTML is stripped by a linear single-pass scanner (capped input and output); links and images are never fetched. Every parsed value is cleaned of CR/LF, C0/C1 controls and bidi overrides, and phones/emails are re-normalized by core before they become contact points, so no parsed field can inject a header into a later outbound message.
- **Token resolver.** `app.resolve_lead_email_key(bytea) → (tenant_id, connection_id, lead_source_id)` (migration `*_lead_email_intake.sql`; security-review item): pre-tenant `SECURITY DEFINER`, owned by `app_definer`, `search_path = pg_catalog, public`, `STABLE`, `EXECUTE` revoked from `PUBLIC`, granted to `app_user`; input is sha256 of the 160-bit key from the recipient `leads+<key>@<LEAD_EMAIL_INBOUND_DOMAIN>` (other domains never resolve); returns at most one row, only for the CURRENT key of a connected/degraded `lead_email` connection whose lead source is active in an active tenant; no write, no payload. Lead inboxes are never resolved through `app.resolve_channel_account`. Keys are stored as a global-unique hash plus an envelope-encrypted copy (Owner/Admin see the address again; support never reads keys); rotation retires the old key in the same transaction (final, trigger) and is audited.
- **Trust rule (R-LEM-3).** A lead is created automatically only when the receiving provider's DKIM verdict is a pass whose `d=` and the From domain both sit under one allowlisted sender domain of that inbox. Sender-written DKIM-Signature / Authentication-Results headers are never evidence (Postmark: only the topmost Authentication-Results with the configured authserv-id). Anything else is held in Needs review with its reason; nothing is created, routed or sent (E153). A person's acceptance goes through the same intake, audited, with no autonomous first response.
- **Replay.** `webhook_events` keyed per inbox (`lead-email:<connection>:<sha256(format, message id)>`, so a sender-chosen Message-ID cannot pre-empt another tenant), then `UNIQUE (tenant_id, message_key_sha256)` on `lead_email_messages` in the lead's own transaction (survives the webhook_events purge), serialized by an advisory lock on the message key.
- **RLS.** Both new tables are ENABLE + FORCE RLS with the Layer-1 tenant and support-read-only policies; writes are the system actor's only; people's decisions re-read their role under the system actor (the `mls/spark` pattern).

### 21.8d Security notes: lead intake continuation (module 22, 2026-11-12; migration `0062_lead_intake`; lane number 0110)
- **Google Ads lead form route.** `POST /api/webhooks/google-ads/lead-form`: one fixed URL with no path secret (nothing to redact in nginx). The key travels in the body (`google_key`); the guard checks it is a 1–200 character string, hashes it (sha256) and resolves it through `app.resolve_google_lead_form` before the handler runs. Google signs no timestamp, so replay protection is idempotency: `gads:<lead_id>` as the intake's external id (a replay is `duplicate_ignored`) plus `webhook_events`. Every failure to resolve (absent, malformed, wrong, retired, off source, inactive tenant) is the same 403 with a tenant-less rejected row. A lookup that throws (pool or statement timeout) is `WEBHOOK_UNAVAILABLE` 503 with no row, so Google retries (its 5XX/429 retry semantics are **VERIFY**); the guard rethrows only that code, every other exception stays a 403. The route is out of the global per-IP limiter (`rateLimit: false`); rejected deliveries are counted per IP only after the key failed to resolve, so no limiter refuses a request whose key resolves. `google_key` never appears in logs, errors, audit rows, the `webhook_events` payload or responses.
- **`app.resolve_google_lead_form(bytea)`** (SECURITY REVIEW ITEM): pre-tenant `SECURITY DEFINER`, owned by `app_definer`, `search_path = pg_catalog, public`, `STABLE`, `EXECUTE` for `app_user` only. Input: a 32-byte hash (anything else resolves nothing). Output: at most one `(tenant_id, hook_id, lead_source_id)`, for the current (unretired) hook of an active source in an active tenant. Writes nothing, reads no payload. Proof: `packages/db/test/rls_smoke_intake.test.ts`, `leadForms/googleLeadForm.test.ts` (E315, E319, E591).
- **`app.rearm_webhook_event(text, text, timestamptz)`** (SECURITY REVIEW ITEM): `SECURITY DEFINER` (webhook_events is definer-only), owned by `app_definer`, pinned `search_path`, `VOLATILE`, `EXECUTE` for `app_user` only. One conditional UPDATE: it re-arms only a row with `signature_valid` and a tenant, whose status is `failed`, or `received` with `COALESCE(last_attempt_at, received_at)` older than 5 minutes (**VERIFY 15** against the slowest handler). Unrouted and rejected rows never re-arm; two concurrent retries serialize on the row and the second sees a fresh `last_attempt_at`, so the row re-arms once. Proof: `rls_smoke_intake.test.ts`, `comms/webhooks.rerun.test.ts` (E354).
- **Credentials.** The Google key is re-showable to Owner/Admin (envelope-encrypted `key_enc`, as the lead-email key), because Google's form setup asks for it again; the source's webhook signing secret is shown once (at rotation) and lives in the system-only `lead_source_webhook_secrets` (not on `lead_sources`, which every member reads). The webhook guard decrypts the secret as the system actor of the token's tenant (`leads/webhookAuth.ts`, the `verifyTwilioAccount` pattern). Every write to a source's credentials (turn on, rotate, PATCH, off, webhook rotation) takes the source's credential lock first (`lockAddresses([sha256('lead-source-credentials:<id>')])`, global lock order step 1), then re-reads the source; `lead_sources` is never locked FOR UPDATE (every lead insert's FK check would wait on it), and a 23505 never reaches the caller (E338, E593).
- **Tracked-number lock order.** On a tracking number of an active source, the inbound transaction takes the caller's address lock and the intake's whole lock set (`intakeLockKeys`: identifier HMACs, fingerprint, external key) in ONE sorted `lockAddresses` call before matching, so a tracked call racing a signed webhook lead for the same new phone never deadlocks (E324). The zero-match path then runs `ingestInquiryInTx` (which takes nothing new at step 1) and clears the ownership-command setting before any further write. The matched-path ingest (a pond or unassigned person, R-TRK-3) runs after step 3's `lockContacts(sharing)`; the intake's attach contact is one of `sharing` (same HMAC), whose lock the transaction already holds (step 2 in order).
- **Routing preview.** `POST /lead-sources/:id/preview` runs as the person (Owner/Admin are tenant-wide under RLS) and calls core `previewRouting` over `loadRules` and the router's own candidate query (`loadRuleMembers` + `loadRoutingCandidates`, no locks, no contact: no release cooldown). It reads `routing_rotation_state` without FOR UPDATE and without the router's INSERT, and writes nothing, not even an audit row (E334's test compares the tables before and after).
- **Why no `integration_connections` kind.** A Google lead form has no external account and no OAuth, and only the system writes it; a new kind would widen 0018's live-account CHECK and the guard's exemption list, which is a weaker change than its own system-only table.
- **Residuals.** Anyone holding a Google key or a webhook secret can post leads until it is rotated. The deployment-wide webhook secret stays valid for sources without their own (Q-LI-6 default). Forwarding trust depends on the original DKIM signature surviving the forward (VERIFY 4). The consumer-mailbox list is finite (VERIFY 6). An unescaped injected `lead_*` meta element in an email from an allowlisted sender is read like a typed form field. Owner/Admin may tie any tracking number to any source. Owned callers on a tracked number are not credited (Q-LI-2 default). The ring target ignores the number's source until module 48. What arrives on an off source is lost (Q-LI-8 default; Google keeps leads 30 days); a partner feed's leads leave a skip receipt (`lead_source_skips`) and are not replayed when the source is turned back on. The SMS media-copy enqueue, which runs in its own transaction after ingest, is not re-run by the re-arm.

### 21.8b Security notes: module 24 `api/mls`
- **No SECURITY DEFINER function, policy or grant is added.** MLS rows are written by the system actor through the 0001 `listings_insert` / `listings_update` policies (`source = 'team' OR actor_kind = 'system'`) and the 0018 `listings_mls_monotonic` trigger (R-MLS-1, E141). The upsert updates only when the incoming modification is strictly newer and from the same connection, so the trigger is the backstop, not the path. Migration `*_mls_listing_indexes.sql` adds partial indexes only.
- **Leakproof note (performance under RLS).** `left()` and `lower()` are not leakproof, so under FORCE RLS the planner keeps a qual that applies them to row data out of index conditions (a scan of every MLS row). The MLS candidate queries compare plain columns with `texteq` (leakproof): postal codes as stored (**VERIFY WITH MLS** that the feed's RESO `PostalCode` is the 5-digit code: core normalize stores it as sent, and the repo's own fixture sends a ZIP+4; open item below) and cities by their stored spellings, found case-insensitively by a loose index scan (`mlsCitySpellings`). Nothing is marked LEAKPROOF. Measured at 500k MLS rows (`apps/api/src/mls/volume.test.ts`).
- **One decision for client output** (`mls/eligibility.ts`): an MLS row reaches a client only while its connection is live, its licence pack is counsel-reviewed with attribution and disclaimer, the feed is fresh, the broker allows internet display, and it does not mirror a team listing; then core `applyDisplayRules(client)` renders it. Saved-search matching, previews, alert rendering and the queue-time re-check in `assistant/searches.ts` all call it, and `mls.compliance_sweep` cancels queued alerts that stopped passing it (R-MLS-2, R-MLS-3, E85, E86). New matches have one sender, the Phase 1 `search_alert` path, so the Owner's `search_alert` switch and the send-time gate apply unchanged (R-MLS-6).
- **Spark key use.** The sync decrypts the key as the system actor in its claim transaction and reads outside any transaction (`openSparkFeed`); only codes and counts are logged or written.
- **D9 sold data (lane V1-A; R-CMA-1).** The sync now stores the MLS sale and property facts as S0's listings columns (close price and date, contract date, original list price, concessions, sale conditions, days on market, integer-microdegree coordinates with the generated `geo_cell`, year built, lot size in sq ft, subdivision, garage spaces and stories in tenths, private pool, the size field's source, `is_lease`, `avm_display_allowed`), plus `address_key` (core `addressKey` over the structured RESO parts, the state folded to its USPS code; computed in the api so core/listings never imports predict, R-PRED-4) and `mls_normalize_version` (2). Core normalize bounds every value by its column's CHECK and type (F3); an invalid or out-of-bound new value is null and named in `valuation.unreadable`; beds / baths_x10 / sqft out of bound are `MLS_FIELD_INVALID`; dates must be real calendar dates. Still the system actor, no definer, no new policy; S0's invoker guard `listings_valuation_facts_guard` keeps every person off these columns. Team rows carry the empty facts.
- **Per-record savepoint (F3).** Each record's database work in the fenced write (nurture's pre-read, the upsert, the backfill fill) runs under `SAVEPOINT mls_record`. SQLSTATE classes **22** (data exception, e.g. a NUL byte) and **23** (integrity violation, e.g. a CHECK) roll back to it and count the record as `skippedInvalid` with the code `MLS_RECORD_REFUSED_BY_DB` (never the message); the batch goes on and the cursor advances. Every other error re-throws, so `40001` / `40P01` still retry the whole transaction (`tenantTx`); a retried attempt restarts its counts (a latent double count on retry was fixed). Proof: `apps/api/src/mls/sold.sync.test.ts` (a forced 23514 in a batch of 500 leaves 499; a forced 40001 retries).
- **Backfill pass (F14, E372).** Migration `0068_cma_mls_data.sql` (lane number 0101) sets `mls_backfill_pending` once on every `mls_feed` connection (as the table owner, which S0's backfill guard admits); it never touches `sync_cursor`. Each run reads its incremental page first, unchanged; only when that page is not full does it read one backfill page (`records(mls_backfill_cursor, SYNC_BATCH)`, outside any transaction); the fenced write also checks the backfill cursor. A backfill record is written by a fill-only UPDATE keyed on connection, MLS number and an EQUAL modification time with `mls_normalize_version < 2`: it never inserts, never sets an existing column (no price history), is never fed to saved-search matching (R-MLS-6) or nurture, and never moves `sync_cursor` or `last_synced_at` (R-MLS-3 freshness stays the incremental feed's). While pending, the next run is due at once (a failed backfill read waits the normal interval); a page with `more` false ends it. Rows the key never returns again stay at version 1 with null facts and are never candidates. Cost: one extra Spark page per run while pending (SECURITY §5).
- **Sold-data reads (`mls/comps.ts`).** `soldCandidates`, `mlsRowsAtAddress` and `liveNearMatches` run under the caller's RLS with plain int4 / text / date / uuid predicates (leakproof on PG16), so they are index conditions on the 0068_cma_mls_data partial indexes (`listings_mls_sold_{postal,subdivision,cell,city}_idx`, `listings_mls_address_key_idx`). Candidates are `status = 'sold' AND NOT is_lease AND close_date IS NOT NULL` inside the window; one ordered, limited subquery per value; each read reports whether it hit its limit. Measured at 500k rows with skewed areas (`volume.test.ts`).
- **Open item, VERIFY WITH MLS: `PostalCode` may carry a ZIP+4.** Every postal read on MLS rows is a plain equality on the stored `postal_code`: `soldCandidates`' postal-code tier, the subdivision tier's near clause and the rate pool's postal branch (`mls/comps.ts`), `liveNearMatches`, api/cma's context listings (`cma/service.ts`, which tries the code as entered and its first 5 digits) and module 24's saved-search and competing reads on `listings_mls_postal_idx`. The subject's code is folded to 5 digits (core `postalKey`), but stored rows are not: a feed that sends `NNNNN-NNNN` in `PostalCode` would match none of them, and a subject without coordinates would reach only the city tier. If an MLS sample shows this, the fix is a new migration (never an edit of 0001, 0042 or 0068): a postal-key column filled at write time from core `postalKey`, backfilled, with partial indexes mirroring the postal ones, and these reads switched to plain equality on it; with a comps/CMA test of a stored ZIP+4 row and a subject without coordinates.
- **Valuation gates (`mls/eligibility.ts`; R-CMA-6, R-CMA-7: licence and eligibility parts).** The licence pack's `valuation` section has its own counsel stamp (E371). `valuationEligible` gives a code and a sentence for every refusal (no connection, not live, the pack does not allow the use, the section not reviewed, no statement for the subject's state, the display pack not client-eligible, `FEED_STALE`); agent CMAs need only a live connection and `valuation.agent_cma`; client uses need all of it. `clientSoldView` is the only client rendering of a mirrored team sale (F4); `clientView` is unchanged.

### 21.8c Security notes: channels and inbox (module 22, wave 2A)
Migration `0044_channels_inbox.sql` (lane branch number 0042; renumbered at merge). Proof: `apps/api/src/channels/{webchat,channels,adversarial}.test.ts`.
- **`app.resolve_webchat_widget(bytea) → (tenant_id, connection_id, lead_source_id, live, allowed_origins)`** (security-review item): pre-tenant `SECURITY DEFINER`, owner `app_definer`, `search_path = pg_catalog, public`, `STABLE`, `EXECUTE` revoked from `PUBLIC`, granted to `app_user`; no write. Input is sha256 of the public 160-bit widget key (`wk_` + 40 hex; any other shape is never looked up). An unknown key returns no row (404). A known key whose widget is not connected/degraded, has no lead source, or whose tenant is not active returns `live = false` with every id NULL (the API answers `CHANNEL_NOT_CONNECTED`, R-INBOX-7) and reveals no tenant. The widget's allowed origins are returned only when live and are enforced by the route (another website: 404). Widget keys are stored only as a global-unique hash; the key is shown once at creation or rotation (Owner/Admin, re-auth, audited `webchat.widget_*`).
- **`app.webchat_session(bytea) → (tenant_id, session_id, connection_id, contact_id, contact_point_id, open, widget_live)`** (security-review item): same shape and grants. Input is sha256 of the 256-bit session token the start response returned once; returns at most one row, nothing for an inactive tenant. `open` = not closed and seen in the last 30 minutes (E77). Every write after it (touching the session, ingesting the visitor's message) runs as the system actor of the returned tenant under the 0018 `webchat_system` policy.
- **`app.webchat_poll(bytea, timestamptz, uuid, int) → (message_id, body, sent_at, author)`** (security-review item, R-TEN-12): same shape and grants; the only read a visitor has. It returns outbound `web_chat` messages whose recipient is THIS session's own chat identity (`wc:` + 32 random hex, never typed by anyone) through this session's widget, in status sent/delivered only (a blocked, canceled, queued or failed message is never shown), while the widget is live and the tenant active, at most 50, after a `(sent_at, id)` cursor. `author` is `team` for a person and `assistant` otherwise (the assistant never passes as human). Nothing about the contact, the team or other channels is returned.
- **`app.purge_webchat_sessions(timestamptz) → integer`** (security-review item, R-INBOX-5): `SECURITY DEFINER` owned by `app_retention` (it holds DELETE on `webchat_sessions`; `app_user` has none), `search_path = pg_catalog, public`, `EXECUTE` revoked from `PUBLIC`, granted to `app_user`. It requires a tenant context and the system actor (else `WEBCHAT_PURGE_SYSTEM_ONLY`) and deletes only that tenant's contact-less sessions whose `purge_after` has passed (spam, E137). Called hourly by the per-tenant `channels.webchat_purge` job; `app.retention_sweep` is not replaced.
- **`app.record_person_opt_out(uuid, uuid)`** is NOT a definer (invoker; self-check in the migration): it sets `contacts.do_not_contact_at` once, citing a revoke of that same contact, under the caller's RLS; it only refuses more. The lifting definers named for M22 (`app.reinstate_person_contact`, `app.undo_language_opt_out`) are not built: lifting stays fail-closed (lane gap).
- **Schema-gap fix (review item).** `gate_decisions.channel` CHECK gains `web_chat` (0018 widened every other channel CHECK but this one, so a blocked chat send could not be recorded, R-GATE-14). Nothing else changes; the Meta channels stay refused.
- **Public routes.** `GET /api/public/chat/:widgetToken` (the widget key), `POST …/:widgetToken/start`, `POST /api/public/chat/session/:token/messages`, `GET …/session/:token/poll`: no session, no cookie (credential-less CORS from any origin on this prefix only, server.ts); the two POSTs are `capabilityUrl` routes (the key/token in the URL is their only authority). The widget's allowed origins are enforced where the key is resolved (config, start); the session routes rely on the token alone, so removing a website or rotating the key stops new chats there, not open ones (disconnecting ends them). An ended session's poll returns nothing (`open: false`), so a token is no read key after the chat. nginx writes the session token as `-` in its access log and no request in its error log (DEPLOY §2). Bodies are strict Zod (no tenant, contact or address fields), 16 KiB limit (413), no control or format characters (header/log injection: 422). DB-backed rate limits (`auth.rate_limit_hit`): starts 5/min and 20/h per IP and 60/min per widget; messages 20/min per session, 60/min per IP, 600/min per widget; polls 90/min per session, 600/min per IP. IPs are stored only as an HMAC. Spam openings create no contact (session purged after 30 days, E137).
- **Trust decisions.** The phone or email a visitor types is stored only as `webchat_sessions.claimed_*`: never a contact point, never matched to attach (R-INBOX-1, E138: a match with a person's point, or with what an earlier chat lead typed, raises a duplicate suggestion "typed in chat, not verified"), never sent anything automated (E154). A person who may see the lead in detail sees it (`GET /contacts/:id` `typedInChat`, read under 0018 `webchat_staff_read`), marked "typed in chat, not verified", on the lead card, in Details (with "Add as phone/email": the agent's own decision, after which it is an ordinary point) and in compose once nothing else can reach them (E77). It is kept with the lead, moves with the person in a merge (the session follows the survivor, §21.13), and is removed with it on erasure (the session's ON DELETE CASCADE); a session without a contact never holds one. Opt-out language in chat sets the person-level do-not-contact of the session's own contact only (E155). Replies go only through `dispatchOutbound` (TX1 → `deliverChannelMessage` → TX2; the reserved name is linted and architecture-tested, R-GATE-15); a closed session or a disconnected widget is `CHANNEL_NOT_CONNECTED` at send time.
- **Reachability of a chat (read-only).** `GET /contacts/:id/reachability` and `GET /reachability` carry `channels.web_chat`: the chat identity a reply goes to (compose's own choice, `chatRoutes`/`chatRoutesFor`: the open session's first; shown as "Website chat", never the `wc:` value), its opt-outs (`pointBlocks` over every chat identity of the person, R-CHAN-4: the gate's own `chatFanoutHmacs` set, which since the batch 6 review reads every record of the person as the 0043 guard does), the person-level do-not-contact (R-CHAN-2) and `session {open, widgetConnected}`, the facts of the gate's check 10 (either false is `CHANNEL_NOT_CONNECTED`), read under the caller's RLS (0018 `webchat_staff_read`). `channels/chatReachability.test.ts` proves it agrees with the send-time gate reason for reason (open, idle 30 minutes, widget disconnected, a language opt-out in the chat, "stop texting me" from an added phone, a do-not-contact a merge carried). It decides nothing: the send-time check does.
- **The person-level do-not-contact before the tap (batch 6 review optouts/F1).** Every channel of the summary, the website chat included, carries `PERSON_DO_NOT_CONTACT` (core `REACHABILITY_REASONS`, `pointBlocks` check 9) whenever any record of the person asked not to be contacted (`repos/reachability.ts personDoNotContactFor`: the gate's `personDoNotContact` set, `app.contact_person_ids`, so one a merge carried counts), with its own evidence and sentence ("Asked not to be contacted at all (recorded by Ava Agent on Nov 10)"); the summary's `doNotContact` names it once for the person. The feed's channel hint (`workspace/repos/feedInputs.ts gateSummaries`) denies every channel, the chat included, with the same words, so a card never puts a chat Reply first for such a person and keeps "Can't reach them right now". Core's property test proves the summary's reasons are exactly canContact's for generated contacts with chat identities and the do-not-contact.
- **Message-ID hash (R-GMAIL-12).** Platform inbound email stores `rfc_message_id_sha256` in the system ingestion; outbound stores the hash of the Message-ID the provider confirmed at TX2 (system). TX1 runs as the person, so the 0030 `messages_rfc_guard` refuses it there. Only the plain SMTP relay and the fake report the Message-ID; SES, Mailgun, SendGrid and Postmark report none until verified (VERIFY), so those rows are not deduplicated. The email handback dedupe key is `email:{contact}:{hash}` (E201).

### 21.9 Core content modules (lane p23-core-content: modules 20, 23, 25, 27, 49)
Pure core only: no migration, no SECURITY DEFINER function and no I/O. Security-relevant choices for reviewers:
- **Fair housing (20).** One pack `fh/1` (`DEFAULT_FAIR_HOUSING_PACK`, deeply frozen) drives the copy check, the assistant/voice guard and the targeting validator. Detection folds obfuscation (NFKC, invisible characters, look-alike letters, leetspeak, spelled-out letters). A bare negation never hides a term; only an equal-opportunity frame within 3 words downgrades a hard match to soft, which still blocks autonomy. Jurisdiction data can only add terms or escalate soft to hard; pack patterns are a closed word grammar, never regular expressions. Mutation score on `check.ts` 99.6 % (`pnpm exec stryker run src/fairhousing/testing/stryker.config.mjs` in `packages/core`). **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL (C3, C5)**: the lexicon itself.
- **Content guard (25).** Any fair-housing match adds `FAIR_HOUSING` to `contentGuard` (so the step is a draft flagged "Check this"); the pack defaults to `fh/1` and cannot be switched off. `inbound_channel` steps validate only in `new_lead_response`; until module 22 lands, `buildStep` skips them visibly.
- **Prediction (49).** `PredictionFeatures` cannot express anything but the six allowlisted features; `realty/no-prediction-import` keeps `core/predict` and `core/coaching` out of ownership, priority, ads, portal, comms and lead routing (R-PRED-4).
### 21.10 Gmail inbox sync (D2)
Read-only copies of an agent's own Gmail conversations with their clients, shown in the contact timeline (module 68 `apps/api/src/mailSync/**`, module 69 web). Dark until `platform:gmail` is cleared (counsel C14 + Google verification N19) and the Owner turns `tenant_settings.mail_sync_enabled` on (R-GMAIL-1). Rules R-GMAIL-1..14, tables DATA_MODEL §6.3.

**Flow.**
1. **Connect.** The agent reads the disclosure (its hash is stored) and starts OAuth with PKCE and a `state` bound to their session; scope `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/gmail.readonly` only (**VERIFY**). Gmail uses its own OAuth client (at least never `include_granted_scopes`), so a calendar grant never rides along and the exact-scope check holds (**VERIFY** Google's granted-scope semantics).
2. **Callback** runs as the system actor: it exchanges the code, refuses any token whose granted scopes are not exactly that one (E225), reads the mailbox profile (`users/me`) and inserts `user_mail_links` with the profile's address and its HMAC under the tenant HMAC key (`mailbox_address_hmac`, immutable). The DB refuses a second live link for the user, or for the mailbox in any tenant (R-GMAIL-2).
2a. **Confirm addresses** (S-D2-1). Module 69 shows "Sync Gmail with these clients?": the current email points of the agent's related contacts, one bulk confirm (`POST /me/mail-sync-points`); later each new address is a one-tap suggestion, and "Stop syncing this address" deletes the confirmation (`DELETE /me/mail-sync-points/:pointId`). Only the link's own user inserts `mail_sync_points`, as themselves, on their own live link; the definer guard `app.mail_sync_points_guard` refuses an unrelated contact, a point not current, and an address that is any live link's mailbox in the tenant. Nothing syncs for an address the agent did not confirm, so a peer who adds a stranger's address to a contact and makes the agent a collaborator or deal member, or a TL who adds the agent's own mailbox as a contact point, captures nothing (E203).
3. **Sync.** Module 68's per-tenant reconcile handler (`mail.sync`) picks links whose `next_sync_at` is due, decrypts the refresh token, gets an access token in memory and calls `history.list` from the stored `history_id`.
