For each added message: skip SPAM, TRASH, DRAFT and CHAT; parse the headers; normalize and HMAC every From/To/Cc/Bcc address; for a message **from** an address that matches any current email point of the tenant, classify once with the deterministic `classifyInbound` **before and regardless of storage** (opt-outs, R-GMAIL-9, S-D2-3), recording each result as platform email does (an unsubscribe or keyword, and a wrong-number reply with source `wrong_number`, revoke email on that point; opt-out language sets the person-level do-not-contact): an excluded thread, the per-thread cap, an unrelated contact or an unconfirmed address stores nothing, and the consent event then cites `{"kind":"synced_gmail","gmail_message_ref":"<sha256 hex of link id + Gmail message id>"}` instead of the copy (E229); outside excluded threads, match the **confirmed**, current email points of contacts the mailbox owner owns, collaborates on or shares a deal with; strip quoted history and the signature (best effort); insert one `mail_messages` row per matched contact (the DB guard re-checks the link, the exclusion, the relation, the point, the confirmation, that the address is no live mailbox, the window, the per-thread cap and the platform-email dedupe). For a **live** one whose Gmail `internalDate` is within 1 hour of the fetch, call `ingestSyncedMailInTx(tx, contactId, mailMessageId, classification)`, module 68's one flagged function in `comms/inbound.ts` (after module 22): the Phase 1 lock, pause, cancel of queued assistant messages and scheduled trigger runs, and exactly one handback "replied in Gmail" citing the `mail_message` by id and time, with no body snippet and no `assistant.enrich_handback` job; no `messages` row is written and nothing is classified again (R-GMAIL-10). Phase 1 `ingestInbound` is not used: it writes a `messages` row, needs a conversation, re-classifies, quotes the body in the handback and enqueues the AI enrichment. An older live message (a resync, an outage) is stored and opt-out-classified but neither pauses nor hands back. The cursor advances only after the page's rows commit.
4. **Deletes.** A message deleted, trashed or marked spam in Gmail deletes its copies; so does an exclusion, a removed contact point, a withdrawn confirmation (S-D2-2; those copies are hidden at once by the read policy), and a platform `messages` row with the same Message-ID hash (the platform row wins, R-GMAIL-12). Losing the relation (a reassignment) deletes nothing: the new owner reads the copies with the timeline (E216).
5. **Expired history id.** A bounded resync inside the backfill window, never a full-mailbox read; what it finds pauses only under the 1-hour rule (R-GMAIL-11, R-GMAIL-10).
6. **Backfill** at connect and on "import again": at most `mail_backfill_days` (Owner, 0–90, default 30) and `backfill_max_messages`, paged with `backfill_page_token`; origin is the DB's (`internalDate` < `connected_at` ⇒ backfill); backfill never pauses automation or creates handbacks (R-GMAIL-8).

**Tokens.** Only the refresh token is stored: envelope-encrypted under the tenant field key with AAD bound to table, column and row (`crypto/envelope.ts`), decrypted only by the system actor inside a sync pass. The link's own user can read `refresh_enc` under RLS (the system actor uses the same `app_user` role, so no column grant can separate them, S-D2-7): module 68's architecture test forbids selecting it outside `mailSync/token.ts`, and no route returns it. A person's disconnect changes no system-owned sync column beyond deleting the token and page token (S-D2-6). Access tokens never leave memory. Revoking deletes the token in the same transaction (CHECK) and revokes it at Google, best effort and retried (R-GMAIL-4).

**Disconnecting** (the agent, member deactivation, the Owner's switch off, the pack uncleared, `invalid_grant`) revokes the link: token deleted at once; copies hidden at once by the RESTRICTIVE policy and deleted by the reconcile handler within one sync interval (`poll_interval_seconds`, 120 s by default; the handler also selects revoked links that still have copies, not only links whose `next_sync_at` is due); the link and its exclusions purged at `purge_after` (≤ 30 d); database backups expire per DEPLOY backup retention (DEPLOY §8: point-in-time window of at least 35 days; **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL**). Erasure deletes the contact's copies (R-DATA-7). Merge: the system re-points the merged contact's copies to the survivor and its confirmed email point with the same address HMAC (the only UPDATE the DB allows on a copy); a copy that would collide with the survivor's own copy of the same Gmail message, or whose survivor point is unconfirmed, is deleted instead (R-GMAIL-13). Default for Q-GMAIL-1: delete, not keep.

**Visibility.** The contact's timeline visibility plus the mailbox owner for their own link; never a support actor (even under a grant) and never a client principal (R-GMAIL-6). Team visibility stays the timeline audience by default (owner question Q-GMAIL-5; the connect disclosure says so; employee and third-party notice is C14). The RESTRICTIVE read policy calls `app.mail_copy_live(tenant, copy)`, a definer that takes only the copy's own identity and checks the link, the exclusion, the point (current) and its confirmation (S-D2-2, S-D2-4), and answers only a caller the read policies admit for that copy (a staff user or the system who can read it: the system, the mailbox owner or a reader of the contact's timeline), so a support actor, a client principal or a peer who holds the copy's id from consent evidence learns nothing (D2 review fixes); no function answers for a link or thread by id. The read policy tests the mailbox link before the timeline helper, so the system's sync, purge and re-point passes and the mailbox owner never pay that helper per row (PERFORMANCE §5). Nobody else sees a link, its token, its sync state, its exclusions or its confirmed addresses. Bodies are plain text at rest like `messages.body` (**VERIFY** under N19 whether field encryption is expected; if so a later migration adds `body_enc`). **Read path:** module 68's `GET /contacts/:id/mail-messages` (`mailSync/routes.ts`, paged, RLS does the visibility); module 69 merges the entries into the thread on the client. Phase 1 search does not index synced mail in v1.

**Dedupe.** Module 22 stores `messages.rfc_message_id_sha256` on platform email, inbound and outbound, as the system: inbound ingestion on inbound, dispatch TX2 on outbound (TX1 runs as the person, and the trigger `messages_rfc_guard` lets only the system set the hash, once, so no person can plant a hash to suppress a teammate's copy or clear one; **VERIFY** whether each provider keeps the Message-ID we generate or assigns its own, which TX2 then stores from the provider response); a Gmail copy with the same hash for the same contact (or one merged into it) is refused, or deleted when the platform row lands later. The database refuses a duplicate only of platform email whose row carries the hash: rows saved before module 22 writes it, rows whose Message-ID it could not learn and imported email (never hashed) are not deduplicated, so a backfill would show such a message twice. Rollout precondition (`platform:gmail` notes): `vendor_ready_at` is set only after module 22 has hashed platform email for at least 90 days (the largest `mail_backfill_days`), or after a one-time module 22 repair hashed the recoverable rows of that window (SendGrid inbound from `provider_message_ref` without its `sendgrid:` prefix, except `sendgrid:sha256:` refs, which had no Message-ID header, and refs whose Message-ID part is 180 characters, which may have been cut; SMTP outbound from the Message-ID dispatch sent, built from the row id `messages.id` (not `messages.idempotency_key`) and the domain of the sending identity's from address; Postmark inbound and SES/Mailgun outbound only from the provider, **VERIFY**). Both paths use the handback dedupe key `email:{contact}:{rfc_message_id_sha256}` when the hash is known, so whichever lands first hands back and the other does not (E201).

**Never.** No send, label, archive or delete in Gmail; synced mail is never a `messages` row, never dispatched, never a touch, speed-to-lead or ownership event, and never sent to an AI model or used in drafts (**VERIFY** Google Limited Use); evidence cites it by id and time (`mail_message`, never quoted, CHECK `evidence_refs_mail_not_quoted`); audits carry no content; an architecture test proves no SQL names `mail_messages` or `user_mail_links` outside `apps/api/src/mailSync/**` (plus `ingestSyncedMailInTx`) and `mailSync/**` imports no model provider (R-GMAIL-14); that test, not the database, is the barrier against AI reads (one login role; a GUC set by the caller is no barrier against the code it guards, S-D2-8).

**Why not.**
- Outlook/Exchange mail: not requested by the owner (D2); calendars stay.
- Push (Gmail `watch` + Pub/Sub): not in v1; polling every `poll_interval_seconds` is simpler and needs no public push endpoint (**VERIFY** Gmail API).
- Sending through Gmail: not built. It would need a send scope and a second send path; replies go through platform email (`dispatchOutbound`) or happen in Gmail.
- "Private but kept" (hidden from the team, kept for the agent): not adopted; an excluded thread is deleted (R-GMAIL-7).

### 21.10a Security notes: module 68 `api/mail-sync` (lane 2C)
- **No new definer, no migration.** Module 68 uses the D2 schema as shipped (0025 + the 0030 review fixes): `app.mail_copy_live` and `app.mail_sync_points_guard` stay the only Gmail definers; every module 68 statement runs as `app_user` under RLS, as the caller (self routes) or as the system actor (callback, sync).
- **OAuth state** (`mailSync/oauthState.ts`). Stateless: base64url JSON {tenant, user, disclosure hash, expiry (10 min), 128-bit nonce} MAC'd with HMAC-SHA256 under a key derived from the session secret with its own purpose string (a module 21 integrations state never verifies as a Gmail state, and vice versa). The PKCE S256 verifier is derived from the nonce, tenant and user, never stored or sent to the browser. **Single use:** the callback first records `sha256(nonce)` in `webhook_events` (provider `gmail_oauth_state`, the existing replay table, through `app.record_webhook_event`), so a replayed state is refused (`STATE_REPLAYED`) whatever happened the first time. The tenant and user come only from a verified state (R-TEN-3). Residual: these replay rows are not purged by `app.purge_webhook_events` (no window for that provider): a few bytes per connect attempt; a later migration may add a window.
- **Exact scope** (E225). The authorize URL asks exactly `gmail.readonly` with PKCE S256 and never `include_granted_scopes`; the callback refuses any granted scope set other than exactly `{gmail.readonly}` (a superset, a narrower `gmail.metadata`, a calendar grant riding along), stores nothing and revokes the token at Google best effort. The `user_mail_links` scopes CHECK backs it. **VERIFY** Google's granted-scope semantics and the `scope` string of the token response.
- **Callback as the system actor.** It re-checks (before and inside the write) that `platform:gmail` is cleared, the Owner switch is on, the user is an active member and has no live link; it reads the profile (`users/me`) and links the profile's address (lower-cased) with its tenant HMAC. A mailbox live in another link in any tenant is refused by the unique index (E210, E211), and the token is revoked.
- **Tokens** (R-GMAIL-4, S-D2-7). Only `mailSync/token.ts` reads or writes `refresh_enc` (architecture test): envelope-encrypted under the tenant field key, AAD = `user_mail_links`/`refresh_enc`/link id; decrypted only for the system actor (the function refuses any other actor). Access tokens live in a local variable for one pass; nothing logs a token, an address, a subject or a body (the job log line carries counts only; a test scans rows, responses and logs for fake token strings). A person's disconnect runs in their own transaction (the S-D2-6 path: token and page token deleted, nothing else of the sync state changes); the system reads the token into memory first only to revoke it at Google after the commit, best effort.
- **Privacy.** Self routes read only the caller's own link under RLS; Owners, Admins and Team Leads read no one else's link, token, sync state, confirmations or exclusions (adversarial test). Suggestions (`GET /me/mail-sync-points`) list only the caller's own related contacts' points visible under their RLS, minus live team mailboxes (the system checks only that set: absence is all that leaks back). `POST /me/mail-threads/:threadId/exclude` answers 404 for a thread the caller's link holds no copy of (no oracle). `GET /contacts/:id/mail-messages` is the only read path; RLS decides (timeline readers plus the mailbox owner; hidden rows and support never); `mine` is computed from the caller's own link.
- **Opt-outs** (R-GMAIL-9, D1). A message from any current email point of the tenant is classified once with `classifyInbound` on its own stripped text, before and regardless of storage; the effects are `planInboundEffects` (channel email) filtered to email revokes (the `consent_events_synced_gmail` CHECK), each citing the stored copy or `gmail_message_ref`; opt-out language sets the person-level do-not-contact through `app.record_person_opt_out` exactly as platform email (which covers every other channel); a replayed message records nothing twice; START and every grant are never recorded from Gmail. Equivalence with platform email is tested for keyword, language, wrong-number, a named other channel and no opt-out.
- **Pause and handback** (R-GMAIL-10, E201). `ingestSyncedMailInTx` (comms/inbound.ts, the one flagged edit) runs only for a stored live copy from the contact whose Gmail `internalDate` is at most 1 hour old at fetch. A handback is created only when the assistant was running the thread (as platform email does); its one claim cites the copy by id and time (`evidence_refs.source_type = 'mail_message'`, no snippet) and therefore carries `needs_review` (R-AST-1: only a verified quote clears it). Dedupe key `email:{contact}:{hash}` shared with platform email.
- **Reconcile** (`mail.sync`, every 60 s per tenant). Revokes (switch off, pack uncleared, member inactive, `invalid_grant`), purges (revoked links, exclusions, removed points and their confirmations, withdrawn confirmations, live-mailbox addresses, platform duplicates, merged/deleted contacts after the re-point), then syncs due links. Provider calls run outside transactions; each page commits alone; the history cursor advances only after every page of the pass committed (a crash replays; the unique key makes it a no-op). Backfill reads `messages.list` with `after` = the window start and the cap only, never the whole mailbox; an expired history id resyncs the same bounded window.
- **Review findings** (independent workflow review): see the lane report.

### 21.11 Publishing (D2)
Posting to a Facebook Page, an Instagram business account or a GBP location goes only through `publishPost` (`marketing/publish.ts`: module 29 ships the Facebook Page and Instagram business branches; module 53 adds the `gbp_location` branch in one flagged edit after 29), and only for an approved piece of the matching kind, to a `connected`/`degraded` connection, once `platform:fair_housing` is cleared; otherwise the piece is exported. TX1: a listing writer taps, as themselves, and a `marketing_publications` row is inserted `publishing`. The provider call follows. TX2: the system records the outcome: `published` (with the provider's post id), `failed` (error code) or `unknown` (a timeout or ambiguous answer). `unknown` is reconciled by looking the post up, never by posting again (one live row per piece and target), and moves to `published` or `failed`; `published → retracted` only after the provider confirmed the deletion, by the one retraction that holds the publication's retract claim (§21.19). Only the system records outcomes. If a timed-out post cannot be found without a post id (**VERIFY**), `unknown` stays unknown and the UI says "Check your Page/Instagram". The product says "Posted" only for a provider-confirmed row (R-MKT-12, E227). A piece is `published` while its public page is live or at least one publication is `published`. A new version never touches the old version's external posts (the UI says the earlier post stays on the Page); retracting one is the system's after the provider confirmed the deletion, where the API supports it, otherwise a feed card asks the agent to remove it (R-MKT-4, R-MKT-9). Instagram (**VERIFY** each): images are fetched from a publicly reachable URL, so assets are exposed by a short-lived signed URL only; whether text-only posts exist; whether published media can be deleted through the API. The Meta connection requests content-publishing permissions only and receives no webhooks (E228). Module 53 adds the `gbp_location` branch (flagged edit): a GBP post goes only to a GBP location through `deps.gbp.publishPost`, once `platform:fair_housing` and `platform:gbp` are cleared and an adapter is configured (`GBP_NOT_CONFIGURED` otherwise, before anything is inserted); reconcile looks GBP rows up through `deps.gbp` only while `platform:gbp` is cleared; while it is uncleared Google is called only to take down a post whose listing must come down (the system's retraction). `sendReviewReply` in the same file is the only `postReviewReply` call site, imported only by `gbp/replies.ts`.

### 21.12 Security notes: batch 5 review fixes (migration `0041_batch5_review_fixes`; SECURITY.md §7.4)
- **`app.purge_contact_internal` (erasure and purge; owner `app_retention`).** Locks before it checks: every record of the person (`app.contact_merge_tree`, re-read until stable) `FOR UPDATE`, sorted by id, then the 0037 documents and do-not-contact checks, then the deletes. `FOR UPDATE` conflicts with the `FOR KEY SHARE` that any child row's foreign-key check holds on its contact, so a point (or any child) inserted by a transaction still open is waited for and seen by the checks, and one inserted later waits and fails its foreign key: the D1 backstop is no longer check-then-act. The erase route (`apps/api/src/admin/erasure.ts`) takes the same locks first in the global lock order (§10, `packages/db/src/locks.ts`): `app.lock_address` on every point of the person, sorted, then the row locks, then the carry, so an intake or accept that matched the person by an address finishes first and its new phone is carried. New in the purge: every held lead email naming an address of the person (`lead_email_messages.point_hmacs`, tenant-HMACs; live or removed points, a confirmed wrong number excepted) ends `erased` with nothing of the person left but the message key (the replay tombstone), and the lead emails linked to the person lose their sender and DKIM domains. Writes stay within `p_tenant`.
- **`app.retention_sweep` (owner `app_retention`).** 0037's steps verbatim, plus: held lead emails older than 30 days by `LEAST(p_now, now())` end `expired` (content, parse, keys, domains gone; **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL**). Using the real clock as a ceiling means a caller passing a far-future `p_now` (app_user may call the sweep) cannot expire a lead that someone could still review.
- **`app.contacts_stage_history` (stage history trigger; owner `app_definer`).** Adds `contact_stage_events.bulk_action_id`, from the transaction-local GUC `app.bulk_action_id` that the bulk runner sets around each stage change. The GUC is caller-settable, so the definer keeps it only when it names a running `stage` bulk action of `NEW.tenant_id` whose creator is the user making the change, else NULL. The value can only add a restriction: the stage automation (`stageAutomation/reconcile.ts`) reads, as the stage changer (who is that creator), whether the action's recorded audience tags or saved list are protected now (`bulk/audience.ts stageAudienceProtected`; unknown counts as protected) and then skips the plan (`protected_audience`, R-PAR-8). A forged or stale id records NULL, which is what every stage change recorded before.
- **`app.lead_email_messages_guard` (invoker).** Unchanged refusals; the only new permissions are for `current_user = 'app_retention'` (the two definers above): `needs_review → erased | expired`, and dropping the sender and DKIM domains (both at once). `point_hmacs` can only be cleared; CHECKs keep keys on held rows only and leave an erased/expired row empty.
- **`app.feed_mark_contacts_deleted` (invoker, AFTER DELETE statement trigger on `contacts`).** Marks (`public.feed_dirty`) every member with a `feed_groups` row for a deleted record, probing the primary key through `memberships`; the next materialization re-scores and drops the group. Under RLS (any future non-definer delete) it marks what the writer can see, and the hourly reconcile repairs the rest (§6.1 of SECURITY.md, 0034).
- **Deploy.** The database owner's credentials never reach the API or the worker: `docker-compose.prod.yml` interpolates them from `.env.owner` into the postgres and release services only, and both entrypoints refuse to start under `NODE_ENV=production` with `MIGRATION_DATABASE_URL`, `POSTGRES_OWNER_PASSWORD` or `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` in their environment (`apps/api/src/dbRole.ts`, DEPLOY §2-§3).

### 21.13 Security notes: batch 6 review fixes, web chat (migration `*_webchat_sessions_follow_merge`, lane number 0046)
- **`app.webchat_sessions_follow_merge()` (new SECURITY DEFINER trigger; owner `app_definer`, `search_path = pg_catalog, public`, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC; review findings optouts/F2, tenancy-public/F1, ui-compose/F2).** Fires `AFTER UPDATE OF merged_into_contact_id ON contacts` only when it goes from NULL to a contact, i.e. on the ownership engine's merge (`applyOwnershipCommand`, whoever the actor is), after 0027's `contacts_merge_children` (same event, earlier name; the migration's self-check and `packages/db/test/webchat_follow_merge.test.ts` hold the order). Inside that row's tenant it re-points the merged-away record's `webchat_sessions` (`contact_id`, `contact_point_id`) to the survivor and the survivor's copy of the session's own `wc:` identity; a session whose copy is missing stays where it was. It writes no opt-out state and moves no message (the merge moved them already). Grant: column `UPDATE (contact_id, contact_point_id)` on `webchat_sessions` to `app_definer`. A person still cannot write a session (0018 `webchat_system`); a staff user reads one only through 0018 `webchat_staff_read`, i.e. with the survivor's detail, as they read the messages the merge moved to it. The visitor's own reads are unchanged: `app.webchat_poll` matches the identity's address, which the copy keeps. The migration also moved the sessions of records merged away before it, to the root survivor of each merge chain. Proof: `apps/api/src/channels/chatMerge.test.ts` (the survivor answers the open chat, keeps the typed claim, a returning visitor is suggested as its duplicate, a reply goes to the chat the visitor is in) and the db test above (the backfill along a chain, the trigger, the shape).
- **A chat identity is not a person's point (review tenancy-public/F3).** The `GET /contacts/:id/reachability` response names the chat identity's id (`channels.web_chat.defaultPointId`), and the points routes (`people/points.ts` remove, replace, primary, label) took any kind: removing it left the open chat unanswerable and `GET /contacts/:id/details` answering 500 (its `removedPoints` are phones and emails). The routes now answer 404 `NOT_FOUND` for a `web_chat` point, exactly as for an unknown one, and the Details list only phones and emails (`repos/people.ts personPoint`). Proof: `channels/webchat.test.ts` (module 57 review tenancy-public/F3).

### 21.14 Security notes: module 26 `api/nurture` (wave 2A)
Trigger playbooks end to end (R-NURT-1…5, R-NURT-7, R-NURT-8, R-FH-2, R-AST-20, R-MKT-11). **No migration, no new SECURITY DEFINER function, policy or grant**: the module uses 0018's objects as they are (`playbook_step_runs` trigger shape and unique `trigger_key`, policy `step_runs_trigger`, the 0021 dispatch guards).
- **Writers.** Firings (`playbook_step_runs` with no enrollment) are written only by the system actor (policy `step_runs_trigger` WITH CHECK `is_tenant_wide()`; a person's insert is refused, tested). Detection runs as the system in the `nurture.listing_changed` job (the MLS sync's write transaction and the person's `updateListing` transaction only enqueue it, so no contact lock is held across a sync page) and in the hourly `nurture.anniversary_scan`; each takes all its contact locks once, in id order. Every statement is tenant-scoped; the `trigger.step` and reconcile jobs run in their job's tenant only (cross-tenant execution is a no-op, tested).
- **Autonomy.** `executeTriggerRun` runs under the contact lock and re-checks the contact (live record: merged-away or deleted ones never get anything), the approval (only an `approved` version whose latest approval binds its hash), the owner (an agent-scoped playbook only while its agent owns the contact; unowned ⇒ nothing), the fact (core `recheckFact`) and the pause. Anything not plainly sendable (paused, fair-housing match on the composed text, any core `authorizeAutonomousAction` downgrade) becomes a `message_draft` for the owner; wiring details are skipped. Revoking a version skips its scheduled firings and cancels their queued messages; superseding moves unbuilt firings to the new version (system job).
- **One outbound path.** Messages are queued with `queueOutboundInTx` and sent only by `comms/dispatch.ts`. Flagged edit (module 22's file): `playbookReasons` accepts a step run with no enrollment only while that run is still `scheduled`, the contact is owned by the owner and ownership period the message was built for (`ASSISTANT_ACTION_STALE` otherwise), and only as its step of the approved trigger content (`storedTriggerStep` + `triggerStepAsPlaybookStep`, the step the validator saw); the enrollment branch is unchanged. The DB guard still refuses an autonomous send while paused or not approved (tested with a forced UPDATE), and D1 opt-outs, R-PAR-12 removed-phone STOPs, person-level do-not-contact (merges included) and suppressions are the gate's and guard's, never re-implemented here (tested).
- **One step shape per message.** A digest holds only items whose step has the lead's channel, purpose and category (the ones dispatch re-checks); other items are built as their own message.
- **Content.** MLS data reaches the text only through `mls/eligibility.ts clientView` (display rules incl. a withheld price, brokerage, attribution, disclaimer; no MLS number in client lines); template markers and line breaks are stripped from data we did not write. Trigger sends are never touches (the assistant initiates them).
- **Residual.** A queued trigger message is not re-checked for MLS staleness between queueing and dispatch (seconds; the saved-search sweep covers only alerts it queued). **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL**: review-request wording (C8, `platform:review_requests` ships unreviewed, so the step is skipped until cleared); whether price-drop alerts are marketing (treated as marketing, stricter).
- **Home values (wave 2C: `avm.refresh`, the `home_value` firing; R-AVM-1…3, R-INT-1, R-INT-6, P15, F7).** Still **no migration, no new SECURITY DEFINER function, policy or grant**.
  - *Writers.* Only the system writes `home_valuations` and home-value firings: `avm.refresh` is a per-tenant daily **standalone** job (like `mls.sync`). TX1 (system) picks due (address, UTC month) pairs: a complete address of a live, owned contact that is a home they bought through the team (batch 8 review K2, §21.25), covered by a current approved trigger playbook with a `home_value` step (core `detectFirings`), nothing recorded yet this UTC month, and a send-time preview (comms `evaluateGate`, never stored) without `CONSENT_REVOKED`, `SUPPRESSED`, `PERSON_DO_NOT_CONTACT`, `CONTACT_POINT_INVALID` or `TENANT_SUSPENDED`. The vendor is called **outside any transaction** (no contact lock is held while it answers; tested against `pg_locks`). TX2 takes the contact lock, re-checks the address (unchanged, still the contact's) and the month (TX2's `now()`), inserts one row (`valued_at = now()`, never the vendor's as-of) and records the fact through `recordFactInTx`. A vendor failure or an answer the parser refuses writes nothing (no `error` rows) and a later pass retries. The vendor is platform configuration (no `integration_connections` or `provider_credentials` row); with none configured nothing is requested. The parser keeps display permission only for a literal `true` and passes vendor strings through `literal()` (template markers, line breaks, control and bidi characters removed); no confidence or score field exists (R-AST-13). No definer is used: `app.platform_cleared` (0018, already noted) is read, never written; `jurisdiction_packs` is never written (a new unreviewed `platform:avm` version turns notes back into drafts, R-INT-6).
  - *Sending.* `executeTriggerRun` re-reads the cited valuation under the contact lock: another contact's, address's or tenant's row, or a key that is not the ref's, is `FACT_MISMATCH`; a deleted or edited address is `ADDRESS_NOT_CURRENT`. Core `avmSendable` decides with the tenant's `avm_max_range_bps`, `DEFAULT_AVM_JUMP_BPS`, the pack's `max_age_days` (35; core's 45 only when absent) and `app.platform_cleared('platform:avm')`: uncleared, too wide or a large jump (E98) ⇒ a draft for the owner; no value (E99), not displayable or stale ⇒ a visible skip. The R-AVM-1 sentence (core `homeValueText`) is added after rendering and before the content guard and `authorizeAutonomousAction`, and the claim cites the `home_valuations` row (`evidence_refs.source_type 'home_valuation'`). One sender (F7): `stepCapabilities` never grants `avm`, so a v1 post-closing enrollment's home-value step stays skipped.
  - *Source (owner decision D9, 2026-11-12; PHASE2_3_PLAN §2).* Home values will come from the team's own MLS-based valuation (comparable sales), never a third-party AVM. `AvmProvider` is source-neutral: an estimate with a low/high range, an as-of date, the method, comparable sales as evidence, the source name clients see and its display permission; no vendor fields, ids or terms (`provider_ref` is stored as NULL). The comparables are not persisted (no column; a schema change if wanted). A later lane builds the in-house provider; the fake stays the default.
  - *Residual.* `platform:avm` is checked when the message is built, not again at dispatch (a pack un-cleared between build and dispatch, normally seconds, does not stop an already queued note; D1 opt-outs are still checked at dispatch). `home_valuations` writes are system-only since the batch 8 review (§21.25, db-public/F3); reads stay under 0001's `contact_detail` policy. Owner/Admin can write trigger runs under `step_runs_trigger` (`is_tenant_wide()`); `executeTriggerRun`'s recheck is the defence (a forged home-value run is `FACT_MISMATCH`, tested). Two passes running at once in one tenant may each call the vendor for the same pair (the periodic job is one live row per tenant, so the worker never does this; rows and firings stay one per address and month either way, tested). **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL (C6)**: the sentence, the template wording, state rules on valuations by non-appraisers; (C1) home-value notes under D1 against state mini-TCPA laws. **VERIFY against the vendor contract (N8)**: display permission, the provider name and attribution, caching and retention (R-DATA-6: rows are purged 13 months after `valued_at` by `app.purge_home_valuations()`, and their citations keep no copy of the estimate, §21.33 and §21.36), whether "no value" is distinct from a failure, request limits.

### 21.15 Security notes: module 29 `api/marketing` (migration `0050_marketing_public_page`; lane number 0052)
Proof: `apps/api/src/marketing/{marketing,publish,page,invites,adversarial,architecture,decide.property}.test.ts`.
- **`app.public_listing_page(text, text) → (state, notice, tenant_id, piece_id, version_no, prose, template_key, bound_fields, listing facts, identity block, operating_jurisdictions, asset_object_ids)`** (security-review item). Pre-tenant `SECURITY DEFINER`, owner `app_definer`, `search_path = pg_catalog, public`, `STABLE`, `EXECUTE` revoked from `PUBLIC` and granted to `app_user` only; writes nothing. Input is the tenant slug and the page slug, both shape-checked in SQL (and in the API before any query). It answers only for the `website_page` piece that holds that slug, in an active tenant, on a TEAM listing: `live` only while the piece is `published`, the listing is not sold/withdrawn/expired and `app.platform_cleared('platform:fair_housing')` holds (R-INT-6: a later unreviewed pack takes every page down); `gone` (410) for a retracted page or an off-market listing, with only the notice and every other column NULL (no tenant id, no prose); no row (404) for everything else (unknown pair, draft/approved/superseded versions, MLS listings). It returns the approved prose, the bound facts columns, the brokerage identity, the jurisdictions and the asset ids read through `marketing_piece_assets` joined to live `marketing_asset` objects only: never the listing agent, a contact, a contact point, another piece or a private listing attribute. The migration grants `app_definer` SELECT on the seven tables it reads. The API renders the page with core `renderPiece` and re-runs the guard on the live rendering (R-MKT-4: a page that no longer passes is not served and the reconcile handler retracts it within the hour). Images are served only after the definer named the object as an asset of the live page, as the system actor of the tenant the definer returned (`objects_marketing_assets`), never by an id the request chose alone. Abuse: DB-backed rate limits per IP (120/min, 2,000/h) and per page (1,200/min), HTML-escaped text, CSP `default-src 'none'; img-src 'self'; style-src <hash>` (no script), one-minute public caching.
- **Signed asset links** (`marketing/assetLinks.ts`): Instagram fetches images from a public URL (**VERIFY**), so publishing hands the provider `GET /api/public/marketing-assets/:token` links: 16-byte tenant id, 16-byte object id and a 4-byte expiry (1 h), MAC'd with HMAC-SHA256 (128-bit tag) under a key derived per purpose from the server secret. A bad MAC, an expired link, or an object that is no longer an asset of an approved/published/exported piece of that tenant is 404. The link names nothing else and is rate-limited per IP.
- **The publishing state machine** (`marketing/publish.ts`, R-MKT-12, R-GATE-15): TX1 runs as the tapping person under FORCE RLS and inserts `marketing_publications` 'publishing' (the 0025 guard is the authority: a person as themselves, an approved social post, a connected/degraded `facebook_page`/`instagram_business`, `platform:fair_housing` cleared, one live row per piece and account); the token is decrypted as the system in memory only; `SocialPublisher.publishPost` runs outside any transaction; TX2 runs as the system and records `published` only with the provider's post id, `failed` only for an explicit rejection, and `unknown` for a timeout or an unreadable answer. `unknown` (and a 'publishing' row older than 5 minutes) is reconciled only by `lookupPost` (by piece id and content hash), never by posting again; a provider that cannot look up a post without its id (**VERIFY**) leaves it `unknown`, and the UI says "Check your Page/Instagram". Retraction claims the publication first (one short system transaction; §21.19), calls `deletePost` outside any transaction and records `retracted` only after the provider confirmed; otherwise the listing agent gets a "remove it yourself" card (asked once per post). TX2 of a confirmed post locks the piece before the publication row, the order TX1 takes them (§21.19). The UI says "Posted" only for a provider-confirmed row (`posted = status published ∧ post id`). Lint reserves `publishPost`/`postReviewReply` for `marketing/publish.ts`; the architecture test proves no other file (tests included) calls `publishPost`, `lookupPost`, `deletePost` or `postReviewReply`, and that only `publish.ts` writes `marketing_publications`. The Meta connection requests publishing scopes only and no messaging webhook exists (E228).
- **Trust decisions.** Approval binds to the content the person saw (`contentSha256` must equal the DB's own `app.marketing_content_sha256`; the guard runs once more against the live facts right before the approval UPDATE, and the 0018 CHECK is the backstop). Invites go only through module 22's `composeHumanMessage → dispatchOutbound` (canContact and the DB dispatch guard per recipient; D1 opt-outs with no override; `MARKETING_PIECE_NOT_APPROVED` in the guard); a recipient the sender cannot see counts as "no email" and gets nothing. Feed cards (`marketing:{piece}`) are written by the system for the listing agent, because a Team Lead or Owner acting on an agent's listing cannot write the agent's feed under RLS. Flyers render locally with pdf-lib (N16; no network, fixed dates). No new SECURITY DEFINER besides `app.public_listing_page`.

### 21.16 Security notes: batch 7 review fixes, merges, sends and listings (migration `*_b7_review_sends_merges`, lane number 0053)
Proof: `apps/api/src/marketing/b7ReviewSends.test.ts`, `apps/api/src/nurture/b7ReviewMerges.test.ts`, `apps/api/src/sellerUpdates/b7ReviewListings.test.ts`, `apps/api/src/workspace/listingAgent.test.ts`, `apps/api/src/insights/b7ReviewPlan.test.ts`.
- **Replaced definers** (security-review item): `app.scoreboard_activity`, `app.scoreboard_rollup_roll`, `app.scoreboard_mark_messages` (each from its newest body, 0035/0047/0035) and `app.messages_dispatch_guard` (0043 body). Same signatures, trigger bindings and grants; owner `app_definer`, `search_path = pg_catalog, public`, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC, re-asserted and self-checked (every earlier rule's marker string is still in the body). The scoreboard ones only count fewer rows: an open-house invite (`marketing_piece_id`) is never a touch, like a bulk send (sends/F1, R-OWN-14, R-MKT-8). The guard only refuses more: a `within_playbook` message reaches `dispatching` only while its assistant action is `proposed` (`ASSISTANT_ACTION_WITHDRAWN`, sends/F2); every opt-out line (whole person, removed phones except wrong numbers, suppressions, the invite-version check) is verbatim.
- **`app.contacts_merge_roles()`** (new `SECURITY DEFINER` trigger function on `contacts.merged_into_contact_id`, owner `app_definer`, pinned `search_path`, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC; ownership-data/F1, R-OWN-24). It acts only for a row the ownership engine's merge just marked merged-away (it checks that the current ownership command appended that row's `merged_away` event: app_user may UPDATE `merged_into_contact_id` and `contacts_guard` does not cover the column, so a bare UPDATE moves nothing here), only in that row's tenant, only on rows naming the merged-away record, and only towards the survivor the merge chose: it moves `listing_contacts` and `deal_contacts` rows (deduped on their primary keys) and `deals.primary_contact_id`, and rewrites the reasons of an open `seller_update` card of a listing whose seller moved (no contact name in it). It moves no opt-out state and sends nothing; who may read a role or a deal is decided by the unchanged policies. Least-privilege grants to `app_definer`: INSERT, DELETE on `listing_contacts` and `deal_contacts`, UPDATE (`role`) on `listing_contacts` (the stronger-role dedupe); UPDATE (`primary_contact_id`) on `deals`; UPDATE (`reasons`, `updated_at`) on `priority_feed_items`.
- **`PATCH /listings/:id/agent`** (`listing.reassign`, Owner/Admin; ownership-data/F2): runs as the caller under FORCE RLS; the target must be an active member who may own leads (`assertAssignable`), MLS rows are 404, audited `listing.agent_changed` with the before/after user ids. Re-auth is not required (an orchestrator choice for this fix: it moves no lead, no money and no opt-out). The "no active listing agent" card (`workspace/listingAgent.ts`) is written by the system to active Owners/Admins only and names the listing's address, which every Owner/Admin may read.
- **Trust decisions.** The weekly plan judges do-not-contact over the whole person (`app.contact_person_ids`, under the caller's RLS like `personDoNotContact`) and drops merged-away records (ownership-data/F5). A merged-away invite recipient is reported with its survivor only when the caller's RLS shows the survivor (`visibleSurvivorOf`, R-PERM-5; sends/F5). No send path gains a bypass: every message still goes through `comms/dispatch.ts`, its gate and the DB guard (D1).

### 21.17 Security notes: batch 7 review fixes, members, calendar, Gmail and deploy
No migration and no new SECURITY DEFINER. Proof: `apps/api/src/calendar/calendar.test.ts`, `apps/api/src/mailSync/{edges,architecture}.test.ts`, `apps/api/src/capabilityPaths.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/security-headers.nginx.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/surfaces/settings/mail/MailSettingsPage.test.tsx`.
- **`app.calendar_links_revoke_on_member_removed()` (0049, module 34; security-review item, recorded here after the fact).** `SECURITY DEFINER` trigger function, owner `app_definer`, `search_path = pg_catalog, public`, `EXECUTE` revoked from `PUBLIC`. It runs only as an `AFTER UPDATE OF status` trigger on `memberships` when the status becomes `removed`, takes no argument and updates only `NEW.tenant_id`/`NEW.user_id`'s links, and only their `status`, `revoked_at`, `purge_after` and `last_error_code` (`MEMBER_REMOVED`). It must be a definer because `user_calendar_links` is self-and-system only (0018 `calendar_links_self`, R-CAL-1): the remover (an Owner/Admin, or the system) can neither see nor write the member's link under RLS. 0049 grants `app_definer` column `UPDATE (status, revoked_at, purge_after, last_error_code)` on `user_calendar_links` (it had `SELECT` only): no token column is writable by the definer. It deliberately leaves the encrypted token on the revoked row, so the system actor can revoke the grant at the provider first; the hourly `calendar.reconcile` then revokes it (best effort) and purges the token, and purges any revoked link that still holds one. A revoked link is never used (every reader requires `connected`/`degraded`).
- **Correction to 0025's rationale.** 0025's "a revoked link holds no token (CHECK)" is about `user_mail_links`, which has that CHECK. `user_calendar_links` has no such CHECK, and since 0049 a revoked calendar link holds its encrypted token until `calendar.reconcile` purges it (normally within the hour). `app.retention_sweep` does not cover `user_calendar_links`, so the R-INT-5 30-day token purge for calendar links rests on `calendar.reconcile` alone: if that job stops, revoked-but-unpurged tokens stay (unusable, encrypted) until it runs again.
- **Suspension is not removal (R-INT-5, R-GMAIL-13).** Gmail sync now revokes a link only when the membership is `removed`; a `suspended`, `deactivating` or `invited` member's link is kept with its token and skipped by the sync pass, and `app.mail_link_live` hides its copies (reversible) until the member is active again. This matches the calendar. Trade-off: a suspended member's encrypted Gmail refresh token stays stored while they are suspended (it was deleted before); the copies stay unreadable to everyone, and removal revokes and deletes as before. `calendar.reconcile` also enqueues every change an active member's connected calendar has not accepted (pushes that paused while they were suspended); degraded links are left to their own recovery.
- **A fresh Gmail reply moves the conversation's inbound counter** (`ingestSyncedMailInTx` calls `bumpInboundSeq`: `inbound_seq` only). That holds a human message scheduled before the reply for a re-tap (E27) and makes an older draft stale (R-AST-6); it only adds holds. It deliberately does NOT move `last_inbound_at` (conversation or contact): R-CONSENT-5 classifies a free-form message as informational within 30 days of an inbound, and an email unsubscribe is a marketing-purpose revoke, so a synced reply would otherwise let free-form email past that unsubscribe (D1). It writes no `messages` row and no touch (R-GMAIL-14, D8). A reply synced by two links (or by Gmail and platform email) can move the counter twice: one extra re-tap at worst.
- **nginx.** The `$log_uri` map redacts `/api/public/marketing-assets/<token>` (in `capabilityPaths.json` since the route exists). `location /p/` proxies module 29's listing pages to the API with the same re-resolved upstream and `X-Forwarded-For $remote_addr` as `/api/`, and adds no headers (the page's own CSP applies). The routes outside `/api` are pinned in `capabilityPaths.ts` (`DOCUMENT_ROUTES`, public slugs only).

### 21.18 Security notes: batch 7 review fixes, web UI and copy (no migration)
- **No migration, no new definer, policy or grant.** Every fix is web copy and state, or API logic that runs as before (`app_user` under FORCE RLS).
- **Gmail connect disclosure** (`apps/web/src/surfaces/settings/mail/disclosure.json`, hash-bound): one sentence added, "Replies you send from Gmail don't count as first contact. Reply here to stop the clock." (R-GMAIL-14, D8). `ACCEPTED_DISCLOSURE_SHA256` now lists only the new hash: module 69 is dark behind `platform:gmail` and no deployed build showed the old text to a real team, so a connect that carries the old hash is refused (`MAIL_DISCLOSURE_STALE`). Trust decision: the agent learns before connecting that Gmail replies never count as first contact. Still **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL** (C14).
- **Coaching** (R-COACH-3, ui/F5): a TC's calls are never reviewed and a TC never gets a daily tip. `core.REVIEWED_ROLES` equals the roles `can()` gives `coaching.view` (a core test proves it); `callReviewFacts` reads the agent's membership role, the scan and `reviewedMembers` filter on it. The `call_scores_guard` (0052) is unchanged, so the role rule is enforced by the job and the reconcile, not by the database; a review stored before this fix stays readable to whoever could read it, but it produces no tip.
- **Calendar** (ui/F3): `pending_review` is reported as `pending` (not live for busy times, not connectable again, disconnectable). With the fake adapters the API's connected sentence and the page say it is a test calendar. No change to token handling or scopes.
- **Web-chat embed code** (ui/F4): the copied snippet carries only the widget key. Nothing secret changed: the key was already in the snippet, and the disclosure text and brokerage name are public through `GET /public/chat/:key`.

### 21.19 Security notes: marketing retract claim (migration `*_marketing_retract_claim`, lane number 0054)
Proof: `apps/api/src/marketing/publish.test.ts` ("races, held deterministically": an injected provider hook holds the first retraction after the provider deleted the post; a held table lock holds a second tap's TX1 between its piece lock and its INSERT; a hook on the transaction builder commits an 'unknown' post's confirmation between a retraction's first and last transactions; an injected provider hook that never answers).
- **No new SECURITY DEFINER function, policy or table grant.** `marketing_publications` gains `retract_claim_id` and `retract_claimed_at`, covered by 0025's table grants and the unchanged system-only UPDATE policy `publications_outcome`. CHECKs: both or neither; only on a `published` row with a post id (a retracted row holds no claim).
- **`app.marketing_publications_guard()` (replaced from its only definition, 0025; still SECURITY INVOKER, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC, every 0025 refusal self-checked).** It only refuses more: a publication is never born with a claim (`PUBLICATION_BORN_PUBLISHING`); a person never writes one (the claim columns join the `PUBLICATION_SYSTEM_ONLY` tuple; RLS already refuses a person's UPDATE); a claim goes only on a provider-confirmed post (`PUBLICATION_RETRACT_CLAIM`); a live claim is never replaced until it is older than the window (`PUBLICATION_RETRACT_CLAIMED`); the claim time is stamped with the database's `clock_timestamp()` when the claim is written (not the start of a transaction that first waited for the row) and never moved without a new claim, so no caller can backdate a claim to steal it or postdate one to keep it; a takeover is judged against `now()`, the transaction's start, so conservatively.
- **`app.marketing_retract_claim_window()`** (new, SECURITY INVOKER, `IMMUTABLE`, `search_path = pg_catalog`, reads nothing; EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC, granted to `app_user`): `5 minutes`, the one window for the guard, `publish.ts` and the reconcile pass. A live caller never holds a claim that long: `publish.ts` waits at most `DELETE_POST_TIMEOUT_MS` (60 s) for `deletePost` and then counts the call as failed (the card), and the `SocialPublisher.deletePost` contract requires the adapter's own request timeout (30 s), so a stale claim is taken over only from a process that died.
- **Why** (R-MKT-12, R-GATE-15): two retractions of one post both called `deletePost`; the second got `POST_NOT_FOUND` and, when it finished before the first recorded `retracted`, raised a "remove it yourself" card for a post that was gone. Now `retractPublication` runs TX1 (as the system: the row `FOR UPDATE`, then the claim), the provider call outside any transaction, and TX2 (`retracted` clears the claim; the card is raised only by the claim's holder, which then releases it so a later request asks the provider again). A second caller answers `in_progress` and never calls the provider: the single-post route answers 409 `PUBLICATION_RETRACTING`, take-down-all counts it as `inProgress` (never "nothing to take down"), and the reconcile pass leaves the post to the holder (`inProgress` in its summary). A claim older than the window is a stored request to retract whose process died: the reconcile pass finishes it whatever the listing (a person's tap on a listing still for sale included), even if an earlier attempt raised the card. An `unknown` row has no post id and takes no claim; its last transaction decides under the row lock: if the row was confirmed meanwhile (reconcile's lookup or a late answer) and another retraction claimed it, that one decides (`in_progress`); confirmed and unclaimed, it is retracted through the claim instead of handed to the agent. A confirmed deletion takes that post's reason off any open "remove it yourself" card (closing a card left with none), so a card raised by an earlier attempt or by a retraction that took over a stale claim never outlives the post.
- **Residual.** A claim whose process died is taken again after the window, so a post can stay up to one window plus one reconcile interval (5 + 5 minutes) after the retraction was asked for. The guard does not stop a system write from releasing a live claim and claiming again in two statements (the database does not know which caller holds a claim); only `publish.ts` writes `marketing_publications` (architecture test), and it releases only the claim it holds. If the dead process had already deleted the post, the new holder gets `POST_NOT_FOUND` and raises the "remove it yourself" card: the post is gone and the card is a false alarm the agent can dismiss (conservative: we never record `retracted` without the provider's confirmation).
- **Publishing lock order (no schema change).** TX2 of a provider-confirmed post now locks the piece (`FOR NO KEY UPDATE`: it still waits for TX1's `FOR UPDATE`, but not for the key-share locks of rows that reference the piece) before it writes the publication row, the order TX1 takes them (the piece `FOR UPDATE`, then the INSERT into `marketing_publications_one_live`). Before, a second tap's TX1 holding the piece waited in that INSERT for TX2's uncommitted row while TX2 waited for the piece: a deadlock, resolved by aborting either TX2 (retried, after a 1 s stall) or the tap's TX1 (answered 500 instead of 409 `PUBLICATION_ALREADY_LIVE`). A row TX2 only locks does not make the INSERT wait, so the tap is refused at once. Nothing was ever posted twice (the one-live index held).
### 21.20 Security notes: outbound photo and video texts (D5; migration `0055_outbound_mms.sql`)
- **`app.outbound_media_upload_shred`** (AFTER DELETE on `outbound_media_uploads`; definer, owner app_retention, pinned search_path, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC).
  - Keyed only on OLD. It shreds the deleted upload's own object only when that object has purpose `mms`, was created by that uploader, is not deleted and is attached to no message.
  - It is reached by removal, the purge job, and erasure (the contact cascade, both in `app.purge_contact_internal` and in `app.retention_sweep`).
  - No role can UPDATE the table, so OLD.object_id is the object the stamp trigger checked. A row a superuser forges (another user's object, a recording's object) shreds nothing (`packages/db/test/outbound_mms.test.ts`).
- **`app.outbound_media_uploads_stamp`** (invoker, BEFORE INSERT). It sets the times itself, binds the row to an unshredded `mms` object of the uploader with the same type and size, and returns early for a foreign tenant so that RLS refuses the row.
- **`app.message_media_outbound_guard`** (invoker, BEFORE INSERT, every inserter).
  - Inbound parents pass.
  - An outbound parent must be the caller's own queued or scheduled human `mms`, with no automation ids (the parent row is locked FOR UPDATE).
  - The object must be the caller's unexpired upload for that person.
  - At most 10 items and 5,000,000 bytes with the text.
  - It returns early for a foreign tenant.
- **`messages_mms_channel_fixed`** (invoker, BEFORE UPDATE OF channel): no message moves into or out of `mms`.
- **`messages_mms_has_media`** (invoker, deferred constraint trigger): an outbound `mms` has media at commit.
- **GET /api/mms/media** (public, global limiter off): MAC first, then 12 fetches per item per hour, then the message state and the object. One 404 for every refusal, byte-exact answers, nosniff, attachment, no-store and a sandbox CSP. No audit row. No Zod query schema, so a missing, repeated or extra parameter is the same 404, never a 422 (`apps/api/src/comms/outboundMedia.fetch.test.ts`).
- **The token**: base64url(tenant ‖ message ‖ position ‖ expiry) and a 32-character linkMac (purpose `mms-media`, the session secret). It is stateless; rotating SESSION_SECRET_BASE64 breaks the links in flight. It lives at most 10 h 20 min, in a query string that the API never logs.
### 21.21 Security notes: texting reactions (D5b)
No definer. `message_reactions` is staff Layer 1 with FORCE RLS. It is readable through the message (`EXISTS` on `messages`, as `media_select`), only the system inserts into it, and it has no UPDATE or DELETE grant, so rows go only by cascade with either message. The invoker trigger `app.message_reactions_guard` refuses any write by anyone but the system, a NULL actor included. It also refuses a link to anything but a non-imported outbound text on the same contact, sent from the number the reaction came in on to the reaction's sender in the 30 days before. The inbound transaction writes the row and its audit inside a savepoint. A refusal is audited and the text is classified as before; a deadlock or serialization failure retries the whole ingestion. A crafted reaction-shaped text cannot hide an opt-out: the parse is anchored, the quote must equal a stored team text, and an unlinked text is classified exactly as before. The most it can do is have the team's own words not read as an opt-out, which is D5(b), audited and shown to the agent.
### 21.22 Security notes: module 47 `core/voice` (lane voice-core)
- **Pure core.** `packages/core/src/voice/**` adds no migration, no SECURITY DEFINER function, no policy, no grant and no route. It decides; module 48 (`api/voice`) carries the decisions out and the DB stays authoritative: `calls_assistant_inbound_only` (no AI outbound, R-GATE-9), `app.calls_voice_guard` (approved `voice_answering` version, the Owner's attested setting, `platform:voice` cleared with `rules.vendor_ready_at`), `calls_assistant_disclosed` and `app.voice_tool_calls_guard` (no tool request before both the disclosure and the recording announcement), the args CHECK and the append-only ledger. `shouldAnswer` refuses whenever the facts say any of these fail, so core never asks the DB for a row it would refuse. Voice stays dark (D7): `platform:voice` ships unreviewed.
- **Mirrored closed sets.** `VOICE_LEDGER_TOOLS`, `VOICE_OUTCOMES`, `TRANSFER_OUTCOMES`, `APPOINTMENT_KINDS` and `VOICE_ANSWER_WHEN` equal the catalog's CHECKs, and every reason code matches `voice_tool_calls.reason_code`; `packages/db/test/voice_core_equivalence.test.ts` reads them from a freshly migrated catalog. Core's argument check (`toolArgsValid`) is identical to or stricter than `app.voice_tool_args_ok` (proved on generated vectors against the real function): plain objects of string values, allowlisted keys only, real ISO instants with an offset, lowercase uuids, and closed code sets for `take_message.topic` and `end_call.reason_code` (the DB allows any code; a "code" could otherwise spell a name or a number into a ledger that outlives the call). `ledgerProjection` keeps only allowlisted valid keys and drops a transfer target that is not the facts' owner, so no foreign id reaches the ledger.
- **Content-to-ledger map (known gap (a)).** `core/assistant` `VOICE_TOOLS` names `share_listing_facts`, `book_appointment`, `take_message`, `transfer_to_owner`; `CONTENT_TOOL_LEDGER_MAP` maps them onto the DB set (`book_appointment` ⇒ `check_availability` + `book_appointment`, `take_message` ⇒ `take_message`, `transfer_to_owner` ⇒ `warm_transfer`, `share_listing_facts` ⇒ none). `record_opt_out` and `end_call` are always available once both disclosures played. Renaming `VOICE_TOOLS` is the orchestrator's decision.
- **Opt-outs are deterministic (R-CONSENT-8, D1).** A spoken opt-out is recognised only by `classifyInbound(turn, keywords, 'voice')`, unchanged; the machine emits `apply_inbound` for module 48 to run `planInboundEffects`. `record_opt_out` is allowed only after that classifier heard one, its logged channel is core's (`voice` for a keyword, `all` for person scope), never the model's argument, and a model request without a match is denied and flagged `POSSIBLE_OPT_OUT` (needs review) in the handback. The model's own words are never classified. A wrong-number or HELP utterance on a live inbound call is not treated as an opt-out (it would revoke the caller's own number on a question like "do I have the wrong number?"). An opt-out never stops the answer (E112); it withdraws the phone callback (voice opted out on any phone, removed ones that still count included, R-PAR-12; a voice suppression; person-level do-not-contact) and limits a confirmation promise to a channel reachability allows. Core never sends: module 48 sends through `dispatchOutbound` (R-GATE-14).
- **Words never choose a target, a slot or an effect.** The caller's words reach a decision only through `callerTurnSignals` (the classifier, the money/contract and voice scope lexicons, `detectAreaQuestion`, `detectHumanQuestion`, the covered-language fact); the model's free text reaches only `voiceTurnGuard` (whether that turn is spoken). A transfer goes only to `transferTarget`'s owner (active, accepting leads, in routing hours, hot by `isHot` over usable claims); a booking only to a slot the facts offered from the owner's free/busy; neither the caller's nor the model's words can name a number, a member or a time. Property tests prove both reachability claims and that no output carries an id absent from the call's facts. `isHot` cannot read a prediction band, a call score or a contact attribute, and an architecture test proves core/voice imports nothing from core/predict or core/coaching and only types from core/ownership (it never changes ownership).
- **Residual risks.** (1) In-call claims come from module 48's normalisation of the transcript; a model can make a caller "hot" only by quoting the caller's verified words, and hotness only permits a transfer to the contact's own owner. (2) Caller speech during the disclosure or the announcement is not processed (R-VOICE-2), so an opt-out said over the disclosure must be repeated; module 48 keeps the transcript. (3) A booking result that arrives after the hang-up is not counted as booked; the slot is in the handback as `UNCONFIRMED_SLOT` and module 48 must reconcile the appointment. (4) The STT may not report a language (E119); with none reported the call goes on. (5) The ledger cannot hold an unknown tool (`ledgerProjection` is null); where that request is recorded is module 48's question (gap (f)).
- **VERIFY.** WITH COUNSEL (C2): the disclosure wording and timing, that an AI call always announces recording (whether live AI transcription is recording or interception in all-party states), the scripted truthful answer and the FCC treatment of AI voices, the decline and uncovered-language lines (`VOICE_LINES`, exported data); whether a bare spoken "stop" on a live call is an opt-out. WITH COUNSEL (C3): `AREA_QUESTION_CLIENT_REPLY` and the `fh/1` lexicon. Counsel may review the legal and lending lexicon (`VOICE_SCOPE_LEXICON`, C2/C7). Vendor (N12, all inputs): `max_turn_latency_ms` (seeded 1500), whether STT reports the language, whether the vendor limits tool calls (core denies regardless), silence/barge-in/hang-up events and timing (`silenceSeconds`, `playbackTimeoutSeconds` default 8 s and 60 s), and that the synthetic voice is never a real person's. Pack value: `voice_transfer_hold_seconds` (default 20 s, gap (b)). Owner/product: the `HotRule` criteria (none ship, gap (c)).
### 21.23 Security notes: module 50 `api/predict` (migration `0057_predict_retention`)
- **`app.purge_prediction_data()` (retention; owner `app_retention`).** `SECURITY DEFINER` with `search_path` pinned to `pg_catalog, public`; `EXECUTE` revoked from `PUBLIC` and granted to `app_user` only. It refuses (`PREDICTION_PURGE_SYSTEM_ONLY`) unless `app.tenant_id()` is set and `app.actor_kind()` is `system`, so a user, a support session (even under a grant) or a pre-tenant transaction cannot call it. It takes no argument: the instant is the database clock (`now()`), never the caller's (the 0016 lesson; unlike `app.purge_webchat_sessions` it has no `p_now`). Because `app_retention` is `BYPASSRLS`, both deletes filter `tenant_id = app.tenant_id()`: `property_records` whose `retention_until <= now()` (fetched + `recordRetentionDays`, at most 12 months, R-DATA-6, **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL** and with the vendor licence) and `contact_predictions` whose `computed_on` is more than 90 days before the tenant's local date ("latest + 90 d"; the nightly pass re-stamps every row it evaluates). It returns two counts. The migration's self-check proves the owner, the pinned path, no `PUBLIC` execute, `app_user` execute, no instant parameter, and that `app_user` still has no `DELETE` on either table. Proof: `apps/api/src/predict/pass.test.ts` (R-DATA-6: only its own tenant's expired rows; a user actor and a missing tenant context are refused; a call with an instant does not exist) and `adversarial.test.ts` (every user role and support refused). Erasure is unchanged: both tables cascade from the contact (R-DATA-7).
- **Trust decisions.** Public records are platform configuration, never an `integration_connections` row (R-INT-1). The adapter receives one postal address and nothing else (no name, contact id or tenant); `parsePublicRecord` rebuilds the answer field by field so no person-level datum is kept (R-PRED-2). Owner names on title and the contact's names are compared in memory by core `attachPropertyRecord` and never stored, hashed or logged. A record that does not attach, and a "not available" answer, are stored with `owner_name_match = false`, every fact and `provider_ref` NULL. Production without `ALLOW_FAKE_PROVIDERS` uses `UnconfiguredPublicRecords`, which looks nothing up. Model rules live in frozen definitions in `predict/models.ts` (empty); a version scores only while its `prediction_models` row is approved, its definition exists and the features are equal; no app code writes `prediction_models`. Cards: one `priority_feed_items` row per contact and model, for the current owner only, under `likely:{contact}:{model}`; withdrawal hides (RLS, feed.ts) and never writes; a person-level do-not-contact hides the card in the feed read at once (`workspace/feed.ts`, R-PRED-6). `predict/architecture.test.ts` proves the module reaches no message, routing, ownership, ads or portal code.
- **Residuals.** (1) The lookup budget counts rows fetched on the local date plus this pass's lookups; a lookup that crashed before its write is not counted, so a crash can cost at most the lookups in flight again. (2) A model withdrawn while a batch transaction is open can still receive that batch's writes; they are hidden by the same policies (`predictions_select`, `likely_to_move_model_live`, feed.ts). The app role cannot lock `prediction_models` rows (no `UPDATE`), so the pass re-reads the status at each batch instead. (3) Core's name match is an exact token set: a middle initial, a trust or a "SURNAME GIVEN & GIVEN" title name fails it and the record is stored without facts (**VERIFY** with the vendor). (4) The night window is in the tenant's zone, from `tenant_settings.default_timezone`; every wake in the window after the first finds nothing to do (the writes are keyed by the local date).

### 21.24 Security notes: module 31 `api/transactions` (wave 2B; migration `0058_transactions_milestone_repair_idx`)
Milestone plans, the one deal-date writer, reminders as trigger firings (R-TXN-1…6, R-OWN-27, E95–E97, E142, E143). **No new SECURITY DEFINER function, policy or grant.** One migration adds one partial index (`deal_dates_repair_idx`) for the reconcile's repair read. The module relies on 0018's `deal_dates_confirm_guard` trigger and CHECKs, policy `deal_dates_all` (deal visibility), policy `step_runs_trigger`, and module 26's path to send.
- **Writers and actors.** A person writes deal dates, as themselves, through one path (`transactions/confirm.ts confirmInTx`): `POST /deal-dates/:id/confirm` and the Phase 1 `POST /deals/:id/dates` and `PATCH /deal-dates/:id`. All three authorize the new Action `milestone.confirm` (DATA_MODEL §8 row "Confirm or move a milestone date": ✅ | ✅ | T | own deals or deals they are on | — | deals they are on). One INSERT or UPDATE writes status, `confirmed_by_user_id` (the session user, never input), `confirmed_at`, `due_at`, `reminder_at`, `due_version` and `basis` together. Core `confirmDate` and the DB guard both refuse a system confirm, entry or move and a forged confirmer (tested through the path and with raw SQL). The system actor writes only (a) the reconcile's repair of a confirmed, unmoved date's missing instants and (b) the clearing of `reminder_at` once a reminder became firings. Both UPDATEs are fenced on `due_on` and `due_version`, so the guard's "system may rewrite an unmoved confirmed date's instants" is the only thing they use. Plans write proposals only (status `proposed`, no confirmer, no instants; CHECK `deal_dates_confirmed_by_human` agrees). Re-planning never deletes or waives (app_user has no DELETE).
- **Firings.** A person's transaction cannot write trigger runs (`step_runs_trigger` WITH CHECK `is_tenant_wide()`). A move, completion or waive therefore enqueues one system job (`milestones.date_changed`): it takes the runs' contact locks in id order, skips stale runs with a visible reason and cancels their queued messages. The reconcile (`milestones.reconcile`, 5 min, as the system) records each due reminder through module 26's `recordFactInTx`, restricted to the approved team playbook made from the `transaction_updates` template. An agent's own `deal_date_upcoming` step never fires from a deal date. Recipients are limited to the deal roles the approved step names (`TriggerStep.roles`, hashed approved content), and only owned contacts receive updates (R-NURT-7). Firing key: `deal_date:{date}:v{due_version}:{step}:{contact}` (unique per tenant).
- **Residual (reported).** An Owner/Admin can insert a trigger run row directly (RLS admits tenant-wide roles by design). `executeTriggerRun` re-checks every run like any other: a forged date, version or due instant is skipped (`DATE_CHANGED`/`FACT_GONE`), and the message text uses the date's **stored** label, never the run's copy (tested). A system-only WITH CHECK would be a policy change for the orchestrator.
- **Send path.** Nothing here sends. Updates are module 26's `executeTriggerRun` → `queueOutboundInTx` → `comms/dispatch.ts`, so D1 opt-outs (R-PAR-12 included), suppressions and the pause apply at send time (E97 tested: a blocked lender update writes `gate_decisions` and never reaches the provider). At send time a completed date is skipped (`DATE_COMPLETED`), and so is a recipient no longer on the deal in a named role (`NOT_A_PARTY`). Contract, money or wire wording downgrades to a draft (R-AST-3); payment details send nothing (R-GATE-7).
- **E142.** `assistant/drafts.ts` re-checks a deal-date draft's bound `due_version` (core `boundVersionStale`) in three places: the approval transaction, the resume of an approved but never-queued approval, and the queue transaction. A re-review re-tap never clears it. The approval-transaction check reads under the approver's RLS; a date they cannot see counts as gone (fail closed).
- **Reads.** Every read route runs under the caller's RLS. Milestone risk on the timeline and the TC board comes from core `milestoneRisk` over stored fields. Queue items show only to callers `exceptions_select` admits (not a TC or a co-agent; the policy is unchanged). Templates (`tenant_settings.milestone_templates`) are read and written only through `milestone_template.manage` (Owner/Admin; §8 names no role, flagged). Every change is audited as `settings.changed` with a diff, and a counsel review is stored only as an Owner/Admin's statement. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL** (C9): business-day conventions (default Monday to Friday, no holidays, shown as unreviewed); sharing deal status with lenders, title and attorneys. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL** (C1): the hour automated updates go out (09:00 local by default, or within one reconcile pass of a confirm whose reminder time already passed).


### 21.25 Security notes: batch 8 review fixes, deal recipients, home values and merges (migration `*_b8_review_deals`, lane number 0059)
- **Who gets the team's automatic deal messages (K1; R-TXN-4, E344–E346).** One rule, core `transactions/recipients.ts teamClientRoles(deals.side)`: buy → buyer, co_buyer; sell → seller, co_seller; dual → all four; lease → none (owner default). The other side's client (often another broker's) never receives milestone updates. Applied when the reminder is planned (`reminderFact`), re-read live under the contact lock when the update is built (`executeTriggerRun`, skip `NOT_OUR_CLIENT`) and on the deal screen. Lender, title and attorney stay exactly as the approved step names them. Nothing here sends: every update still goes through `comms/dispatch.ts` (D1 opt-outs unchanged); none is a touch (D8).
- **Home values (K2; R-AVM-3, E347, E348, E351).** Candidates are only buyer or co_buyer of a closed buy or dual deal (never through `deals.primary_contact_id` alone, never a lease deal); the address is the deal's listing address when it matches one of the contact's addresses, otherwise their `home`; never an address matching a house the person sold through the team. The same SQL predicate (`nurture/repos.ts avmQualifies`) filters TX1, re-checks TX2 under the contact lock before the row is stored and before the vendor answer is used, and re-checks at send time (`ADDRESS_NOT_CURRENT`). The month check and the prior estimate read every record of the person (`app.contact_person_ids`, invoker, existing) on a normalized address key, so a merge never doubles a note. All reads are tenant-filtered (`app.tenant_id()` and the joined rows' `tenant_id`).
- **Merges (ownership-data/F5, sends/F5, ownership-data/F3; R-OWN-24, E349, E350).** `app.contacts_merge_roles()` (SECURITY DEFINER, owner `app_definer`, `search_path = pg_catalog, public`, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC; nobody may execute it) is replaced from 0053's body; every 0053 rule stays (ownership-command fence, tenant fence, listing-role rank, primary contact, seller-update card), and a deal role the survivor already holds now yields to a stronger one ranked by the deal's side (new plain IMMUTABLE `app.deal_role_rank(text, text)`, not a definer, EXECUTE to `app_definer` only). New least-privilege grant: `UPDATE (role) ON deal_contacts TO app_definer`. `milestones.reconcile` (system) re-reads recipients after taking the contact locks, and a new pass plans a consumed reminder once for a merge survivor when the merge skipped or invalidated the loser's run (firing key keeps it idempotent). It never re-plans when a record of the person was sent the update, its message is in flight (`dispatching`, `dispatch_unknown`) or delivered, it failed, or its draft still waits; then it notes that run on the date (`deal_dates.basis.mergedUpdates`), and when nothing can be planned (no approved playbook, a role the side or the step rules out, no owner) it writes a terminal `not_planned` note so the bounded pass never re-selects the row so the deal screen, read under the TC's own RLS, is honest. The deal screen also reads the person's merged records' runs where the caller's RLS shows them; no policy is widened.
- **`home_valuations` writes are system-only (db-public/F3).** RESTRICTIVE `home_valuations_system_insert` / `_update` require `app.actor_kind() = 'system'`; app_user still has no DELETE; reads unchanged. Referential actions (address delete sets `contact_address_id` NULL, erasure cascades) are unaffected (tested).
- **Residual (reported).** R-DATA-6 home-value retention: closed by `app.purge_home_valuations()` (§21.33). `closedDealsOn` (home anniversaries) still counts the primary contact in any role on any side: outside these findings, unchanged.

### 21.26 Security notes: batch 8 review fixes, photo texts, reactions, tests and docs (migration `b8_review_texting`)
**No new SECURITY DEFINER function, table, policy or grant.** One `CREATE OR REPLACE` of the invoker trigger function `app.message_reactions_guard` (only earlier definition: `0056_sms_reactions`; the body is that one verbatim except one line; EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC again).
- **What changed (ownership-data/F6).** The guard and the API's candidate query (`comms/reactions.ts readReactionInTx`) accept a target text on any record of the reaction's person, `app.contact_person_ids` (0043, invoker SQL), instead of only the same contact. Reason: a Team Lead's merge moves the loser's messages in its later `ownership.followup`; until then an inbound tapback resolves to the survivor while the team's text sits on the loser, the link was refused, and the whole body, quote included, was classified, so a quoted "no more texts" became a false person-level do-not-contact.
- **Trust.** The guard still runs only for the system (`REACTION_SYSTEM_ONLY` first). `app.contact_person_ids` reads `contacts` under the caller's RLS, so the system sees its own tenant's records and no other's. Every other bound is unchanged: the team's own non-imported outbound text, sent from the number the reaction came in on to the very phone that reacted, at least `dispatching` and not failed, dispatched before the reaction, created in the 30 days before it. A different person (a shared phone landing on another contact) is still never a target.
- **Opt-outs (D1).** Unchanged and not narrowed further: a reaction is still read by its own words, an unlinked text is classified exactly as before, and opt-out wording outside the quote counts. The fix only stops the team's own quoted words being taken for the person's.
- **Tests.** `apps/api/src/comms/reactions.test.ts` (a reaction between a Team Lead's merge and its follow-up), `packages/db/test/sms_reactions.test.ts` (the guard and the candidate query admit the same targets, a merged record and a real photo text included).

### 21.27 Security notes: telephony safeguards (migrations `0063_telephony_daily_cap`, `0064_mms_text_parts`, `0065_mms_keyword_reply_guard`; lane numbers 0130-0132)
Proof: `apps/api/src/providers/twilio/killswitch.test.ts`, `numberConfig.architecture.test.ts`, `apps/api/src/comms/telephony.safeguards.test.ts`, `telephony.optouts.test.ts`, `telephony.textparts.test.ts`, `webhooks.contract.test.ts`, `webhooks.redaction.test.ts` (real Postgres via `createTestDb()`, the deterministic fakes, and the real Twilio adapter behind a fetch that fails the test if called).
- **No new SECURITY DEFINER function.** `telephony_daily_usage` and `message_text_parts` are new tenant tables with FORCE RLS, the restrictive tenant isolation and `support_read_only_*` policies and the `support_read_only` statement trigger. `telephony_daily_usage`: SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE for members of the tenant (a human send's TX1 runs as that human, R-TEN-3), and a SECURITY INVOKER trigger `app.telephony_daily_usage_guard()` lets a day's count only grow and never move its key, so no caller can reopen a budget. `message_text_parts`: SELECT through the message's own visibility (EXISTS on `messages` under the caller's RLS: the `message_media_skipped` pattern), INSERT/UPDATE tenant-wide only (the system ingestion and job), and the SECURITY INVOKER trigger `app.message_text_parts_guard()` allows only `pending → read` with the key and provider ref unchanged; ON DELETE CASCADE from `messages`, so erasure needs nothing new.
- **`app.messages_dispatch_guard()` replaced (from its newest definition, 0053; still SECURITY DEFINER owned by `app_definer`, `search_path = pg_catalog, public`, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC; the migration re-checks the shape and every earlier rule).** One clause of the keyword-reply exemption changed: an `sms` STOP confirmation / HELP reply also matches an inbound keyword that arrived as `mms`. The exemption still requires the system initiator, the `opt_out_confirmation` / `help_reply` category, the informational purpose, the same conversation, the SAME number (`im.from_address = NEW.to_address`) and the last 24 hours. Why: the gate (`keywordSenderHmacs`) and the guard both excluded MMS, so a STOP carried by an MMS (its Body, or now only a text part, R-TWL-11) could never be confirmed. Risk considered: it widens which reply reaches an opted-out number only to the number that itself sent the keyword by MMS; no other category, recipient or channel is exempted (adversarial cases in `telephony.textparts.test.ts`).
- **Trust decisions.** A status callback's tenant is only ever the signature guard's (or the number's, from a signed `From`); a missing `From` without a guard tenant is unrouted (R-TWL-4). Twilio's `OptOutType=STOP` can only ADD a revoke our classifier did not produce (R-TWL-7). A text part is fetched only by validated SID through the adapter (never a URL from the webhook), size-capped and strictly decoded before it is classified, and only ever adds opt-outs (R-TWL-11). 30032 / 30034 only ever make a sender LESS ready (R-TWL-9); only a provider read restores it.

### 21.28 Security notes: batch 9 review fixes, comms / routing / opt-outs (migration `*_b9_review_comms`, lane number 0066)
