Proof: `apps/api/src/comms/b9review.routing.test.ts`, `b9review.senders.test.ts`, `b9review.alerts.test.ts`, `telephony.optouts.test.ts`, `telephony.safeguards.test.ts`, `apps/api/src/leadEmail/leadEmail.test.ts` (ownership-data/F3), `apps/web/src/surfaces/admin/NumbersPage.test.tsx` (real Postgres via `createTestDb()`, the deterministic fakes, and the real Twilio adapter behind a fetch that fails the test if called).
- **No new SECURITY DEFINER function, table, policy or grant.** The migration adds `phone_numbers.sender_not_verified_at` / `sender_not_verified_detail` (a pair CHECK; detail ≤ 200 chars) under the table's existing FORCE RLS tenant policies and grants. It is written by the system (dispatch TX2 or the status event) and cleared by an Owner/Admin's "Check registration status" (carrier reports approved) or by attaching a DIFFERENT registration, each audited.
- **Sender health is per number (sends/F1).** A 30032 / 30034 now makes only the failing number less ready (`repos/gate.ts numberFacts` reports an approved registration as pending for a flagged number), never the shared `sender_registrations` row, so one misconfigured number cannot stop texting for the team. It still only ever makes a sender LESS ready; only the provider read (or attaching a different registration) clears it.
- **Keyword replies may go from the team number (sends/F3).** A STOP confirmation or HELP reply for a number that cannot text is sent from the team main number when that one can. The recipient restriction is unchanged: the gate's `keywordSenders` and `app.messages_dispatch_guard()` (untouched) still let such a reply reach only the phone that texted the keyword in this conversation in the last 24 hours, so this widens WHICH of the team's own numbers may send the one exempt reply, not who receives it. A system keyword reply from a tracking number never becomes the conversation's sticky sender.
- **Tracked inquiries and opt-outs (sends/F2, ownership-data/F2, F5, F6).** An MMS with declared text parts never routes at ingestion; `comms.mms_text` makes the R-TRK-3 inquiry only after every part is read and none revokes, looks like a reaction or was unreadable, under the ingestion's lock order (address + intake keys, then contacts) and the same external id, so it is idempotent. The inquiry attaches to the threaded contact (`IngestOptions.attachTo`, a contact the caller found by the same phone HMAC under its address lock). The lead source is re-read `FOR SHARE` under the delivery's locks (inbound, the job and the partner-feed poll), so turning a source off waits for an in-flight delivery and never makes it throw.
- **Alert-phone keywords are idempotent (sends/F4).** The agent keyword path keys its consent event on the provider MessageSid (`evidence.providerRef`) and its Admin notice on it, so an R4 re-armed delivery writes nothing twice and cannot re-revoke after a later START.
- **Kill switch before the touch (ownership-data/F4).** The adapter reports `liveRequestsOff()`; dispatch and dial refuse in TX1 with `TELEPHONY_LIVE_OFF` (a recorded gate deny) before the cap unit and the touch. The adapter's own refusal stays as defence in depth; the switch's default stays off.
- **Team addresses (ownership-data/F3).** The lead-email team-point filter reads every member who joined (not only active ones), so a suspended or removed member's account email and alert phone never become a lead's point (and so never disable E60's alert-phone path).

### 21.29 Security notes: batch 9 review fixes, web UI and copy (migration `0067_b9_review_ui`)
- **No new SECURITY DEFINER function, policy or grant.** The migration only widens `message_media_skipped_reason_check` with `LIVE_REQUESTS_OFF` (ui/F7); the table keeps FORCE RLS and its 0019 policies.
- **Trust decision (ui/F3).** `telephony` (`live`/`off`/`simulated`) on GET /numbers and the Lead sources overview is computed from configuration only (`comms/telephonyMode.ts`) and shown to Owner/Admin; the kill switch is still enforced in the Twilio adapter, never by the UI.
- Proof: `apps/api/src/leads/sources.test.ts` (b9 ui/F3), `apps/api/src/comms/media.test.ts` and `telephony.textparts.test.ts` (b9 ui/F7), on real Postgres via `createTestDb()`.

### 21.30 Security notes: CMA, modules 70 `core/valuation` and 71 `api/cma` (D9 lanes V1-B and V1-C; no migration)
The in-house valuation engine and the agent-facing CMA API (R-CMA-2…8; E360–E371, E373). **No new SECURITY DEFINER function, table, policy, grant or migration.** It writes only `cma_reports` (S0, migration `*_cma_schema`), under 0061's policies and guard, and reads listings, contact addresses, connections and `jurisdiction_packs` under the caller's RLS.
- **Writers and readers.** Every route authorizes the new Action `cma.prepare` (DATA_MODEL §8: ✅ | ✅ | T | O | — | — | —; never the system, no support key) on the contact's facts (own, collaborating, team) from RLS-visible rows; a contact whose detail the caller cannot see (a pond card), an invisible CMA, another tenant's id and every denial are 404 (R-PERM-5). A research CMA without a contact is `{own}` for its creator only. A person stores a CMA as themselves (`created_by_user_id = app.user_id()`, insert policy); a Team Lead's edit of an agent's draft matches no row under the update policy and is 404 (R-CMA-8). The one-tap path supersedes only the caller's own open draft (`myOpenDraft` filters on `app.user_id()`), and its insert uses `cma_reports_one_open_draft` as the `ON CONFLICT` arbiter, so a double tap yields one draft. Support reads through RLS only (no route); the client principal reads nothing (S0's staff-only layer).
- **Licence and counsel gates.** The agent gate needs a live MLS connection whose licence pack records `valuation.agent_cma`; otherwise `CMA_NO_MLS_CONNECTION` / `CMA_VALUATION_NOT_ALLOWED`, in words (E371). Client readiness (`clientShare`) is computed on every view from live rows and never stored, for every MLS connection the stored CMA was built from (the run's reads, rate pool included, each comp's and the subject record's) as well as those the agent gate admits now: `app.platform_cleared('platform:cma')`, each connection's display pack (review, attribution, disclaimer; judged apart from freshness), freshness, `valuation.client_cma`, the valuation section's own counsel stamp, a statement for the subject's state, every comp's client rendering with its sold price and date (V1-A's `clientView`; a mirrored pair through `clientSoldView`), the subject's live records' `avm_display_allowed` under `avm_flag_scope`, and unconfirmed near matches. A subject with no address key is never allowed (`SUBJECT_ADDRESS_UNKNOWN`: those two checks need it); a contact or entered street address the key cannot be built from is refused at prepare time, naming the missing part. Nothing is shared in V1 (no comms path is called), so the queue-time re-check and the sweep are V2's (R-CMA-9).
- **Strict input schemas and the import boundary.** Request bodies are strict Zod objects (no tenant id, creator, status or stored result; entered facts accept property facts only, so no school, demographic, crime, person or listing-text field reaches the engine; R-CMA-2). `include`, `exclude` and `nearMatchDismissed` hold at most 100 ids and keep only ids the run itself offered to the engine (read under RLS), so a forged, leased, future or other tenant's id is never stored, and an exclusion past the engine's 50-item not-used list is kept (the engine always lists the agent's exclusions); a dismissal names only a current near match and is audited `cma.near_match_dismissed` with listing ids only. `apps/api/src/cma/**` is on the R-PRED-4 `no-prediction-import` lint list. A subject fact or address part outside the engine's strict bounds (V1-A stores wider values) is left out and said (`subject.factSentences`) or refused by name, never a 500; a ZodError from the engine is a 422.
- **Module 70 `core/valuation`.** Pure and tenant-free: no database, no I/O. Its inputs are strict Zod objects (`CmaSubjectSchema`, `CmaCandidateSchema`, `CmaTierReadSchema`: unknown keys, `__proto__` and out-of-range numbers are refused), so only property, transaction, time and location facts reach comp selection (R-CMA-2, fair housing). An architecture test holds an import allowlist: a non-test file imports only its siblings and zod (never core/listings, core/predict or a contact module), and `packages/core/src/valuation/**` is on the `no-prediction-import` lint list. `CMA_DEFAULTS` is pinned by digest to `CMA_METHOD_VERSION` (`valuation.test.ts`), so a changed default needs a new method version.
- **The seam (batch 10).** `apps/api/src/cma/deps.ts` re-exports lane V1-A's and lane V1-B's real modules and nothing else: from core/listings `addressKey`, `streetOfKey`, `cellsWithin`, `uspsCode`, `packReviewed` and `valuationStatement`; from api/mls `soldCandidates` (its `TierQuery` / `SoldQuery`), `mlsRowsAtAddress`, `liveNearMatches`, `valuationEligible`, `clientView`, `clientSoldView`, `factsOfRow` and `LISTING_COLS`; from core/valuation `prepareCma`, `CMA_DEFAULTS`, the strict `Cma*Schema` types, `addMonths`, `postalKey` and `subdivisionKey`. Its only code adapts shapes (a listings row to the engine's sale, V1-A's tier names to V1-B's). A stored CMA carries V1-B's method version (`cma/1`). The tier reads are V1-A's: leakproof plain-column predicates under the caller's RLS on the 0068_cma_mls_data indexes, one `soldCandidates` call per run with each tier's limit from `CMA_DEFAULTS` (300; 600 for the rate pool), reaching back as far as the engine's back-test needs (its window, 6 months, plus 24 months: 30 months). The agent gate is V1-A's `valuationEligible(…, 'agent_cma')`; client readiness lists every blocker and always includes whatever `valuationEligible(…, 'client_cma')` refuses, so it is never looser than V1-A's client gate. A mirrored team sale is one candidate through core `dedupeMirrors`: the team row's identity and facts, the MLS mirror's close price, size and sale facts (E366), shown to clients only through `clientSoldView`. A stored MLS record whose values V1-B's strict schemas refuse (a price above $1 billion, a count above 10⁹) is left out of the run and counted in the response, so one bad record never stops a CMA.
- **Residual (reported).** A merged-away record's CMAs are listed on the survivor only for callers who can still see that record (contacts RLS hides a closed, unowned loser from its former agent); no policy is widened here. The licence scope (V1) and state statement text (V4) are unconfirmed; no legal text ships.

### 21.31 Security notes: module 42 `api/documents` (migration `0069_documents`, lane number 0140)
Upload, versions, scan gating, share, hold and retention, contract-date proposals (R-DOC-1…10, E130, E131, E159, E400–E419). **No new SECURITY DEFINER function, table, policy or grant.** The migration adds two invoker triggers (each with `search_path = pg_catalog, public`, a foreign-tenant early return so RLS refuses, and the `app_definer`/`app_retention` skip copied from `app.documents_guard`) and seven indexes.
- **`app.documents_integrity_guard` (BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF shared_to_portal ON documents; fires after `documents_guard` by name).** Gap closed: referential checks bypass RLS, so the foreign key alone accepted another person's or another purpose's object (proved first in `packages/db/test/documents_0069.test.ts` with the trigger disabled in a rolled-back transaction). INSERT: a user actor naming themselves (`DOCUMENT_UPLOADER_SELF`); the object, read under the caller's RLS, is purpose `document`, live with its DEK, created by the uploader, PDF/JPEG/PNG, 1–10,000,000 bytes, and used by no other document (`DOCUMENT_OBJECT_INVALID`; unique index `documents_object_uq` is the backstop); a superseded row visible to the caller in the same scope (`DOCUMENT_VERSION_INVALID`), `version_no := prev + 1`, `kind := prev.kind` (else `version_no := 1`); `documents_supersedes_uq` keeps chains linear (E406). UPDATE: changing `shared_to_portal` needs a user actor and `app.deal_writable` / `app.listing_writable` (`DOCUMENT_SHARE_NOT_ALLOWED`): TCs and ISAs never share; any Team Lead may share a listing document, as listing.edit. The system can no longer insert a document (no code path did).
- **`app.deal_dates_evidence_guard` (BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF evidence_claim_id ON deal_dates).** NULL passes (manual dates, ON DELETE SET NULL). Otherwise `source = 'document'` and the claim, read under the caller's RLS, is not `needs_review`, is `date_time`, and belongs to a `contract_extraction` whose `trigger_ref->>'dealId'` is the date's deal (`DEAL_DATE_EVIDENCE_INVALID`); `deal_dates_evidence_claim_uq` allows one date per claim (E414). A claim the caller cannot read (another member's extraction) is refused like a missing one.
- **Trust decisions.** (1) `objects_select` (0001) lets an object's creator and tenant-wide actors read every object row, so the uploader and an Owner can read a quarantined object's `storage_key` and `dek_wrapped`: **only the API** stops them streaming it. The one query that fetches a document's key (`documents/repos.ts cleanObjectOf`) joins `scan_status = 'clean'` and a live `document` object; `documents/architecture.test.ts` pins it and limits `getObject` to `read.ts`, `scan.ts` and `extract.ts`. (2) The scanner and the PDF reader are platform configuration (R-INT-1); production without `ALLOW_FAKE_PROVIDERS` gets `UnconfiguredScanner` / `UnconfiguredTextExtractor` (uploads stay pending, nothing is read), never the fakes, which pass every file (`providers/index.ts`; tested in `documents/authz.test.ts`). (3) The scan runs as the system in two short transactions with the call between them; a ProviderError retries; `UPDATE … WHERE scan_status = 'pending'` applies a verdict once. A blocked object is shredded at once unless held (then by the reconcile after the hold is lifted). (4) Extraction is the PDF text layer through `DocumentTextExtractor` with a built-in, unreviewed label table; no model reads contract text; claims are verified by core `verifyQuote` plus the snippet's own date; nothing becomes a date without a person's confirm through `confirmInTx` (module 31's one writer; `documents/**` writes no `deal_dates` SQL). (5) Support (under a grant) lists metadata (`document.view`) but `document.download` has no support grant. (6) No file or display name reaches a log, job payload (ids only) or audit row (kind, version, size, type; the verdict, reason and engine; ids for downloads). (7) `providers/index.ts` returns `{ ...fakes, …, scanner, textExtractor, … }`: the production overrides come after the `...fakes` spread, so a provider added to the fakes without its own override line would reach production as a fake; the scanner and the reader each have their line (and `documents/authz.test.ts` proves the production bag). (8) Unscannable fails closed: a password-protected or corrupt file is `infected` like malware, never clean, and its object is shredded (E404). (9) Evidence snippets are at most 160 characters of the page text (`finder.ts SNIPPET_LIMIT`); the text itself is never stored. (10) The generic `POST /assistant/actions/:id/approve` only acknowledges a contract extraction (status `approved`, nothing written); since batch 11 the generic reject and claim review refuse it (`EXTRACTION_USE_DOCUMENT`, §21.35) and the inbox does not list it.
- **Residuals (reported).** (1) No column holds why a file was blocked: the reason is read from the `document.scanned` audit row, which only Owners and Admins can read, so an uploader who is neither sees one sentence true for both reasons that still asks for a copy without a password (batch 11, Q5; a later reviewed column would name the reason). (2) A version chain is grouped from rows the caller can see: a peer who sees v1 and v3 but not a pending or blocked v2 sees two entries. (3) Closed in batch 11 (§21.35): the generic `POST /assistant/claims/:id/review` and reject refuse a contract extraction (`EXTRACTION_USE_DOCUMENT`). (4) Purge after retention is not built (R-DOC-10 gap), and the 7-year default is a code constant (a tenant setting needs a reviewed schema change). (5) An upload transaction that rolls back (for example `DOCUMENT_VERSION_CONFLICT`, E406) leaves an orphan blob in storage: `putObject` runs before the INSERTs, and the blob stays unreadable because its wrapped DEK never committed; no sweep removes it (the brief names MMS and marketing uploads as the same case; that was not checked here). (6) Vendor checks for a real adapter, none done (nothing was read on a vendor page): N11 (ClamAV or a commercial scanner, not chosen) **VERIFY** the clamd INSTREAM protocol and its `StreamMaxLength` default, signature update cadence, how encrypted PDFs are reported, sizing and rescans; N17 (pdfjs-dist or a vendor, not chosen) **VERIFY** the text-content offsets, turning off eval (the CVE-2024-4367 class), worker caps and the licence. (7) **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL:** retention (C9, C13) and whether listing documents follow the sell-side close; whether a held malicious file must be kept and whether blocked uploads are records (E405); sharing listing agreements to a seller's portal (C10); the copy "Blocked by the virus check".

### 21.32 Module 52 `api/ads`: Google Ads within the Owner's cap (migration `0070_ads_platform_pack`, lane number 0160)
**No new SECURITY DEFINER function, table, column, policy or grant.** The migration adds the `platform:ads` pack row and two SECURITY INVOKER trigger functions, each with `search_path` pinned to `pg_catalog, public` and every table `public.`-qualified (R-TEN-5).
- **Why invoker.** `app.ad_allocation_events_ads_gate()` (BEFORE INSERT of a `planned` row) and `app.assistant_actions_ads_gate()` (BEFORE INSERT / UPDATE of an `ad_reallocation`) read `jurisdiction_packs` (readable by `app_user`), `ad_campaigns` and `integration_connections` as the caller, through the caller's RLS. A campaign the caller cannot see is refused (`ADS_GOOGLE_ONLY`), so nothing needs a definer's wider view. The deferred definer `app.ad_allocation_cap_check()` (0018) is unchanged and still decides the cap; the gates run before it. Neither gate has a superuser bypass.
- **Order.** PostgreSQL fires same-event BEFORE triggers in name order, so `assistant_actions_ads_gate_ins/_upd` fire before `assistant_actions_decision_guard`: an approval while dark is `ADS_NOT_CLEARED` even for the system (proved in `packages/db/test/rls_smoke_ads.sql`; **VERIFY** on any PostgreSQL upgrade).
- **One apply path.** `apps/api/src/ads/dispatch.ts` is the only caller of `AdsProvider.applyBudgetChange` and `pauseCampaign` (reserved names in `realty/no-provider-outbound`; `ads/architecture.test.ts`). TX1 runs as the tapping Owner/Admin (decision + planned rows + audit); the provider call runs outside any transaction with the planned event's id as idempotency key, bounded by `APPLY_TIMEOUT_MS`; TX2 runs as the system (`systemActor(tenant, 'provider')`). An unknown outcome writes nothing and is resolved by readback in `ads.reconcile`. The only automatic money action is the pause at the monthly cap. Nothing in `ads/**` imports `comms/dispatch`, `core/predict` or a model provider (D1, R-PRED-4).
- **The freeze.** `app.assistant_actions_ads_gate()` also refuses any change to an approved or executed `ad_reallocation`'s payload, edited_payload or kind (`AD_APPROVAL_FROZEN`), for every role and the system, so a plan can only ever name what the person approved.
- **System-only mirror and costs.** `ad_campaigns` (external id, status, targeting, budget mirror), the outcome rows and the `ads_sync` `lead_source_costs` rows are written only by api/ads as the system (0018 policies and `AD_BUDGET_THROUGH_LEDGER`; `ads/architecture.test.ts` for the rest); a person only links a campaign to a listing and an approved creative.
- **A null owner closes the generic route.** Proposals and holds have no owner and no contact, so `POST /assistant/actions/:id/approve|reject` (which requires `owner_user_id` = the caller) answers 404 and the feed's `feed_mark_actions` skips them; only `/ads/proposals/:id/approve|reject` and the resume tap decide them (`apps/api/src/ads/propose.test.ts` checks no inbox or feed shows them).
- **Support's read-only view.** A support session under an Owner's grant reads ownerless `ad_reallocation` rows through the existing `assistant_actions` read policy (it reads no ads table row and changes nothing). This is a tenancy trust decision recorded here: no policy may be added for these rows in this module.
- **Tokens and logs.** The Google Ads token is decrypted in a short system transaction each time it is used (each reconcile pass, each apply; R-INT-4) and never logged; job payloads are `{}`; logs, audit `changes` and notification text carry ids, codes, amounts and counts only, never a campaign or account name (R-TEN-7).
- **Clock.** Ledger `occurred_at` is the DB `now()` of each transaction, so planned < applied in one database (**VERIFY** in every harness that injects a clock: the pass clock and the DB clock can differ in tests). The pass clock (`AdsDeps.now`, the job's DB clock in production) sets account-zone months, plan staleness and the 2-hour "not checked" check; tests inject the fake platform's clock.
- **Operation budget (VERIFY).** 4 reads per connection-hour (accountInfo, listCampaigns, daily and hourly spend; 96 a day), plus the weekly proposal's 2 reads, each approved change and each cap pause, against the owner-reported Explorer limit of about 2,880 operations a day (whether reads count is **VERIFY**). `APPLY_TIMEOUT_MS` (30 s), `STALE_PLANNED_MS` (10 min) and the property test's run count are engineering estimates (**VERIFY**).
- **Residuals.** (1) The Google Ads adapter is a fake only; production gets `UnconfiguredAds`. Every vendor shape is **VERIFY** (N14). (2) The housing, budget and currency facts of the last sync live in `integration_connections.sync_cursor` (JSON), because `ad_campaigns` has no column for them. (3) Month-to-date for a resume refusal is read from the stored `ads_sync` rows, not from a live provider call; a connection without a lead source stops those rows, so its resumes and taps are refused (`ADS_NO_LEAD_SOURCE`), while the cap pause still covers its mirrored campaigns (b11 sends-gates/F2).

### 21.33 Security notes: backlog gaps (migrations `0071_backlog_merge_guard`, `0072_backlog_merge_work`, `0073_backlog_home_value_retention`; lane numbers 0200–0202)
- **`app.contacts_guard()` gains the merge guard (R-OWN-24, E546–E548).** SECURITY DEFINER, replaced with CREATE OR REPLACE from 0001's body verbatim plus one block; owner `app_definer`, `search_path = pg_catalog, public`, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC, self-checked (shape, every 0001 rule marker, `MERGE_UNAUDITED`, the trigger still BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE FOR EACH ROW). Under a tenant context it refuses (`MERGE_UNAUDITED`, insufficient_privilege) any INSERT with `merged_into_contact_id`, any re-pointing, and any first set that is not the ownership engine's merge: `app.ownership_command()` = `NEW.ownership_command_id`, the row closed as `merged`, and that command's `merged_away` event for the row naming the survivor (`lower(payload->>'survivorId')`). The block runs before the "nothing changed" return, which previously let a bare UPDATE of the column through, so the merge triggers that carry no engine check of their own (`contacts_dnc_follow_merge` 0018, `contacts_merge_children` 0027, `contacts_merge_webchat_sessions` → `app.webchat_sessions_follow_merge` 0046: they check only the tenant and that the survivor exists) can no longer be fired by a bare UPDATE from app_user. Only `contacts_merge_roles` (0053/0059) and `contacts_merge_work` (0072) check `app.ownership_command()` and the `merged_away` event; 0071's header says every merge trigger does, which is wrong (erratum in `0076_b11_review_backlog`, batch 11 review db-public/F1). With no tenant context nothing is checked: only a superuser or BYPASSRLS role reaches a contact then (RESTRICTIVE + FORCE RLS), no definer writes the column, and such a bare UPDATE is a **partial merge** (points, tags, notes, chat sessions and the do-not-contact follow; deal roles, tasks and appointments stay; no ledger event): platform maintenance only, never an application path. A clear to NULL is not checked by the guard, but it cannot happen to an engine-merged record: `contacts_check2` (0001) requires the column while `closed_reason = 'merged'`, and `app.purge_contact_internal` deletes a merge tree deepest first, so the FK's ON DELETE SET NULL never fires on one; only a record set by maintenance without closing it can be cleared. It only refuses more; no grant, table or policy changes; the other merge triggers are unchanged. This supersedes the "contacts_guard does not cover the column" notes of 0053 and 0059 (§21.16, §21.25). Proof: `packages/db/test/backlog_merge_guard.test.ts` (E547 (fences): the partial merge and the CHECK refusing a clear), smoke `rls_smoke_p23.sql`.
- **`app.contacts_merge_work()` (new; R-OWN-24, R-PLAN-3, E549–E552).** SECURITY DEFINER trigger function, AFTER UPDATE OF `merged_into_contact_id` ON contacts WHEN the column goes from NULL to a value; trigger `contacts_merge_work` sorts last among `contacts_merge_*`; owner `app_definer`, `search_path = pg_catalog, public`, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC, self-checked. Same fence as `app.contacts_merge_roles`: the row's own tenant, the engine merge's command and its `merged_away` event (so a superuser's bare UPDATE moves nothing; the guard above refuses an unaudited set from app_user). It moves the loser's tasks and appointments to the survivor (`contact_id`, `updated_at` only); the invoker `appointments_sync_version` bumps `sync_version` once and `feed_mark_*` mark the assignee's or user's feed groups (app_definer already has INSERT on feed_dirty, 0034). Nothing is sent and no opt-out state changes; who may read the rows is the unchanged policies. Least-privilege grants: `UPDATE (contact_id, updated_at)` ON tasks and ON appointments TO app_definer (the self-check proves no table-wide UPDATE and none on the assignee/user columns). The migration's backfill (as the migration owner) moves rows of records merged earlier to the root survivor (at most 64 hops); because each moved appointment's `sync_version` moves, the next `calendar.reconcile` re-pushes each future one once to the member's linked calendar (the event title may name the person); past ones are never re-pushed. Proof: `packages/db/test/backlog_merge_work.test.ts`, `apps/api/src/calendar/backlogMergeWork.test.ts` (fake calendar adapter only).
- **Weekly plan (api `insights/plan.ts`, no migration).** `renderFilter` also reads each visible evidence row's current contact (task/appointment `contact_id`, deal `primary_contact_id`) under the member's RLS; an item whose stored contact is no longer live is shown and accepted on that contact only when every visible evidence row names it and it is visible, live and not do-not-contact (`personDnc`); otherwise dropped as before. The stored payload is not rewritten; core `dropInvisible` is unchanged. Proof: `apps/api/src/insights/backlogPlan.test.ts`, `b7ReviewPlan.test.ts`.
- **`app.purge_home_valuations()` (retention; owner `app_retention`; R-DATA-6, E553, E554).** `SECURITY DEFINER` with `search_path` pinned to `pg_catalog, public`; `EXECUTE` revoked from `PUBLIC` and granted to `app_user` only. It refuses (`HOME_VALUE_PURGE_SYSTEM_ONLY`) unless `app.tenant_id()` is set and `app.actor_kind()` is `system`, so a user, a support session (even under a grant) or a pre-tenant transaction cannot call it. It takes no argument: the instant is the database clock (`now()`). Because `app_retention` is `BYPASSRLS`, the delete and both citation subqueries filter `tenant_id = app.tenant_id()`. It deletes `home_valuations` with `valued_at < now() - 13 months` (**VERIFY WITH COUNSEL** C13 retention defaults, C6 valuation wording; V13: whether 13 months fits D9's in-house valuation built from MLS data under the MLS licence term, since no third-party AVM licence applies) except rows cited by `playbook_step_runs.trigger_ref->'ref'->>'valuationId'` of a run still `scheduled`, or `converted_to_draft` whose `assistant_actions` row is `proposed` or `approved`, and returns one count. New index `home_valuations (tenant_id, valued_at)`. The self-check proves the owner, the pinned path, no `PUBLIC` execute, `app_user` execute, no parameter, the index, and that `app_user` still has no `DELETE` on `home_valuations`. Called only by `avm.refresh` (api/nurture/avm.ts), first, in its own system transaction, before the vendor check; it never reaches the vendor. No grant to `app_user` on any table; erasure unchanged. Since `0076_b11_review_backlog` (§21.36) it also strips the deleted rows' citations of the estimate. **V10** (how the web shows a purged row's citation): web `design/Evidence.tsx` has no route for `home_valuation`, so a citation always renders as plain text, never a link; before 0076 it went on quoting the estimate ("… in the home value estimate") after the row was gone, and since 0076 the API returns it with `sourceAvailable: false` and no snippet, which `feed/lib/format.ts` shows as "Source removed under retention policy". Proof: `apps/api/src/nurture/homeValueRetention.test.ts`.
- **The system listing-agent writer (no migration; R-OWN-22, E540–E545).** `workspace/listings.ts setListingAgentInTx` is the one writer of `listings.listing_agent_user_id`: `PATCH /listings/:id/agent` (Owner/Admin, `listing.reassign`, eligibility by `assertAssignable`; the web offers it on the listing page as "Listing agent" since the batch 11 review, api-ui/F8, so the `MEMBER_HAS_LISTINGS` and "no active listing agent" instructions name a step that exists) and the staged deactivation job (system worker actor; destination: the member's active Team Lead, else the Owner/Admin who ran it while active and not a TC, else the first active Owner, owner question Q2) both call it. It locks the row and writes only while `source = 'team'` and the agent is still the one the caller read, so a concurrent change wins and the loser is a no-op with no audit; for the job (`openOnly`) also only while the locked row is still open (`OPEN_LISTING_STATUSES`, `workspace/listingAgent.ts`, the one definition the job, preview, stage 4 and the `MEMBER_HAS_LISTINGS` refusal read), so a listing closed after the job listed it keeps its agent (batch 11 reconciliation backlog-4, backlog-5); every write is audited `listing.agent_changed` (the system's with reason `OWNER_DEACTIVATED`). The system write runs under the existing `listings_update` policy for the system (`is_tenant_wide`); no policy or grant changed. In the same transaction the job calls `marketing/cards.ts syncReviewCards`, which closes the departed agent's review cards and opens the new agent's. MLS, sold, withdrawn and expired listings are never touched; open deals are not moved (no deal-owner change exists). Proof: `apps/api/src/leads/deactivationListings.test.ts`.
  - *V11 (**VERIFY WITH COUNSEL**, both halves open).* (1) That moving a listing's agent in Realty OS is an internal record change and not a change to the listing agreement (the Members page says so, `MembersPage.tsx`, E540). (2) Whether the sellers must be told when their listing's agent changes, by the staged removal or by an Owner/Admin's change: nothing notifies a seller today (owner question 12 covers only the new agent).
  - *V12 (report only; dispatch unchanged).* A marketing or open-house send the departed listing agent queued before stage 1 is **refused at dispatch, not re-sent**: `comms/runtime.ts senderActor` gives no sender actor to a membership that is not `active` (`deactivating` from stage 1, then `removed`), so `comms/dispatch.ts` judges the queued human send as the system with `forceDeny` `SENDER_NOT_AUTHORIZED` (open-house invites are human sends through `composeHumanMessage`, `marketing/invites.ts`). Nothing re-addresses it to the new listing agent, who sends again if it is still wanted.

### 21.34 Security notes: homeowner profile (owner decision D10; modules 77-79; migration `0074_homeowner_profiles`, lane number 0210)
**No new SECURITY DEFINER function.** The system purges in its own transaction through the system write policy, so there is no retention definer.
- **Tables and policies.** `homeowner_profiles` and `homeowner_profile_facts` (DATA_MODEL §6.z): FORCE RLS, RESTRICTIVE `tenant_isolation` (staff only: the client principal reads nothing, E583), the `support_read_only_*` policies and the statement trigger (support reads under an Owner's grant and writes nothing), `profiles_select` (`retention_until > now()` AND `app.contact_detail_row`), `facts_select` (a visible parent), and FOR ALL `profiles_write`/`facts_write` for the system actor tenant-wide. Grants: SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE to `app_user`; every write needs the system policy. Proof: `packages/db/test/rls_smoke_homeowner.sql` (every user role refused, support refused by RLS and trigger, cross-tenant FKs refused, the CHECKs and guards), and the generic isolation, invariant and rule-coverage suites.
- **Invoker guards.** `app.homeowner_profiles_guard` (subject address belongs to the contact; contact and address immutable; leaving `matched` only without facts) and `app.homeowner_facts_guard` (facts only under `matched`); `EXECUTE` revoked from `PUBLIC`; a row of another tenant returns early so RLS rejects it (as 0056).
- **Trust decisions.** (1) One adapter: the Beaufort connector fills module 50's `PublicRecordsProvider`; its property `lookup` always throws not configured (`PUBLIC_RECORDS_PROPERTY_LOOKUP_OFF`), so predict never queries a county for every owned home (decision (b)); predict never calls `lookupHomeowner` (`homeowner/architecture.test.ts`). (2) The adapter gets one postal address and nothing else (R-INT-1). (3) SSRF: service URLs are code constants (`providers/publicRecords/fields.ts`), checked at construction against `PUBLIC_RECORDS_ALLOWED_HOSTS` (public DNS names only), https, default port, no credentials, query or fragment; GET only, `redirect: 'manual'` (any 3xx rejected), 15 s timeout, 2 MiB streamed cap, a generic User-Agent, one limiter per instance (≥ 1 s apart, ≤ 3 requests per lookup). The where clause is built only from uppercased normalized tokens matching `^[A-Z0-9 ]{1,60}$` (house number `^\d{1,6}[A-Z]?$`), quotes doubled as a second guard. (4) `PUBLIC_RECORDS_LIVE_CALLS=false` everywhere by default; while false no request is built. (5) Person-level data is stored only after an exact address and person-owner match (entity owners dropped first); owner names live in memory only and are never stored, hashed or logged; logs carry counts and codes only. (6) The read route runs under the viewer's RLS, returns a strict schema with no owner name, score or estimate, writes nothing and reads no `home_valuations` (decision (d)).
- **Residuals.** (1) Every county path, layer and field is **VERIFY** (no live call has been made); the county's terms of use and rate limits are unconfirmed. (2) The ZIP-to-county table is approximate (**VERIFY**): an address in a ZIP outside the table is never looked up. (3) The name match is an exact token set, so a middle initial fails it (stored as `not_matched`, nothing kept). (4) A failure on an address edited since the last answer keeps the earlier values until the next answer (shown with their check date). (5) The outreach pack gates nothing; any future outreach path must check it and is a reviewed change. (6) Owner-name formats on the real layer (middle initials, "LAST FIRST & FIRST2", ETAL, JR) are **VERIFY** (brief verify_items 14): measure the `not_matched` rate on a sample before relying on it; any tolerance is a reviewed module-49 change. (7) Open with counsel before any live call (**VERIFY WITH COUNSEL**; DEPLOY "Before any live county call", step 9): SC Code 30-2-50 for internal display on existing contacts, absentee prospecting, vendor data, texts, LLC and out-of-state owners, and whether D11's clearance covered these (verify_items 9); Beaufort County's GIS distribution policy ("no passing data on to others") inside a multi-tenant CRM (verify_items 10; ask the county's Mapping and Applications Department too); the 12-month retention and keeping owner mailing addresses at all (verify_items 11); showing D9's in-house value to agents on this panel (`valuationEligible`, C6; verify_items 12). (8) Owner action: whether Jasper's `Parcels/FeatureServer/0` (TaxPIN) holds owners (verify_items 13); until then Jasper is links only. (9) The 1 s request spacing is per process, so it multiplies with the number of worker processes (verify_items 8). (10) A merge moves no addresses, inquiries or intent, so a recognised duplicate's home stays on the merged-away record and its profile is purged; the survivor builds its own and its panel says a record was merged into it (batch 11 review ownership-data/F6).

### 21.35 Security notes: batch 11 review fixes, documents (migration `0075_b11_review_documents`; module 42)
Findings ownership-data/F1, sends-gates/F1 and api-ui/F1 of the batch 11 interaction review (R-DOC-1, R-DATA-7, R-OWN-24, R-AST-21).
- **`app.contact_merge_root(uuid, uuid)` and `app.contact_merge_tree_ids(uuid, uuid)` (new SECURITY DEFINER functions; owner `app_definer`, `search_path = pg_catalog, public`, STABLE, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC and granted to `app_user` only; the migration self-checks all four).** Gap closed: documents never move on a merge, but contacts RLS hides a closed, unowned merged-away record from the survivor's agent and Team Lead, so `documents_select`'s `app.contact_detail_visible(loser)` and the invoker `app.contact_merge_tree(survivor)` (0037) both stopped at what the caller sees and the record's contact documents vanished for everyone but the Owner and Admins, the uploader included. The root helper follows `merged_into_contact_id` up from a record inside the caller's own tenant (`app.tenant_id()`; another tenant's id comes back unchanged) and returns the record the chain ends at, or its input when the record was never merged, the chain is corrupt (CYCLE clause) or longer than 64 hops. The tree helper returns a record and every record merged into it (at most 64 hops), or nothing for another tenant's id. Output: ids of the caller's own tenant only, which reveal where a merge chain goes and nothing else (no column, no name). Who may read is still decided under the caller's RLS: `documents_select` is replaced (deal and listing branches verbatim) so its contact branch also admits a document whose record's ROOT SURVIVOR the caller sees in detail (`app.contact_detail_visible(tenant_id, app.contact_merge_root(tenant_id, contact_id))`); `documents_clean_only`, `documents_write`, `documents_update` and `objects_documents` are unchanged, so an unclean document stays its uploader's and tenant-wide roles', an object is readable only through a clean document the caller can read, and a merged-away record takes no upload or new version (the API answers `CONTACT_MERGED` with `mergedInto` when the caller sees the survivor). The listing (`documents/repos.ts docsOfScope`) reads the tree's ids and RLS decides each row. Proof: `packages/db/test/documents_0069.test.ts` (a two-hop chain: the agent, the Team Lead and the Owner read the document, the record stays hidden, a peer agent, the TC and the ISA gain nothing, another tenant learns nothing, the grants) and `apps/api/src/documents/documents.test.ts` (list, detail and download on the survivor; the peer's 404; the version's `CONTACT_MERGED`).
- **Residual (reported, not changed here).** A merged-away record's CMA reports (0061 `cma_reports`, module 71, `platform:cma` dark) have the same gap (§21.30); the helpers above would serve the same policy change, which needs module 71's own review and tests. A merged-away record's call recordings and transcripts had the same gap (batch 11 review ownership-data/F2, queued for the comms lane): closed by migration `0078_b11_review_recordings` with `app.contact_merge_root`, §21.42.
- **Generic assistant paths (no migration).** A `contract_extraction` (owned by its uploader, no contact) is no longer a card in `GET /assistant/inbox` (`assistant/repos/actions.ts OWN_DECISION_PATH_KINDS`), and `POST /assistant/actions/:id/reject` and `POST /assistant/claims/:id/review` refuse it with 409 `EXTRACTION_USE_DOCUMENT` (`details.documentId`): a generic reject closed the extraction for good (`EXTRACTION_CLOSED`, then `EXTRACTION_EXISTS` on a re-read) and a generic review marked a claim decided with no deal date. The generic approve stays acknowledge-only (status `approved`, nothing written; the document confirm still works). Proof: `apps/api/src/documents/extraction.test.ts`.


### 21.36 Security notes: batch 11 review fixes, backlog gaps (migration `0076_b11_review_backlog`)
Finding ownership-data/F3 and the reconciliation's backlog-6 and backlog-9 (R-DATA-6, E553, V10, V13), plus the 0071 erratum (db-public/F1, §21.33).
- **`app.purge_home_valuations()` is replaced (SECURITY DEFINER; owner `app_retention`, `search_path = pg_catalog, public`, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC and granted to `app_user` only, no argument; re-asserted and self-checked).** Gap closed: every home-value firing copies the estimate (value, range, source name, as-of date) into its run's `trigger_ref.evidence[].snippet` and its drafts' `evidence_refs.snippet`, and 0073 deleted only the row, so the value outlived it and its citation still read as an available source. The refusal (`HOME_VALUE_PURGE_SYSTEM_ONLY`), the database clock, the 13-month cut-off and the "still cited" exemptions are verbatim; for the ids this call deleted, in the caller's tenant only (`tenant_id = app.tenant_id()` on both statements, because the owner bypasses RLS), it sets `evidence_refs.snippet`/`snippet_enc` NULL and `source_available = false` (the recordings pattern of `app.retention_sweep`) and removes the snippet from each trigger run's stored evidence element, adding `sourceAvailable: false` (the rest of `trigger_ref`, the ref and any skip, is kept). It only removes text and sets a flag false; `app_retention` already held UPDATE on both tables (0001); no new grant, table, column, policy, trigger or definer; `app_user` still has no DELETE on `home_valuations`. `GET /contacts/:id/trigger-runs` (`nurture/routes.ts factOf`) words such a run's fact "Source removed under retention policy". No backfill: 0073 lands in the same batch, and erasure deletes a person's runs and drafts with their valuations (FK cascade).
- **Not changed (recorded in R-DATA-6; owner question).** A draft's own text (payload body and evidence line, `assistant_claims.text`) and a message already sent keep the estimate as written: they are the record of what was proposed or sent and follow the message retention rule.
- Proof: `apps/api/src/nurture/homeValueRetention.test.ts` "R-DATA-6 E553 (copies)" (the purged row's evidence rows and stored run evidence, the trigger-runs view, the kept row's copies untouched).

### 21.37 Security notes: AI ISA (D12 PROPOSED; lane ISA-2, migration `0190_ai_isa`, lane number kept until batch 11 lands; docs/AI_ISA.md §8)
- **One changed definer: `app.messages_dispatch_guard()`** (still SECURITY DEFINER, owned by `app_definer`, `search_path = pg_catalog, public`, no PUBLIC EXECUTE; the migration's DO block re-asserts the shape and that every earlier rule is in the body). The newest body (0065, telephony S3) is kept verbatim; the one addition is the `ai_isa_mandate` branch of the autonomy CASE, after D1's do-not-contact, opt-out and suppression checks (unchanged and first), plus a refusal of the AI ISA categories for any non-assistant initiator. Every read is keyed by `NEW.tenant_id` (the definer bypasses RLS).
- **`app.ai_isa_send_check(messages)` is an INVOKER function with EXECUTE granted only to `app_definer`** (revoked from PUBLIC; the DO block refuses the migration if app_user can execute it). It runs only inside the guard, so with the guard's rights; called by anyone else it is refused by the grant. It raises one coded refusal per check of §5.4: binding (action withdrawn, category, turn, assignment, purpose marketing, channel, no media, body hash, bound recipient), liveness (stop signal since start, person block, paused, epoch, pinned inbound seq, or the post-handoff closed set), the chain (platform:ai_isa cleared, tenant switches, the approved or narrowing-superseded version with its latest approval hash, the active opt-in with a recomputed attestation hash, owner/seq/root, the owner's membership), shared address and active client, unread MMS text parts, the seller packs for sold-derived facts, consent re-derived from messages and consent_events (reply tier: the person's own inbound from the bound address within the pack's reply window and after the last AI outbound, an aligned DKIM pass for email; initiated tier: an express-written grant (any level ≥ inquiry for email) from the person's own disclosure, scope stated, the tenant named in the body, not a source fallback, not imported, not agent-entered, after the D12 date in pack rules, not revoked by a later event on any of the person's points; plus a fresh "not listed" `us_national` DNC result for SMS), quiet hours over a DB-derived minimum zone set (explicit zone, else continental set ∪ tenant zone ∪ area-code zones ∪ point zones ∪ address-state zones) that the turn's zones must cover, the window clipped to the pack ceiling and initiated weekdays, and the caps under sorted `ai_isa.send:{tenant}:{hmac}` advisory locks (AI 3/24 h, automated+bulk 3/24 h, initiated 1/24 h and 3/7 d, the 120 s gap), the tenant-day guarded upsert last so a refusal never counts.
- **Invoker triggers, no new definer:** the assignment, opt-in, person-block, handoff, shared-fact and turn guards; the playbook-enrollment `AI_ISA_ACTIVE` refusal; the stop-signal triggers on `consent_events` (revoke, expire, import_revert), `suppressions` (any reason) and `contacts.do_not_contact_at`, which write only inside the writer's own tenant context (a writer without one still meets D1's direct checks in the guard); the appointments booking lock (`calendar.book:{tenant}:{user}`, the same key as calendar/repos/links.ts) and the assistant-overlap refusal (`APPOINTMENT_OVERLAP`). `app.ai_isa_codes_only(jsonb)` is an IMMUTABLE SQL function (CHECKs on turn decisions and denials: codes and handles only).
- **Grants:** `app_definer` gets INSERT on `ai_isa_stop_signals` and `jobs` (the stop-signal triggers also fire inside definer functions, e.g. `app.contacts_dnc_follow_merge`), SELECT on the AI ISA tables and SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE on `ai_isa_daily_counts` (written only by the guard). app_user has no write on `ai_isa_daily_counts`, no UPDATE/DELETE on the controls and stop-signal ledgers, and a column-limited UPDATE (status, revoked_*) on opt-ins.
- **Policies:** every new tenant table has FORCE RLS, the restrictive tenant policy, the three restrictive support policies and the support statement trigger; the global zone tables are readable by staff principals only. The stop-signal INSERT policy allows any tenant actor (a spurious row can only stop care).
- **Trust decisions:** an email reply is reply-tier evidence only with an aligned DKIM pass stored on the message (`messages.email_dkim_aligned`; no verdict ⇒ not aligned, fail closed); the AI ISA never writes a consent grant; `AI_ISA_LIVE_SENDS` defaults to false (R-ISA-25) and refuses AI ISA messages on non-fake adapters in TX1.
- Proof: `packages/db/test/rls_smoke_ai_isa.test.ts` (every guard code, RLS per role, the cap race, the stop-signal writers, the lock in both orders), `apps/api/src/isa/isa.test.ts` and `adversarial.test.ts`, on real Postgres via `createTestDb()`.

### 21.38 Security notes: module 48 `api/voice` (lane api-voice; migration `0150_voice_reconcile_idx`, lane number kept until batch 11 lands)
- **No new SECURITY DEFINER function, policy, grant, table or public route.** The migration adds one partial index (`calls_voice_handback_missing`, plain columns). The routes are staff routes under `/api/voice/**` and `GET /api/calls/:id/voice`; the telephony callbacks stay the nine documented paths (`comms/webhooks.ts` gains branches inside the existing `/voice`, `/voice/screen`, `/voice/accept` and `/voice/dial-result` handlers; `webhooks.contract.test.ts` unchanged). Writes in `voice/**` run as `systemActor(tenant, 'provider')`, except enabling and disabling, which run as the user so `compliance_policy_voice_guard`, `compliance_policy_guard` and the two `tenant_settings` guards decide (proof: `apps/api/src/voice/routes.test.ts`).
- **Core decides, module 48 executes.** The runner feeds core's `step` and carries out its actions; it never re-decides a tool, target, slot, outcome or opt-out and never classifies the model's words. Where it must stop a session (ledger cap, lost transport, an exception) it feeds core a `vendor_error`. The facts are read once in the takeover transaction (E150); slots are read after it commits, outside the contact lock.
- **Trust decisions.** (1) The takeover decision is stored on the call at the ring (`recording_decision.voiceTakeover`) and replayed; the DB guard `calls_voice_guard` decides again at the takeover UPDATE, inside a savepoint, and every refusal is Phase 1's voicemail (E420; `voice/refusals.test.ts`). (2) The provider's `in_progress` status of an AI call is ignored: `answered_at` is set only by the announcement-played update (`calls_assistant_disclosed`). (3) `calls.transfer_outcome` is the single arbiter of a transfer (every write `WHERE transfer_outcome IS NULL`). (4) A signed call ref names a tenant and a call; a ref for another tenant's call resolves nothing in RLS and changes nothing (R-TEN-1, `voice/adversarial.test.ts`). (5) The vendor context carries no contact field (R-VOICE-13, architecture test). (6) No hot rule ships (`hotRuleFor` answers null outside tests, R-VOICE-24), so nobody is transferred live in production.
- **Sends.** The session sends nothing; a booking confirmation is a draft on the handback, sent only at the agent's tap through `drafts.ts` → `queueOutboundInTx` → `dispatchOutbound` (the send-time gate refuses it after a later STOP, `voice/optouts.test.ts`). `voice/**` names no send, dial or telephony call (architecture test).
- **Residual risks.** (1) Core requires the owner's user id in `warm_transfer`'s arguments, but the vendor context has no owner field (R-VOICE-13): how a real model names its transfer target is a vendor question (N12); the fake maps a fixture id. (2) A session lives in the process that took the call; a restart loses it (voicemail on the next fetch, recovery handback by `voice.reconcile` within 15 minutes). (3) A policy version created through `POST /compliance-policies` does not copy the voice columns (`insertVersion`), so changing any policy field turns AI answering off (fails safe; admin/** is outside this lane). (4) `voice_answer_*` widening is enforced by the API only (O4): the DB has no guard on those two columns. (5) The in-call transcript is not stored by module 48; the handback cites call fields, not quotes. (6) An AI call's recording is transcribed like any call, and that transcript also feeds the call summary (`assistant/callPipeline.ts` `callsAwaitingSummary`), which may add its own promise tasks beside the handback's (VERIFY with the orchestrator whether they may duplicate E115's). (7) E437: a version created or activated through module 10 (voice columns not copied) stops answering, and `GET /voice/answering` then shows `VOICE_ANSWERING_OFF` without naming the module-10 change; an Owner re-activating an older voice-on version there turns answering back on under that version's attestation (GET shows the attestation date). No module-48 test covers E437 yet. (8) A spoken opt-out over the disclosure is not processed (R-VOICE-2): core ignores caller turns before both announcements; the needs-review note E438 asks for is not built yet.
- **Enablement and the Admin-disable limit (R-VOICE-20, O14).** On is the Owner, re-authenticated, with the exact attestation, an approved version, a cleared pack, a configured agent and recording on; the new policy version copies every column of the active one (drift-tested against the catalog). Off is the Owner, or an Admin unless the active version is attested: an Admin cannot copy an Owner-attested version (0001 `compliance_policy_guard`), so the API answers `OWNER_REQUIRED` with a plain sentence and points to the team AI switch (`ai_enabled`, Admin-settable). Widening `voice_answer_*` is the Owner's (API-enforced, O4).
- **Flagged edits outside `voice/**` (O10).** `calendar/service.ts` `busyForBooking` (one additive export), `comms/recordings.ts` `mayKeepVoicemail` (keeps the voicemail of an AI call that fell back before it was answered) and `callByRef` (selects `answered_at`), `nurture/routes.ts` (trigger templates only), `assistant/templates.ts` (the `voice_answering` template), the voice branches in `comms/webhooks.ts`, and type-only narrowing in two nurture tests. `core/voice/lines.ts` is unchanged (O1's line edit is not made: see PHASE2_3_PLAN row 48).
- **VERIFY.** Vendor (N12): every transport capability in `voice/provider.ts` (played events, hand-off and reconnect of a live call, latency, language). WITH COUNSEL (C2): the template's disclosure and announcement, the transfer whisper, the confirmation wording, the attestation sentence.

### 21.39 Security notes: module 53 `api/gbp` (migration `0170_gbp_coaching_trends`, lane number kept until batch 11 lands)
- **`app.gbp_reviews_guard()`** (invoker trigger, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC): only refuses (and turns a drafted review whose action the FK set to NULL back into "needs a reply"). A review row exists only for a connected/degraded `gbp_location` of the same tenant while `platform:gbp` is cleared; its identity never changes; a removed review keeps no text; reply states move only along the allowed edges and a drafted review names a waiting or approved `review_reply_draft` of that very review. RLS still decides who writes (0018 `gbp_reviews_write`: the system only). It reads `assistant_actions` and `integration_connections` as the writer (the system).
- **`app.marketing_publications_gbp_gate()`** (invoker trigger, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC, BEFORE INSERT, trigger `marketing_publications_guard_gbp`): refuses a publication to a `gbp_location` while `platform:gbp` is uncleared. It sits next to the 0054 guard (not a replacement); its name sorts after the guard's, so the guard's refusals come first and both refuse independently.
- **`platform:gbp`** ships unreviewed and counsel-only (not in `VENDOR_CHECKED_PACKS`, not in D11); only the counsel-review command records its review on a deployment. No code, seed or test default marks it reviewed (tests mark it through the superuser and reset it).
- **Trust decisions.** Reply drafts are owner-less `review_reply_draft` actions (contact_id and owner_user_id NULL), so only tenant-wide actors read them, the inbox and feed never list them, and the generic action routes refuse them (ownActionFacts, E471). The GBP routes check the action's kind, its `payload.review_id` and that `gbp_reviews.reply_action_id` names it. `gbp.reply` is Owner/Admin only (owner question 3's default). The person's TX1 claims the approval (proposed → approved, as themselves, `assistant_actions_decision_guard`), and only the claim holder calls `sendReviewReply`, the one `postReviewReply` call site (`marketing/publish.ts`), outside any transaction. A public reply is publishing (R-GATE-15): no messages row, contact point, touch or consent event, and reviewers are never matched to contacts (architecture test). The model sees only the star rating, the delimited review text and the team's name; its draft passes the privacy guard (`gbp/guard.ts`) or nothing is created; the run is recorded in `ai_runs` with review ids only. Tokens are decrypted as the system in memory only. Proof: `apps/api/src/gbp/{sync,replies,adversarial,architecture,guard.property}.test.ts`, `apps/api/src/marketing/publish.test.ts` (module 53 block).
- **VERIFY / counsel.** Every GBP vendor shape (`gbp/provider.ts`), the 1,500-character post and reply limits, whitespace normalisation of owner replies, and a removal signal are VERIFY; the reply privacy list and whether a public reply needs anything more are VERIFY WITH COUNSEL (C15).
- **FK path.** `gbp_reviews.reply_action_id` is `ON DELETE SET NULL`: the referential update runs the BEFORE UPDATE guard, which turns a drafted review whose action is gone into 'none' instead of blocking the delete (DB test). Nothing deletes assistant actions today.

### 21.40 Security notes: module 54 `api/coaching-trends` (migration `0170_gbp_coaching_trends`, lane number kept until batch 11 lands)
- **`app.coaching_trends(uuid[], integer, text)`** (SECURITY DEFINER, STABLE, owner `app_definer`, `search_path = pg_catalog, public`, every table schema-qualified and filtered by `app.tenant_id()`; EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC, granted to `app_user` only). It reads `call_scores`, `calls` and `recordings` past RLS: the aggregate exception R-COACH-7 grants ("a Team Lead sees aggregates only"). **Trust:** it refuses the whole call (`COACHING_TRENDS_SCOPE`, insufficient_privilege) unless the caller is a staff user actor with a role that takes call reviews (never support, the system, a client or a TC); 1-200 distinct members of the tenant; 1-4 weeks; one rubric version. Scope: the caller's own id always; Owner/Admin (by `app.my_role()`, never `app.is_tenant_wide()`, which is also true for support) any member; a Team Lead only ids in `app.my_team_member_ids()` and only while `tenant_settings.tl_sees_call_scores`. Zero rows unless `platform:coaching` is cleared and call scoring is on. It counts only calls of the version with a recording that is not shredded (the 0052 RESTRICTIVE policy's condition), and returns counts only. **Small numbers:** met is returned only when scored ≥ 5 and every (member, ISO week, item) cell inside has 0 or ≥ 5 scored, except when the caller asks for exactly themselves (who may read those reviews anyway, R-COACH-3); two overlapping answers therefore differ only by whole cells of ≥ 5. **What it does not hide:** call counts per member and week (`calls`, `scored`) are shown, as the n every number needs; a Team Lead with the switch on learns their team's review volume. Proof: `packages/db/test/gbp_coaching_trends_0170.test.ts` (scope matrix, floor and differencing, other versions, shredded recordings, other tenants, DST and year windows), `apps/api/src/coaching/trends/trends.test.ts` (the definer equals the pure mirror), `aggregate.property.test.ts`, `adversarial.test.ts`.
- **`app.coaching_trend_bounds(timestamptz, text, integer)`** (invoker, STABLE, pinned `search_path = pg_catalog`, reads nothing; EXECUTE to `app_user` and `app_definer`): the week windows, computed on local timestamps and converted once.
- **Residual risk.** Two reads at different moments can differ by more than whole cells: a call scored late, or a recording shredded, between them changes one (member, week, item) cell, so the difference of the two answers may describe fewer than 5 calls. Accepted: the change is one call's appearance or disappearance, never its content, and the API reads once per page. **VERIFY:** the cell-floor query's plan at 250k contacts (it should use `calls_agent_idx` or `call_scores_agent_idx`; no new index is added; EXPLAIN not yet run). **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL (C11 extended):** a Team Lead seeing aggregate trends that include calls whose recordings they cannot play (employee monitoring); only with the Owner's `tl_sees_call_scores`.

### 21.41 Security notes: pilot deploy readiness (deploy track; no migration)
- **No new SECURITY DEFINER function, table, policy or grant in the schema.** The track changes how roles are provisioned and how clients connect, not the schema: `packages/db/src/rolePreflight.ts` (owner inspection, the FORCE-RLS probe, the schema CREATE window, post-checks, the app-role checks, the rolled-back trial probe), `release.ts`, `roleBlock.ts`, `tls.ts`, `scripts/preflight.ts`, and the start-up checks in `apps/api/src/dbRole.ts` (DEPLOY §3, §12; DATA_MODEL R-DEPLOY-1..7).
- **Trust decisions.** (1) A non-superuser owner is granted the definer roles `WITH INHERIT TRUE, SET TRUE` by the release step: it can then act as them, which adds nothing to an owner that already owns every object and bypasses RLS. (2) The definer roles hold CREATE on the owner's schemas only between "window opened" and "window closed", around `migrate()` alone; the window closes in `finally`, a leftover one is closed by the next run first, and the post-checks refuse a release that leaves a grant or default privilege (E479). (3) The definer-ownership post-check skips extension members (`pg_depend` deptype `e`), so a provider's extension functions are not refused; a SECURITY DEFINER function of ours that is not owned by a definer role still is. (4) The rolled-back trial probe (`preflight --trial`, G1) creates a BYPASSRLS role, a schema and a SECURITY DEFINER function inside one transaction that always rolls back; its test compares pg_roles, pg_namespace and pg_default_acl before and after. (5) Roles are cluster-wide: the owner's definer membership persists across releases and databases in that cluster, and the per-release definer-attribute check (NOLOGIN NOSUPERUSER BYPASSRLS) repeats 0001's one-time check. (6) The app checks read only catalogs `app_user` can read (pg_roles, pg_auth_members, pg_class, pg_proc, pg_namespace, its own pg_stat_ssl row) plus `tenants` through RLS, and ignore other sessions' `pg_temp_*` objects.
- **TLS.** Every production client passes `pgSsl(databaseTls)` (`{ rejectUnauthorized: true, ca? }` or false; never `servername`, which pg sets from the host); production refuses URL query strings and `PG*` variables because either would change the login, host or TLS mode behind the checked URL. ARCHITECTURE §8's `sslmode=verify-full` baseline is now enforced (`DATABASE_SSL_MODE=verify-full`, the production default).
- Proof: `packages/db/test/role_preflight.test.ts`, `release.test.ts` (the non-superuser release end to end on fresh databases of the local test cluster, the failure and crash paths, E478), `restore_roles.test.ts`, `db_tls.test.ts`; `apps/api/src/dbRole.test.ts`, `config.test.ts`, `deploy.env.test.ts`, `deploy.appvm.test.ts`.

### 21.42 Security notes: batch 11 review fixes, call recordings (migration `0078_b11_review_recordings`)
Finding ownership-data/F2 of the batch 11 interaction review, the comms twin of ownership-data/F1 (§21.35) (R-OWN-24, R-PERM-8, R-PERM-9). 0077 is the CMA reports fix of the same class.
- **Gap closed.** A merge re-points the merged-away record's messages and calls to the survivor (`db/ownership/merge.ts mergeCommunication`), but nothing moves `recordings.contact_id` or `transcripts.contact_id`, so a moved call's recording and transcript keep the merged-away record's id. Contacts RLS hides that record (closed, no owner) from everyone who is not tenant-wide, so `recordings_select` / `transcripts_select` (`app.contact_detail_visible` on that record), the RESTRICTIVE `recording_scope` (its 'own' and 'team' branches look that record's owner up; 'deal' compares it with the caller's deal contacts, which the merge moved to the survivor, 0053/0059) and the API's `recordingFacts` (inner join to that record) all stopped at it: the survivor's agent, Team Lead and the TC on the person's deal got 404 on `POST /recordings/:id/stream-token`, `GET /recordings/:id/stream` and `GET /recordings/:id/transcript` (and, through the same policies, a voicemail left on the merged-away record is missing from the survivor's `GET /contacts/:id/attachments`; not separately tested); only the Owner and Admins kept them.
- **Policies replaced (ALTER POLICY; no new function, table, column, trigger or grant).** Each one is its 0001 condition OR the same condition on the record's ROOT SURVIVOR (`app.contact_merge_root(tenant_id, contact_id)`, the 0075 SECURITY DEFINER helper, re-asserted by the migration's self-check), so every row admitted before is admitted now and a never-merged record is decided exactly as before (its root is itself). `recordings_select` and `transcripts_select`: + `app.contact_detail_visible(tenant_id, root)`. `recording_scope` stays RESTRICTIVE with the same scopes from `app.recording_scope()` and the same tenant settings: 'all' verbatim; 'own' and 'team' look up `c.id IN (contact_id, root)` with the 0001 owner conditions verbatim; 'deal' adds `root = ANY (app.my_deal_contact_ids())`; the `agent_user_id` alternatives are verbatim. Unchanged: `transcript_scope` (a transcript needs its recording visible, so it inherits all of this), `recordings_write` / `transcripts_write` (system only), `tenant_isolation` and `support_read_only_*` on both tables, `objects_select` (a recording's object through a visible recording) and `recording_stream_tokens` (own tokens; INSERT needs the recording visible). The self-check refuses the migration if either table's policy set changed, a replaced policy does not follow the root, a scope gate is no longer RESTRICTIVE SELECT, or the helper is no longer an `app_definer` definer that only `app_user` executes.
- **Who reads more.** Only readers who already see the person (the root survivor) in detail and whose recording scope covers that person: the survivor's agent (as owner, even when someone else made the call), the Team Lead of the survivor's agent while `team_lead_recording_access` is on, the TC on the survivor's deal while `tc_recording_access` is on. **Not widened:** a peer agent; the agent who made the call but does not see the survivor (a merge into another agent's record), as with the call itself; another tenant (the helper resolves only the caller's tenant; `tenant_isolation` applies). Every play and transcript view is still audited (R-PERM-9).
- **API (`comms/recordings.ts recordingFacts`).** The `core.can` facts for `recording.play` / `transcript.view` are read about the root survivor (LEFT JOIN, so a survivor the caller cannot see only makes own/team false; RLS on `recordings` decides whether the row exists), and `deal` is true for the record or its root. Without this the policies admit the row and the authorize step still answered 404. The coaching quotes' transcript check (`coaching/views.ts`) uses the same facts.
- **Cost.** Each evaluated recording or transcript row may add one bounded recursive walk (at most 64 hops, primary-key lookups on `contacts`), as `documents_select` does since 0075; recordings are read by id or per call.
- **Not changed (reported).** Recordings and transcripts still carry the merged-away record's id (the denormalized `contact_id` exists for these policies); moving them in the merge instead would need a system write path and a backfill. `app.purge_contact_internal` and retention already take the whole merge tree (0037).
- Proof: `apps/api/src/comms/b11review.recordings.test.ts` (fails without the migration, and without the facts change: the agent on the call and owner of the survivor, the Team Lead with the setting on and off, the Owner, a peer agent, another tenant's Owner; the survivor's owner who was not on the call after the Owner merged another agent's record into theirs; the TC on the person's deal with the setting on and off; the data stays on the merged-away record; the plays are audited).
